===Original===
To 

 all good Irishmen 

 in general 

 and one 

 Charles Darwin Porter 

 in particular

===Chinese===
献给

天下善良的爱尔兰人

一体同敬

而其中一位

查尔斯·达尔文·波特（Charles Darwin Porter）

敬爱独深

## I Wherein great risks are taken and the Limberlost guard is hired. / ## I 本章大险当前，林伯洛斯特守林人受雇。

===Original===
Freckles came down the corduroy that crosses the lower end of the Limberlost. At a glance he might have been mistaken for a tramp, but he was truly seeking work. He was intensely eager to belong somewhere and to be attached to almost any enterprise that would furnish him food and clothing.

Long before he came in sight of the camp of the Grand Rapids Lumber Company, he could hear the cheery voices of the men, the neighing of the horses, and could scent the tempting odors of cooking food. A feeling of homeless friendlessness swept over him in a sickening wave. Without stopping to think, he turned into the newly made road and followed it to the camp, where the gang was making ready for supper and bed.

The scene was intensely attractive. The thickness of the swamp made a dark, massive background below, while above towered gigantic trees. The men were calling jovially back and forth as they unharnessed tired horses that fell into attitudes of rest and crunched, in deep content, the grain given them. Duncan, the brawny Scotch head-teamster, lovingly wiped the flanks of his big bays with handfuls of pawpaw leaves, as he softly whistled, “O wha will be my dearie, O!” and a cricket beneath the leaves at his feet accompanied him. The green wood fire hissed and crackled merrily. Wreathing tongues of flame wrapped around the big black kettles, and when the cook lifted the lids to plunge in his testing-fork, gusts of savory odors escaped.

===Chinese===
弗雷克尔斯（Freckles，意即『雀斑』，因满脸雀斑得名）顺着横穿林伯洛斯特（the Limberlost，印第安纳州的大沼泽）低处的那条圆木路走了下来。乍一看，他说不定会被当成个流浪汉，可他实实在在是在找活儿干。他一心一意盼着能有个归属，恨不得随便什么营生，只要管他饭吃、给他衣穿，他都愿意投靠。

还没望见大急流城伐木公司（Grand Rapids Lumber Company）的营地，他就先听见了工人们欢快的说笑声、马儿的嘶鸣，还闻到了饭菜喷香的诱惑。一股无家可依、无亲无故的感觉像浪头一样涌上来，压得他发慌。他不由分说拐上了那条新修的路，一路走到营地——工队正在张罗晚饭，准备歇息。

眼前的景象热闹迷人。低处，茂密的沼泽化作一团深色的厚重背景；高处，参天巨木拔地而起。工人们一边给疲惫的马卸套，一边你一言我一语地打趣；马儿摆出歇息的架势，心满意足地咀嚼着喂到嘴边的谷物，咯吱作响。壮实的苏格兰籍车队领班邓肯（Duncan）抓起一把把巴婆树（pawpaw）的叶子，疼爱地擦拭他那几匹高大的栗色马的身侧，嘴里轻轻吹着「啊，谁做我亲爱的人，啊！」，脚边草叶下一只蟋蟀给他伴奏。青柴烧起的火欢快地嘶嘶啪啪作响。舌状的火苗盘旋缠绕着几口黑色大锅，厨师揭开锅盖把试肉叉插进去时，一阵阵浓香便窜了出来。

===Original===
Freckles approached him.

“I want to speak with the Boss,” he said.

The cook glanced at him and answered carelessly: “He can’t use you.”

The color flooded Freckles’ face, but he said simply: “If you will be having the goodness to point him out, we will give him a chance to do his own talking.”

With a shrug of astonishment, the cook led the way to a rough board table where a broad, square-shouldered man was bending over some account-books.

“Mr. McLean, here’s another man wanting to be taken on the gang, I suppose,” he said.

===Chinese===
弗雷克尔斯朝他走了过去。

「俺想跟老板（the Boss）说话。」他说。

厨师瞥了他一眼，漫不经心地答道：「他用不上你。」

一股血色涌上弗雷克尔斯的脸，可他只朴朴实实地说：「要是您行行好，给俺指一指他在哪儿，俺们也好让他亲口回话、自己拿主意。」

厨师惊讶地耸了耸肩，领着他来到一张粗木板桌前；一个肩宽背阔的汉子正伏在几本账簿上。

「麦克莱恩（McLean）先生，又来了一个想上工的。」他说。

===Original===
“All right,” came the cheery answer. “I never needed a good man more than I do just now.”

The manager turned a page and carefully began a new line.

“No use of your bothering with this fellow,” volunteered the cook. “He hasn’t but one hand.”

The flush on Freckles’ face burned deeper. His lips thinned to a mere line. He lifted his shoulders, took a step forward, and thrust out his right arm, from which the sleeve dangled empty at the wrist.

“That will do, Sears,” came the voice of the Boss sharply. “I will interview my man when I finish this report.”

He turned to his work, while the cook hurried to the fires. Freckles stood one instant as he had braced himself to meet the eyes of the manager; then his arm dropped and a wave of whiteness swept him. The Boss had not even turned his head. He had used the possessive. When he said “my man,” the hungry heart of Freckles went reaching toward him.

===Chinese===
「好啊，」传来一声爽快的回答，「我从来没有像眼下这样缺一个好手。」

经理翻过一页，仔仔细细起了新的一行。

「您何必在这个小子身上费工夫，」厨师自作主张地搭了一句，「他只有一只手。」

弗雷克尔斯脸上的红晕烧得更深了。他的嘴唇抿成一条细线。他耸起肩膀，上前一步，猛地伸出右臂——袖管空荡荡的，从腕口直晃荡。

「行了，西尔斯（Sears），」老板的声音严厉地响起，「等我写完这份报告，就见我的人。」

他回头继续做事，厨师赶忙回灶火那边去了。弗雷克尔斯本来挺直了身子，等着迎接经理的目光；可才站了一瞬，胳膊便垂了下来，一阵惨白掠过他的脸。老板连头都没有回。他用的是所有格的说法。当他说出「我的人」时，弗雷克尔斯那颗饥渴的心，一下子朝他伸了过去。

===Original===
The boy drew a quivering breath. Then he whipped off his old hat and beat the dust from it carefully. With his left hand he caught the right sleeve, wiped his sweaty face, and tried to straighten his hair with his fingers. He broke a spray of ironwort beside him and used the purple bloom to beat the dust from his shoulders and limbs. The Boss, busy over his report, was, nevertheless, vaguely alive to the toilet being made behind him, and scored one for the man.

McLean was a Scotchman. It was his habit to work slowly and methodically. The men of his camps never had known him to be in a hurry or to lose his temper. Discipline was inflexible, but the Boss was always kind. His habits were simple. He shared camp life with his gangs. The only visible signs of wealth consisted of a big, shimmering diamond stone of ice and fire that glittered and burned on one of his fingers, and the dainty, beautiful thoroughbred mare he rode between camps and across the country on business.

No man of McLean’s gangs could honestly say that he ever had been overdriven or underpaid. The Boss never had exacted any deference from his men, yet so intense was his personality that no man of them ever had attempted a familiarity. They all knew him to be a thorough gentleman, and that in the great timber city several millions stood to his credit.

He was the only son of that McLean who had sent out the finest ships ever built in Scotland. That his son should carry on this business after the father’s death had been his ambition. He had sent the boy through the universities of Oxford and Edinburgh, and allowed him several years’ travel before he should attempt his first commission for the firm.

===Chinese===
少年颤抖着吸了一口气。接着他一把摘下旧帽子，细细掸去上面的尘土。他用左手攥住右边的空袖管，抹了抹脸上的汗，又用手指抿了抿头发。他折下身边一枝开花的铁草（ironwort），拿那紫色花穗掸去肩头和四肢的尘土。老板虽然埋头写报告，背后这场梳洗他却隐隐觉着，心里暗暗替这条汉子记了一分。

麦克莱恩是个苏格兰人。他做事一向从容不迫、有条不紊。营地里的人从没见他着过急、发过脾气。纪律铁面无私，可老板待人总是和气。他生活简朴，跟自己的工队同吃同住。能看出他富来的，只有两样：一样是他一根手指上那颗大钻石，晶莹如冰、灼灼如火，闪着烧着；另一样是他往返各营地、外出办事时骑的那匹娇好俊美的良种母马。

麦克莱恩工队里的人，谁也没法凭良心说自己被往死里使唤过、被克扣过工钱。老板从不要求手下对他毕恭毕敬，可他气度慑人，没有一个人敢跟他随随便便。他们都知道他是位地道的绅士，还知道在那座木材大城里，他名下存着好几百万。

他就是那位麦克莱恩的独子——老麦克莱恩放船下水，造出了苏格兰（Scotland）有史以来最出色的船。让儿子在自己身后接掌这份产业，是老人的志向。他把儿子送进牛津（Oxford）和爱丁堡（Edinburgh）两所大学，又让他游历了几年，才让他头一回替商行出面办事。

===Original===
Then he was ordered to southern Canada and Michigan to purchase a consignment of tall, straight timber for masts, and south to Indiana for oak beams. The young man entered these mighty forests, parts of which lay untouched since the dawn of the morning of time. The clear, cool, pungent atmosphere was intoxicating. The intense silence, like that of a great empty cathedral, fascinated him. He gradually learned that, to the shy wood creatures that darted across his path or peeped inquiringly from leafy ambush, he was brother. He found himself approaching, with a feeling of reverence, those majestic trees that had stood through ages of sun, wind, and snow. Soon it became difficult to fell them. When he had filled his order and returned home, he was amazed to learn that in the swamps and forests he had lost his heart and it was calling﻿—forever calling him.

When he inherited his father’s property, he promptly disposed of it, and, with his mother, founded a home in a splendid residence in the outskirts of Grand Rapids. With three partners, he organized a lumber company. His work was to purchase, fell, and ship the timber to the mills. Marshall managed the milling process and passed the lumber to the factory. From the lumber, Barthol made beautiful and useful furniture, which Uptegrove scattered all over the world from a big wholesale house. Of the thousands who saw their faces reflected on the polished surfaces of that furniture and found comfort in its use, few there were to whom it suggested mighty forests and trackless swamps, and the man, big of soul and body, who cut his way through them, and with the eye of experience doomed the proud trees that were now entering the homes of civilization for service.

When McLean turned from his finished report, he faced a young man, yet under twenty, tall, spare, heavily framed, closely freckled, and red-haired, with a homely Irish face, but in the steady gray eyes, straightly meeting his searching ones of blue, there was unswerving candor and the appearance of longing not to be ignored. He was dressed in the roughest of farm clothing, and seemed tired to the point of falling.

===Chinese===
后来他奉命去加拿大南部和密歇根（Michigan），采购一批高大笔直、可作桅杆材的木料，又南下印第安纳州（Indiana）采购橡木梁。年轻人走进这些浩瀚的森林——有些地方自开天辟地以来就不曾有人染指。清冽、沁凉、微辛的空气令人陶醉。那种深沉的寂静，像一座空旷的大教堂，把他迷住了。他渐渐明白：对那些羞怯的林间生灵——从他路上窜过的，或从枝叶掩映处探出头张望的——来说，他是兄弟。他不自知地怀着敬畏，走近那一棵棵历经无数寒暑风霜、巍然屹立的大树。不久，砍倒它们竟变得难以落斧。等他办完采购回到家，他才吃惊地发现：自己的心已经丢在那些沼泽和森林里了，而且它在呼唤——永远地呼唤着他。

他继承了父亲的产业，随即把它变卖干净，带着母亲在大急流城（Grand Rapids）郊外一处华美的宅邸安了家。他和三个合伙人办起一家伐木公司。他负责采购、砍伐，把木材发运去锯木厂。马歇尔（Marshall）掌管锯木加工，再把木材送进家具厂。巴塞洛（Barthol）用这些木料做出又美观又实用的家具，厄普特格罗夫（Uptegrove）再从一家大批发行把它们销往世界各地。成千上万的人在那家具光洁的表面上照见自己的脸，从使用中得到慰藉；可他们当中极少有人会由此想起那些浩瀚的森林、无径的沼泽，想起那个身心都伟岸的汉子——他在林莽中辟路而行，凭老练的眼光，宣判了这些骄傲的大树的命运，如今它们正走进文明人家的宅子里去服役。

麦克莱恩写完报告转过身来，面对的是个年轻人：还不到二十岁，瘦高个子，骨架却宽大，满脸密密的雀斑，一头红发，一张不起眼的爱尔兰人的脸；可那双沉稳的灰眼睛直直迎上他那双探究的蓝眼睛，里头是毫不动摇的坦率，和一股不容忽视的渴望。他穿着一身最粗陋的农家衣裳，看样子已经累得快站不住了。

===Original===
“You are looking for work?” questioned McLean.

“Yis,” answered Freckles.

“I am very sorry,” said the Boss with genuine sympathy in his every tone, “but there is only one man I want at present﻿—a hardy, big fellow with a stout heart and a strong body. I hoped that you would do, but I am afraid you are too young and scarcely strong enough.”

Freckles stood, hat in hand, watching McLean.

“And what was it you thought I might be doing?” he asked.

===Chinese===
「你是来找活儿干的？」麦克莱恩问。

「是啦。」弗雷克尔斯答道。

「真不巧，」老板的语气字字恳切，满是同情，「眼下我只要一个人——要个吃得了苦的壮实大汉，心要刚健，身要强壮。我原指望你能顶事，只怕你太年轻，身子骨也还不够硬朗。」

弗雷克尔斯手里拿着帽子站在那儿，望着麦克莱恩。

「那您方才是想着，俺能干个什么活儿呢？」他问。

===Original===
The Boss could scarcely repress a start. Somewhere before accident and poverty there had been an ancestor who used cultivated English, even with an accent. The boy spoke in a mellow Irish voice, sweet and pure. It was scarcely definite enough to be called brogue, yet there was a trick in the turning of the sentence, the wrong sound of a letter here and there, that was almost irresistible to McLean, and presaged a misuse of infinitives and possessives with which he was very familiar and which touched him nearly. He was of foreign birth, and despite years of alienation, in times of strong feeling he committed inherited sins of accent and construction.

“It’s no child’s job,” answered McLean. “I am the field manager of a big lumber company. We have just leased two thousand acres of the Limberlost. Many of these trees are of great value. We can’t leave our camp, six miles south, for almost a year yet; so we have blazed a trail and strung barbed wires securely around this lease. Before we return to our work, I must put this property in the hands of a reliable, brave, strong man who will guard it every hour of the day, and sleep with one eye open at night. I shall require the entire length of the trail to be walked at least twice each day, to make sure that our lines are up and that no one has been trespassing.”

Freckles was leaning forward, absorbing every word with such intense eagerness that he was beguiling the Boss into explanations he had never intended making.

“But why wouldn’t that be the finest job in the world for me?” he pleaded. “I am never sick. I could walk the trail twice, three times every day, and I’d be watching sharp all the while.”

===Chinese===
老板几乎抑制不住心里的一惊。在时运不济、家道中落之前的某一代，这孩子的祖上必是说过一口文雅英语的人，甚至还带着口音。这少年说话嗓音圆润，是爱尔兰腔，甜美纯净。那腔调还够不上分明到称作土音的地步，可句子的转折里、个别字母走了样的读音里，自有一种说不出的味道，让麦克莱恩几乎招架不住；这也预示着往后他会把不定式和所有格用错——那种错处麦克莱恩再熟悉不过，几乎戳中他自己。他也是外邦出生，尽管背井离乡多年，一到情绪激动的时候，仍会犯下从祖上继承来的读音和句法上的老毛病。

「这可不是小孩子干的活儿。」麦克莱恩答道，「我是一家大伐木公司的驻场经理。我们刚租下林伯洛斯特的两千英亩林地，那里头许多树木都价值不菲。我们在南边六英里的那处营地，差不多一年内还撤不走；所以我们沿租林地开出一条巡林小道，四周牢牢拉上了带刺铁丝网。在回来开工之前，我必须把这份产业交到一个可靠、勇敢、强壮的人手里，叫他白天时时刻刻看守，夜里睁着一只眼睡觉。我要求整条巡林小道每天至少走上两遍，好确信我们的铁丝网线都完好立着，没有外人擅闯进来。」

弗雷克尔斯身子前倾，一个字一个字地往心里吸，那份急切竟哄得老板滔滔不绝，把原本不打算细讲的种种都讲了出来。

「可这活儿对俺来说，咋就不是天底下顶好的呢？」他恳求道，「俺从来不生病。俺能一天把巡林小道走两遍、三遍，还时时刻刻都替您瞅得紧紧的。」

===Original===
“It’s because you are scarcely more than a boy, and this will be a trying job for a work-hardened man,” answered McLean. “You see, in the first place, you would be afraid. In stretching our lines, we killed six rattlesnakes almost as long as your body and as thick as your arm. It’s the price of your life to start through the marsh-grass surrounding the swamp unless you are covered with heavy leather above your knees.

“You should be able to swim in case high water undermines the temporary bridge we have built where Sleepy Snake Creek enters the swamp. The fall and winter changes of weather are abrupt and severe, while I would want strict watch kept every day. You would always be alone, and I don’t guarantee what is in the Limberlost. It is lying here as it has lain since the beginning of time, and it is alive with forms and voices. I don’t pretend to say what all of them come from; but from a few slinking shapes I’ve seen, and hair-raising yells I’ve heard, I’d rather not confront their owners myself; and I am neither weak nor fearful.

“Worst of all, any man who will enter the swamp to mark and steal timber is desperate. One of my employees at the south camp, John Carter, compelled me to discharge him for a number of serious reasons. He came here, entered the swamp alone, and succeeded in locating and marking a number of valuable trees that he was endeavoring to sell to a rival company when we secured the lease. He has sworn to have these trees if he has to die or to kill others to get them; and he is a man that the strongest would not care to meet.”

===Chinese===
「就因为你几乎还是个孩子，而这活儿连干惯了重活的汉子都要吃苦头。」麦克莱恩答道，「你想想，头一条，你会害怕。当初拉铁丝网的时候，我们打死过六条响尾蛇，差不多有你身子那么长、胳膊那么粗。想踏进环绕沼泽四周的沼草，那是要拿命来抵的——除非你膝盖以上都裹着厚实的皮绑腿。

「你还得会游泳，以防涨水掏空我们在睡蛇溪（Sleepy Snake Creek）注入沼泽处搭的那座便桥。秋冬两季天气说变就变，又冷得厉害，而我要的是天天严加看管。你将永远孤身一人，况且林伯洛斯特里头有什么，我可不敢打包票。它从太初以来就躺在这儿，至今原样，里头满是形形色色的身影和声音。我不敢妄称它们都打哪儿来；可就凭我见过的那几个悄悄溜过的影子、听过的那几声叫人毛发倒竖的嚎叫，换成我也宁愿不去会一会它们的主人——而我这人，既不算孱弱，也不算胆小。

「顶顶要紧的是，凡是敢闯进这片沼泽来标记、偷盗木料的，都是亡命之徒。我南边营地有个雇工，约翰·卡特（John Carter），因为好几桩严重的事由，我不得不把他辞了。他跑到这儿来，独自进了沼泽，居然寻到并标记下了不少贵重的树木，正设法卖给一家跟我们竞争的公司，恰好这时我们租下了这片林子。他发过毒誓，这些树他是要定了——哪怕自己搭上性命，或者得害死别人；何况他还是个连最强壮的人也不愿招惹的角色。」


===Original===
“But if he came to steal trees, wouldn’t he bring teams and men enough: that all anyone could do would be to watch and be after you?” queried the boy.

“Yes,” replied McLean.

“Then why couldn’t I be watching just as closely, and coming as fast, as an older, stronger man?” asked Freckles.

“Why, by George, you could!” exclaimed McLean. “I don’t know as the size of a man would be half so important as his grit and faithfulness, come to think of it. Sit on that log there and we will talk it over. What is your name?”

===Chinese===
「可他要是来偷树，还能不带上够多的车马人手？到那时候，任谁也做不了别的，只能盯住他们、赶紧去追告您呀，不是吗？」少年问道。

「正是。」麦克莱恩答道。

「那俺为啥就不能跟岁数大、力气壮的人一样，盯得一样紧、报信来得一样快呢？」弗雷克尔斯问。

「哎呀，老天爷，你当然能！」麦克莱恩叫了起来，「细想起来，一个人的块头，怕还没有他的胆气和忠心一半要紧呢。坐到那根木头上，咱们好好谈谈。你叫什么名字？」

===Original===
Freckles shook his head at the proffer of a seat, and folding his arms, stood straight as the trees around him. He grew a shade whiter, but his eyes never faltered.

“Freckles!” he said.

“Good enough for everyday,” laughed McLean, “but I scarcely can put ‘Freckles’ on the company’s books. Tell me your name.”

“I haven’t any name,” replied the boy.

“I don’t understand,” said McLean.

===Chinese===
人家让座，弗雷克尔斯摇了摇头，抱着双臂，站得跟身边的大树一样直。他的脸色又白了一层，可眼睛一瞬也不曾躲闪。

「弗雷克尔斯！」他说。

「平常叫叫倒挺好，」麦克莱恩笑道，「可我总不能把『弗雷克尔斯』写进公司的账册呀。告诉我你的名字。」

「俺没有名字。」少年答道。

「我不明白。」麦克莱恩说。

===Original===
“I was thinking from the voice and the face of you that you wouldn’t,” said Freckles slowly. “I’ve spent more time on it than I ever did on anything else in all me life, and I don’t understand. Does it seem to you that anyone would take a newborn baby and row over it, until it was bruised black, cut off its hand, and leave it out in a bitter night on the steps of a charity home, to the care of strangers? That’s what somebody did to me.”

McLean stared aghast. He had no reply ready, and presently in a low voice he suggested: “And after?”

“The Home people took me in, and I was there the full legal age and several years over. For the most part we were a lot of little Irishmen together. They could always find homes for the other children, but nobody would ever be wanting me on account of me arm.”

“Were they kind to you?” McLean regretted the question the minute it was asked.

===Chinese===
「听您这声气，瞧您这张脸，俺就寻思您准得不明白。」弗雷克尔斯慢慢地说，「这事俺这辈子费的工夫，比费在什么都多，俺自个儿也不明白。您倒想想，世上会有人把个刚落地的娃娃抱去，一顿好打，打得浑身乌青，剁掉一只手，再在一个砭骨的寒夜把他撂在一家慈善孤儿院的台阶上，丢给素不相识的人照管吗？这么待俺的，就有那么一个人。」

麦克莱恩骇然瞪着他，一时无言以对，半晌才低声问了一句：「后来呢？」

「孤儿院（the Home）的人收留了俺，俺在那儿待满了法定年限，又多待了好几年。里头的娃娃大半是一群小小的爱尔兰人。别的孩子总有人肯领养，可谁也不肯要俺，就因为俺这只胳膊。」

「他们待你好吗？」话一出口，麦克莱恩就后悔了。

===Original===
“I don’t know,” answered Freckles. The reply sounded so hopeless, even to his own ears, that he hastened to qualify it by adding: “You see, it’s like this, sir. Kindnesses that people are paid to lay off in job lots and that belong equally to several hundred others, ain’t going to be soaking into any one fellow so much.”

“Go on,” said McLean, nodding comprehendingly.

“There’s nothing worth the taking of your time to tell,” replied Freckles. “The Home was in Chicago, and I was there all me life until three months ago. When I was too old for the training they gave to the little children, they sent me to the closest ward school as long as the law would let them; but I was never like any of the other children, and they all knew it. I’d to go and come like a prisoner, and be working around the Home early and late for me board and clothes. I always wanted to learn mighty bad, but I was glad when that was over.

“Every few days, all me life, I’d to be called up, looked over, and refused a home and love, on account of me hand and ugly face; but it was all the home I’d ever known, and I didn’t seem to belong to any place else.

===Chinese===
「俺说不上来。」弗雷克尔斯答道。这回答听在他自己耳朵里都觉得没了指望，便赶紧找补一句：「您瞧，是这样的，先生。人家拿了工钱，把善心整批整批地摊派下来，一份善心几百个孩子均摊，这样的善心，落在哪个孩子身上也渗不了多深。」

「接着说。」麦克莱恩点点头，一脸了然。

「也没什么值得耽误您工夫的事好讲。」弗雷克尔斯答道，「孤儿院在芝加哥（Chicago），俺打出生起就在那儿，直到三个月前。等俺长大些，过了他们教小娃娃的那套课，他们就按法律许下的期限，把俺送到最近的分区学校去念书；可俺从来跟别的孩子不一样，大家心里都有数。俺得像个囚犯似的早出晚归，还得在孤儿院里里外外起早贪黑地干活，挣俺的吃穿。俺一直想念书想得不行，可那段日子到头的时候，俺倒也松了口气。

「俺这辈子，隔三差五就得被叫上去，让人从头到脚打量一遍，再因为俺这只手、这张丑脸，被人回绝一回，领不走一个家、一份疼爱；可那好歹是俺认得的唯一的家，出了那儿，俺哪儿都像沾不上边。

===Original===
“Then a new superintendent was put in. He wasn’t for being like any of the others, and he swore he’d weed me out the first thing he did. He made a plan to send me down the State to a man he said he knew who needed a boy. He wasn’t for remembering to tell that man that I was a hand short, and he knocked me down the minute he found I was the boy who had been sent him. Between noon and that evening, he and his son close my age had me in pretty much the same shape in which I was found in the beginning, so I lay awake that night and ran away. I’d like to have squared me account with that boy before I left, but I didn’t dare for fear of waking the old man, and I knew I couldn’t handle the two of them; but I’m hoping to meet him alone some day before I die.”

McLean tugged at his mustache to hide the smile on his lips, but he liked the boy all the better for this confession.

“I didn’t even have to steal clothes to get rid of starting in me Home ones,” Freckles continued, “for they had already taken all me clean, neat things for the boy and put me into his rags, and that went almost as sore as the beatings, for where I was we were always kept tidy and sweet-smelling, anyway. I hustled clear into this State before I learned that man couldn’t have kept me if he’d wanted to. When I thought I was good and away from him, I commenced hunting work, but it is with everybody else just as it is with you, sir. Big, strong, whole men are the only ones for being wanted.”

===Chinese===
「后来换了个新院长。他跟旁人不一样，一上任就发狠，头一件事就要把俺清出去。他想法子把俺打发到本州南头，说是给一个他认得、正缺个小子使唤的人家。他偏偏想不起来告诉人家，俺少一只手；那人一见送来的是俺，抬手就把俺撂倒了。从晌午到那天傍晚，他跟他那个跟俺差不多大的儿子，把俺收拾得差不多成了俺当初被捡到时的那副模样。于是那天夜里俺躺着没合眼，爬起来就逃了。临走俺本想跟那小子把账算清，可又怕惊动那老头儿，也知道他们爷俩俺一个都对付不了；不过俺盼着，死前哪天能单独会一会他。」

麦克莱恩捻着小胡子，掩住唇边的笑意；可听了这番自陈，他反倒更喜欢这孩子了。

「俺逃走的时候，连衣裳都不必去偷来换下身上那套孤儿院的。」弗雷克尔斯接着说，「因为他们早把俺干净齐整的衣裳都剥去给了那小子，把他的破烂穿在了俺身上——这桩事几乎跟挨打一样戳心窝，因为不管怎么说，俺们待的那个地方，一向都让俺们穿得整整齐齐，干干净净带着股皂香。俺一路紧赶，赶进了这个州，才知道那人就算想留俺也留不住。等俺觉得稳稳当当甩脱了他，俺就开始找活儿干，可人人都跟您一个样，先生——要的净是些高大、强壮、四肢齐全的汉子。」

===Original===
“I have been studying over this matter,” answered McLean. “I am not so sure but that a man no older than you and similar in every way could do this work very well, if he were not a coward, and had it in him to be trustworthy and industrious.”

Freckles came forward a step.

“If you will give me a job where I can earn me food, clothes, and a place to sleep,” he said, “if I can have a Boss to work for like other men, and a place I feel I’ve a right to, I will do precisely what you tell me or die trying.”

He spoke so convincingly that McLean believed, although in his heart he knew that to employ a stranger would be wretched business for a man with the interests he had involved.

“Very well,” the Boss found himself answering, “I will enter you on my pay rolls. We’ll have supper, and then I will provide you with clean clothing, wading-boots, the wire-mending apparatus, and a revolver. The first thing in the morning, I will take you the length of the trail myself and explain fully what I want done. All I ask of you is to come to me at once at the south camp and tell me as a man if you find this job too hard for you. It will not surprise me. It is work that few men would perform faithfully. What name shall I put down?”

===Chinese===
「这件事我方才一直在心里盘算，」麦克莱恩答道，「如今我倒觉得，一个年纪不比你大、样样跟你相仿的人，未必就干不好这活儿——只要他不是个懦夫，骨子里信得过，又肯下力。」

弗雷克尔斯上前一步。

「只要您给俺一份活儿，让俺挣得着俺的饭食、衣裳和一个睡觉的地方，」他说，「只要俺也能像旁人一样，有个替他出力气的老板，有个俺觉得自个儿有权利待的地方，那您吩咐的事，俺就一丝不差地办到，不然就死在半道上。」

他说得那样恳切，麦克莱恩信了——尽管他心里明白，对他这样一个身家利害都押在上头的人来说，雇一个来历不明的陌生人，实在是一桩糟糕的买卖。

「好吧。」老板发觉自己已经答应了，「我把你记进我的工册。先吃晚饭，然后我给你置备干净衣裳、涉水靴、修铁丝网的工具，还有一把左轮手枪。明天一早，我亲自带你把整条巡林小道走一遍，把要办的事一样一样交代清楚。我对你的要求只有一条：一旦觉得这活儿太苦吃不住，就立刻到南边营地来找我，像个男子汉那样对我说实话。我不会觉得意外——这活儿，没有几个人能尽心尽力地干下去。我把什么名字记下呢？」

===Original===
Freckles’ gaze never left McLean’s face, and the Boss saw the swift spasm of pain that swept his lonely, sensitive features.

“I haven’t any name,” he said stubbornly, “no more than one somebody clapped on to me when they put me on the Home books, with not the thought or care they’d name a house cat. I’ve seen how they enter those poor little abandoned devils often enough to know. What they called me is no more my name than it is yours. I don’t know what mine is, and I never will; but I am going to be your man and do your work, and I’ll be glad to answer to any name you choose to call me. Won’t you please be giving me a name, Mr. McLean?”

The Boss wheeled abruptly and began stacking his books. What he was thinking was probably what any other gentleman would have thought in the circumstances. With his eyes still downcast, and in a voice harsh with huskiness, he spoke.

“I will tell you what we will do, my lad,” he said. “My father was my ideal man, and I loved him better than any other I have ever known. He went out five years ago, but that he would have been proud to leave you his name I firmly believe. If I give to you the name of my nearest kin and the man I loved best﻿—will that do?”

Freckles’ rigid attitude relaxed suddenly. His head dropped, and big tears splashed on the soiled calico shirt. McLean was not surprised at the silence, for he found that talking came none too easily just then.

===Chinese===
弗雷克尔斯的目光一刻也没有离开麦克莱恩的脸，老板看见一阵急剧的痛楚，掠过他那张孤单而敏感的面容。

「俺没有名字，」他倔强地说，「就算有，也不过是人家把俺记进孤儿院名册的时候，随手给俺扣上的一个，起的工夫连给一只家猫起名的那点心思和讲究都没费。那些被丢下的可怜小鬼是怎么个记法，俺见得多了，心里有数。他们叫俺的那个，算不得俺的名字，就跟它算不得您的一样。俺不知道俺的名字是什么，这辈子也不会知道了；可俺要做您的人、干您的活儿，您愿意叫俺什么，俺就欢欢喜喜地应什么。麦克莱恩先生，求您行行好，赏俺一个名字吧？」

老板猛地转过身去，动手把账簿摞起来。他心里想的，多半也是换了任何一位体面人身处这般境地都会想到的。他两眼仍旧低垂，用一副沙哑发紧的嗓音开了口。

「我告诉你咱们这么办，孩子。」他说，「我父亲是我心中完人的楷模，我爱他胜过我爱过的任何一个人。他五年前去世了，可我坚信，能把他的名字留给你，他在天上也会觉得骄傲。如果我把至亲的、也是我最爱的人的名字给你——这样行吗？」

弗雷克尔斯绷得紧紧的身子忽然松了下来。他垂下头，大颗大颗的泪珠砸在脏了的印花布衬衫上。麦克莱恩并不奇怪他不出声，因为他发现自己这时要开口，也着实不容易。

===Original===
“All right,” he said. “I will write it on the roll﻿—James Ross McLean.”

“Thank you mightily,” said Freckles. “That makes me feel almost as if I belonged, already.”

“You do,” said McLean. “Until someone armed with every right comes to claim you, you are mine. Now, come and take a bath, have some supper, and go to bed.”

As Freckles followed into the lights and sounds of the camp, his heart and soul were singing for joy.

===Chinese===
「好吧。」他说，「我就把它写上工册——詹姆斯·罗斯·麦克莱恩（James Ross McLean）。」

「多谢您了，多谢。」弗雷克尔斯说，「这一来，俺几乎觉得自己已经有归属了。」

「你已经有了。」麦克莱恩说，「除非哪天有个握着十足凭据的人来认领你，在那之前，你是我的。来吧，去洗个澡，吃点晚饭，睡觉去。」

弗雷克尔斯跟着走进营地的灯光和人声里，他的心和灵魂都在欢喜地歌唱。

## II Wherein Freckles proves his mettle and finds friends. / ## II 本章弗雷克尔斯显出胆气，交得朋友。

===Original===
Next morning found Freckles in clean, whole clothing, fed, and rested. Then McLean outfitted him and gave him careful instruction in the use of his weapon. The Boss showed him around the timberline, and engaged him a place to board with the family of his head teamster, Duncan, whom he had brought from Scotland with him, and who lived in a small clearing he was working out between the swamp and the corduroy. When the gang was started for the south camp, Freckles was left to guard a fortune in the Limberlost. That he was under guard himself those first weeks he never knew.
Each hour was torture to the boy. The restricted life of a great city orphanage was the other extreme of the world compared with the Limberlost. He was afraid for his life every minute. The heat was intense. The heavy wading-boots rubbed his feet until they bled. He was sore and stiff from his long tramp and outdoor exposure. The seven miles of trail was agony at every step. He practiced at night, under the direction of Duncan, until he grew sure in the use of his revolver. He cut a stout hickory cudgel, with a knot on the end as big as his fist; this never left his hand. What he thought in those first days he himself could not recall clearly afterward.
His heart stood still every time he saw the beautiful marsh-grass begin a sinuous waving *against* the play of the wind, as McLean had told him it would. He bolted half a mile with the first boom of the bittern, and his hat lifted with every yelp of the sheitpoke. Once he saw a lean, shadowy form following him, and fired his revolver. Then he was frightened worse than ever for fear it might have been Duncan’s collie.
The first afternoon that he found his wires down, and he was compelled to plunge knee deep into the black swamp-muck to restring them, he became so ill from fear and nervousness that he scarcely could control his shaking hand to do the work. With every step, he felt that he would miss secure footing and be swallowed in that clinging sea of blackness. In dumb agony he plunged forward, clinging to the posts and trees until he had finished restringing and testing the wire. He had consumed much time. Night closed in. The Limberlost stirred gently, then shook herself, growled, and awoke around him.

===Chinese===
第二天早晨，弗雷克尔斯（Freckles）已穿上干净囫囵的衣裳，吃饱歇足了。接着麦克莱恩（McLean）替他置备了行装，又就枪械的用法细细指点了他一番。老板（the Boss）领他把林界线（timberline）巡看了一圈，又在自己的车队领班邓肯（Duncan）家里替他订下了膳宿——这邓肯是老板从苏格兰随身带来的，住在沼泽与圆木路（corduroy）之间一小片正垦殖着的空地上。工队开赴南营之后，弗雷克尔斯便独自留下，看守林伯洛斯特（the Limberlost）沼泽里的一大笔财货。至于最初那几个星期里他自己也在别人的看守之下，他始终一无所知。
对这少年说来，每个钟头都是煎熬。大都市孤儿院里那种处处受拘束的日子，比起林伯洛斯特来，恰是世界上的两个极端。他无时无刻不在为自己的性命担惊。暑气炙人。沉重的涉水靴把他的双脚磨得见了血。长途跋涉加上露宿野外，他浑身又酸又僵。七英里的小道，步步都是苦刑。夜里他在邓肯指点下练枪，直到使起左轮手枪来渐渐有了把握。他砍了一根结实的山核桃木（hickory）棍子，末端一个木疙瘩足有他的拳头大；这棍子从此不离他的手。那些最初的日子里他心里都想了些什么，连他自己事后也追忆不清。
每逢他看见美丽的沼泽青草*逆着*风势开始蜿蜒摇曳——麦克莱恩早叮嘱过他会这样——他的心都要骤然一停。麻鳽（bittern）头一声鸣叫，惊得他撒腿跑出半英里；绿鹭（sheitpoke）每叫一声，他的帽子都要掀一掀。有一回，他瞥见一条精瘦的影子尾随着他，便开了一枪；随后想到那没准儿是邓肯的柯利牧羊犬（collie），更是吓得魂不附体。
头一个下午，他发现铁丝倒了，不得不蹚进没膝的黑沼泥里去把它们重新绷起来。恐惧和紧张弄得他难受至极，几乎管不住发抖的手来干活。每挪一步，他都觉得自己就要踏空，被那一片黏缠的黑海吞没。他默默忍着钻心的苦楚扑上前去，攀住木桩和树干，直到重新绷好、试完铁丝。这一来费去了许多工夫。夜幕四合。林伯洛斯特先是轻轻一动，继而抖擞身子，低吼一声，在他四周醒了过来。

===Original===
There seemed to be a great owl hooting from every hollow tree, and a little one screeching from every knothole. The bellowing of big bullfrogs was not sufficiently deafening to shut out the wailing of whip-poor-wills that seemed to come from every bush. Nighthawks swept past him with their shivering cry, and bats struck his face. A prowling wildcat missed its catch and screamed with rage. A straying fox bayed incessantly for its mate.
The hair on the back of Freckles’ neck arose as bristles, and his knees wavered beneath him. He could not see whether the dreaded snakes were on the trail, or, in the pandemonium, hear the rattle for which McLean had cautioned him to listen. He stood motionless in an agony of fear. His breath whistled between his teeth. The perspiration ran down his face and body in little streams.
Something big, black, and heavy came crashing through the swamp close to him, and with a yell of utter panic Freckles ran﻿—how far he did not know; but at last he gained control over himself and retraced his steps. His jaws set stiffly and the sweat dried on his body. When he reached the place from which he had started to run, he turned and with measured steps made his way down the line. After a time he realized that he was only walking, so he faced that sea of horrors again. When he came toward the corduroy, the cudgel fell to test the wire at each step.
Sounds that curdled his blood seemed to encompass him, and shapes of terror to draw closer and closer. Fear had so gained the mastery that he did not dare look behind him; and just when he felt that he would fall dead before he ever reached the clearing, came Duncan’s rolling call: “Freckles! Freckles!” A shuddering sob burst in the boy’s dry throat; but he only told Duncan that finding the wire down had caused the delay.

===Chinese===
仿佛每一棵空心树里都有一只大猫头鹰在鸣叫，每一个树节孔里都有一只小的在尖啸。大牛蛙们鼓噪咆哮，却还不够震耳，盖不住那仿佛从每一丛灌木里传出的三声夜鹰（whip-poor-will）的哀啼。美洲夜鹰（nighthawk）挟着颤巍巍的叫声从他身边扫过，蝙蝠扑打着他的脸。一头游荡的野猫（wildcat）扑空了猎物，恼得尖声长啸。一只离群的狐狸嗥个不停，声声唤着它的伴侣。
弗雷克尔斯后颈的头发根根倒竖，两条膝盖在身子底下直打晃。他看不清那些叫人胆寒的蛇是否盘在小道上；在这一片鼎沸的喧闹里，也听不出麦克莱恩叮嘱他留心细听的响尾蛇的响环声。他站在那里，吓得一动也不能动。气息从牙缝里嘘嘘地响。汗水顺着脸庞和身子，一道一道往下淌。
一个又大又黑又沉的物事哗啦啦地穿过沼泽，直冲他身边而来，弗雷克尔斯惊叫一声，撒腿便跑——跑了多远他自己也不知道；直到最后他克制住了自己，才循原路折回。他咬紧牙关，身上的汗也收干了。回到起跑的地方，他转过身，迈着沉稳的步子沿林界线走下去。走了一程，他发觉自己只是在走路罢了，便重又直面那一片恐怖之海。快到圆木路时，每走一步，木棍便敲一敲铁丝，一步一试地探着走。
令他血液凝冻的声响仿佛四面围拢，恐怖的形影越逼越近。恐惧彻底占了上风，他连头也不敢回；正当他觉得自己还没走到那片垦地就要倒毙的时候，传来了邓肯滚雷般的呼喊：“弗雷克尔斯！弗雷克尔斯！”一阵战栗的呜咽从少年干涩的喉咙里迸了出来；但他只对邓肯说，是发现铁丝倒了，才耽搁了这半天。

===Original===
The next morning he started on time. Day after day, with his heart pounding, he ducked, dodged, ran when he could, and fought when he was brought to bay. If he ever had an idea of giving up, no one knew it; for he clung to his job without the shadow of wavering. All these things, in so far as he guessed them, Duncan, who had been set to watch the first weeks of Freckles’ work, carried to the Boss at the south camp; but the innermost, exquisite torture of the thing the big Scotchman never guessed, and McLean, with his finer perceptions, came only a little closer.
After a few weeks, when Freckles learned that he was still living, that he had a home, and the very first money he ever had possessed was safe in his pockets, he began to grow proud. He yet sidestepped, dodged, and hurried to avoid being late again, but he was gradually developing the fearlessness that men ever acquire of dangers to which they are hourly accustomed.
His heart seemed to be leaping when his first rattler disputed the trail with him, but he mustered courage to attack it with his club. After its head had been crushed, he mastered an Irishman’s inborn repugnance for snakes sufficiently to cut off its rattles to show Duncan. With this victory, his greatest fear of them was gone.
Then he began to realize that with the abundance of food in the swamp, flesh-hunters would not come on the trail and attack him, and he had his revolver for defence if they did. He soon learned to laugh at the big, floppy birds that made horrible noises. One day, watching behind a tree, he saw a crane solemnly performing a few measures of a belated nuptial song-and-dance with his mate. Realizing that it was intended in tenderness, no matter how it appeared, the lonely, starved heart of the boy sympathized with them.

===Chinese===
第二天早晨，他准时上路。日复一日，他心口怦怦直跳，能躲便躲，能闪便闪，跑得掉就跑，被逼得走投无路了就拼。他若起过一丝撂挑子的念头，也没有人知道；因为他抱着这份差事，从没有过半点动摇。这些事，凡是他料想得到的，都由邓肯——头几个星期奉命照看弗雷克尔斯干活的人——一一带去禀报了南营的老板；只是这件事最深处的、精微的苦刑，那大个子苏格兰人从不曾猜着，而麦克莱恩凭着更细腻的知觉，也只靠近了一小步。
过了几个星期，弗雷克尔斯知道自己还活着，有了一个家，平生头一笔属于自己的钱正稳稳当当揣在口袋里，他便渐渐生出几分自豪。他仍旧侧身闪避、东躲西让，也仍旧急急赶路，免得再误了时辰；但他正一天天养成那股无所畏惧的胆气——对于朝夕相处的危险，人向来都会养成这样的胆量。
头一条响尾蛇跟他争起小道来的时候，他的心仿佛要从腔子里蹦出来；但他鼓足勇气，抡起木棍向它进攻。蛇头砸烂之后，他又强压下爱尔兰人对蛇与生俱来的憎恶，割下那串响环，带去给邓肯看。经此一胜，他对蛇最深的恐惧烟消云散了。
随后他渐渐明白过来：沼泽里吃食这样丰盛，那些猎肉的野兽犯不上寻到小道上来袭击他；真来了，他手里还有左轮手枪护身。不久，那些发出骇人怪叫的大扁翅鸟，他也能笑着看待了。有一天，他躲在树后张望，看见一只鹤（crane）正与它的伴侣郑重其事地表演几节迟到的婚舞婚歌，一板一眼，煞有介事。那少年孤寂饥渴的心看出：不论模样如何，这终究是出于一片柔情，便由衷地同情起它们来。

===Original===
Before the first month passed, he was fairly easy about his job; by the next he rather liked it. Nature can be trusted to work her own miracle in the heart of any man whose daily task keeps him alone among her sights, sounds, and silences.
When day after day the only thing that relieved his utter loneliness was the companionship of the birds and beasts of the swamp, it was the most natural thing in the world that Freckles should turn to them for friendship. He began by instinctively protecting the weak and helpless. He was astonished at the quickness with which they became accustomed to him and the disregard they showed for his movements, when they learned that he was not a hunter, while the club he carried was used more frequently for their benefit than his own. He scarcely could believe what he saw.
From the effort to protect the birds and animals, it was only a short step to the possessive feeling, and with that sprang the impulse to caress and provide. Through fall, when brooding was finished and the upland birds sought the swamp in swarms to feast on its seeds and berries, Freckles was content with watching them and speculating about them. Outside of half a dozen of the very commonest they were strangers to him. The likeness of their actions to humanity was an hourly surprise.
When black frost began stripping the Limberlost, cutting the ferns, shearing the vines from the trees, mowing the succulent green things of the swale, and setting the leaves swirling down, he watched the departing troops of his friends with dismay. He began to realize that he would be left alone. He made especial efforts toward friendliness with the hope that he could induce some of them to stay. It was then that he conceived the idea of carrying food to the birds; for he saw that they were leaving for lack of it; but he could not stop them. Day after day, flocks gathered and departed: by the time the first snow whitened his trail around the Limberlost, there were left only the little black-and-white juncos, the sapsuckers, yellowhammers, a few patriarchs among the flaming cardinals, the blue jays, the crows, and the quail.

===Chinese===
头一个月还没过完，他对这份差事已经相当安心；到第二个月，他竟有点儿喜欢上了。一个人的日常活计若叫他独自处在大自然的形色、声响与静默之间，大自然必会在他心里行她自己的神迹——这一点尽可放心。
日复一日，唯一能排遣他那彻骨孤独的，只有沼泽里禽鸟野兽的陪伴；于是弗雷克尔斯转而向它们去求友谊，便是天下再自然不过的事了。他起先是出于本能，护惜那些弱小无依的。待它们认明他不是猎户、他手里那根棍子更多是为它们而不是为他自己使唤的时候，它们那么快便跟他厮熟、那么不把他的一举一动放在心上，倒叫他吃惊不小。他几乎不敢相信自己亲眼所见的一切。
由护惜这些禽鸟野兽，到把它们当作自己所有的，中间只隔着一小步；有了这份据为己有之情，跟着便生出抚爱供养的心思。整个秋天，育雏的事一了，高地的鸟便成群结队涌进沼泽，来赴它种子浆果的盛宴；弗雷克尔斯光是望着它们、琢磨它们，就已经心满意足。除了六种顶顶常见的之外，其余于他都是生客。它们的一举一动与人类何其相似，时时刻刻都叫他称奇。
待到黑霜开始剥林伯洛斯特的衣裳——割倒蕨草，剪去树上的藤蔓，刈平沼泽草甸（swale）里多汁的青绿，卷得满树叶子打着旋儿往下落——他眼睁睁望着朋友们一队队开拔，心里惶惶不安。他开始意识到自己就要孤身一人了。他格外用心同它们亲善，巴望劝得动几位留下来。也正是那时，他起了一个念头，要给鸟儿送吃食去；因为他看出来，它们是为着缺食才走的；可他留不住它们。鸟群一日日聚拢了又启程：到头一场雪把林伯洛斯特四周的小道染白的时候，留下来的只有小小的黑白两色灯芯草鹀（junco）、吸汁啄木鸟（sapsucker）、金翼啄木鸟（yellowhammer），火红的北美红雀（cardinal）里几位族长，还有蓝松鸦（blue jay）、乌鸦和鹌鹑（quail）。

===Original===
Then Freckles began his wizard work. He cleared a space of swale, and twice a day he spread a birds’ banquet. By the middle of December the strong winds of winter had beaten most of the seed from the grass and bushes. The snow fell, covering the swamp, and food was very scarce and difficult to find. The birds scarcely waited until Freckles’ back was turned to attack his provisions. In a few weeks they flew toward the clearing to meet him. During the bitter weather of January they came halfway to the cabin every morning, and fluttered around him as doves all the way to the feeding-ground. Before February they were so accustomed to him, and so hunger-driven, that they would perch on his head and shoulders, and the saucy jays would try to pry into his pockets.
Then Freckles added to wheat and crumbs, every scrap of refuse food he could find at the cabin. He carried to his pets the parings of apples, turnips, potatoes, stray cabbage-leaves, and carrots, and tied to the bushes meat-bones having scraps of fat and gristle. One morning, coming to his feeding-ground unusually early, he found a gorgeous cardinal and a rabbit side by side sociably nibbling a cabbage-leaf, and that instantly gave to him the idea of cracking nuts, from the store he had gathered for Duncan’s children, for the squirrels, in the effort to add them to his family. Soon he had them coming﻿—red, gray, and black; then he became filled with a vast impatience that he did not know their names or habits.
So the winter passed. Every week McLean rode to the Limberlost; never on the same day or at the same hour. Always he found Freckles at his work, faithful and brave, no matter how severe the weather.

===Chinese===
于是弗雷克尔斯开始施展他的魔法。他清理出一片草甸，一天两次摆下鸟儿的筵席。到十二月中旬，凛冽的冬风已把草丛灌木上的种子大多刮落。雪下了下来，盖住沼泽，吃食又稀少又难寻。鸟儿们等不得弗雷克尔斯转过身去，便扑上来抢他备下的口粮。几个星期之后，它们就朝垦地飞来迎他了。一月里天气最酷烈的那几天，它们每天早晨都飞到半路上来接他，像鸽子似的绕着他扑腾，一路陪到喂食场。没到二月，它们已跟他熟到、也饿到了这个地步：肯落在他头上肩上，那些没正形的松鸦还要来翻他的口袋。
于是弗雷克尔斯在麦粒和面包屑之外，又添上小木屋里找得到的一切残食。他给他的宠儿们送去苹果皮、芜菁皮、土豆皮、散落的白菜叶和胡萝卜，把带着零星肥肉和筋头巴脑的肉骨头拴在灌木上。一天早晨，他到喂食场到得格外早，看见一只艳丽的红雀和一只兔子并排蹲着，亲亲热合同啃一片白菜叶；这情景顿时让他开了窍：把原先给邓肯的孩子们攒下的坚果敲开来喂松鼠，好把它们也收进自己的家眷。不多久，松鼠们也招之即来了——红的、灰的、黑的；跟着他又满心发起急来：自己竟不认得它们的名字，也不知道它们的习性。
冬天就这样过去了。麦克莱恩每星期都骑马来林伯洛斯特；日子从不固定，时辰也从不重样。不论天气多么严酷，他回回都看见弗雷克尔斯守在岗位上，尽忠职守，勇敢无畏。

===Original===
The boy’s earnings constituted his first money; and when the Boss explained to him that he could leave them safe at a bank and carry away a scrap of paper that represented the amount, he went straight on every payday and made his deposit, keeping out barely what was necessary for his board and clothing. What he wanted to do with his money he did not know, but it gave to him a sense of freedom and power to feel that it was there﻿—it was his and he could have it when he chose. In imitation of McLean, he bought a small pocket account-book, in which he carefully set down every dollar he earned and every penny he spent. As his expenses were small and the Boss paid him generously, it was astonishing how his little hoard grew.
That winter held the first hours of real happiness in Freckles’ life. He was free. He was doing a man’s work faithfully, through every rigor of rain, snow, and blizzard. He was gathering a wonderful strength of body, paying his way, and saving money. Every man of the gang and of that locality knew that he was under the protection of McLean, who was a power, this had the effect of smoothing Freckles’ path in many directions.
Mrs. Duncan showed him that individual kindness for which his hungry heart was longing. She had a hot drink ready for him when he came from a freezing day on the trail. She knit him a heavy mitten for his left hand, and devised a way to sew and pad the right sleeve that protected the maimed arm in bitter weather. She patched his clothing﻿—frequently torn by the wire﻿—and saved kitchen scraps for his birds, not because she either knew or cared anything about them, but because she herself was close enough to the swamp to be touched by its utter loneliness. When Duncan laughed at her for this, she retorted: “My God, mannie, if Freckles hadna the birds and the beasts he would be always alone. It was never meant for a human being to be so solitary. He’d get touched in the head if he hadna them to think for and to talk to.”

===Chinese===
这少年挣下的工钱，是他平生头一笔钱；老板向他讲明，这笔钱可以稳稳当当存在银行里，只消带走一张写明数目的纸片，于是每逢发薪日，他都径直去存上一笔，只留下膳宿衣著刚够用的数目。这笔钱拿来做什么用，他自己也不知道；但只要想到它在那里——它是他的，想什么时候要就什么时候能拿到手——他便觉出一种自由，一种力量。他学着麦克莱恩的样子，买了一本小小的袖珍账簿，挣来的每一美元、花掉的每一分钱，都仔仔细细记上去。他开销既小，老板发给他的工钱又慷慨，那一小笔积蓄长起来之快，真叫人吃惊。
那个冬天里，有弗雷克尔斯平生最初几小时的真正的幸福。他自由了。他尽忠做着男子汉的活计，无论雨雪冰霜、狂风怒号，一日不歇。他攒下一副了不起的筋骨，自食其力，还有了储蓄。工队里的人和那一带的乡邻个个知道，他受着麦克莱恩的庇护——麦克莱恩是个有势力的人——这就替弗雷克尔斯在许多方面把路铺平了。
邓肯太太（Mrs. Duncan）对他怀着一种他饥渴的心久所渴慕的、体己的慈爱。他在小道上冻了一整天回来，总有一杯热腾腾的饮料给他备着。她给他左手织了一只厚厚的手闷子，又想出法子把右边袖子缝棉加衬，好教那条残臂在苦寒天气里不受亏。她替他补衣裳——衣裳常叫铁丝刮破——还替他的鸟儿攒下厨房里的下脚；倒不是她认得那些鸟、把它们放在心上，只因她自己离沼泽够近，也叫那份彻骨的孤寂给触着了。邓肯为这个取笑她，她回敬道：“老天爷，老伙计！弗雷克勒斯要是没有这些鸟儿野兽，就得永远孤零零一个人。造化从来不容许一个人这样孤单地活着。要是没有它们给他操心、陪他说话，他脑子是会出毛病的。”

===Original===
“How much answer do ye think he gets to his talkin’, lass?” laughed Duncan.
“He gets the answer that keeps the eye bright, the heart happy, and the feet walking faithful the rough path he’s set them in,” answered Mrs. Duncan earnestly.
Duncan walked away appearing very thoughtful. The next morning he gave an ear from the corn he was shelling for his chickens to Freckles, and told him to carry it to his wild chickens in the Limberlost. Freckles laughed delightedly.
“Me chickens!” he said. “Why didn’t I ever think of that before? Of course they are! They are just little, brightly colored cocks and hens! But ‘wild’ is no good. What would you say to me ‘wild chickens’ being a good deal tamer than yours here in your yard?”

===Chinese===
“你看他那些话能得着几分回音，丫头？”邓肯笑道。
“他得着的回音，能让眼睛亮着，心快活着，还能让两只脚忠心耿耿，走完他给它们安排下的那条崎岖路。”邓肯太太一本正经地答道。
邓肯走开了，一副若有所思的样子。第二天早晨，他从正剥着喂自家鸡的玉米里抽出一穗，给了弗雷克尔斯，叫他捎去给林伯洛斯特里他那些野鸡。弗雷克勒斯欢畅地笑了。
“俺的鸡！”他说。“俺以前怎么就没想到呢？可不是嘛！它们可不就是一群花花绿绿的小公鸡小母鸡！不过‘野’字可不对劲儿。俺那些‘野鸡’比你院子里的这些还驯得多，你说这话该怎么说？”

===Original===
“Hoot, lad!” cried Duncan.
“Make yours light on your head and eat out of your hands and pockets,” challenged Freckles.
“Go and tell your fairy tales to the wee people! They’re juist brash on believin’ things,” said Duncan. “Ye canna invent any story too big to stop them from callin’ for a bigger.”
“I dare you to come see!” retorted Freckles.

===Chinese===
“嗬，娃儿！”邓肯叫道。
“叫你的鸡落到你头上、从你手心里口袋里吃食，”弗雷克尔斯将了他一军。
“快把你的神仙故事讲给小家伙们听去吧！他们正是一听就信的年纪，”邓肯说。“你编的故事再大也白搭——他们听了，还要嚷嚷着叫你编个更大的。”
“你敢不敢来看看！”弗雷克尔斯回敬道。

===Original===
“Take ye!” said Duncan. “If ye make juist ane bird licht on your heid or eat frae your hand, ye are free to help yoursel’ to my corncrib and wheat bin the rest of the winter.”
Freckles sprang in air and howled in glee.
“Oh, Duncan! You’re too, aisy,” he cried. “When will you come?”
“I’ll come next Sabbath,” said Duncan. “And I’ll believe the birds of the Limberlost are tame as barnyard fowl when I see it, and no sooner!”

===Chinese===
“就这么定！”邓肯说。“只要你叫哪怕一只鸟落到你头顶上，或是从你手里吃食，这个冬天剩下的日子，我的玉米仓和小麦柜，你尽管随便取用。”
弗雷克尔斯一蹦老高，乐得直欢呼。
“哦，邓肯！你这人可太好说话啦，”他嚷道。“你几时来？”
“我下个安息日来，”邓肯说。“林伯洛斯特的鸟儿跟场院里的家禽一样驯——这话我得亲眼见了才信，早一天都不成！”

===Original===
After that Freckles always spoke of the birds as his chickens, and the Duncans followed his example. The very next Sabbath, Duncan, with his wife and children, followed Freckles to the swamp. They saw a sight so wonderful it will keep them talking all the remainder of their lives, and make them unfailing friends of all the birds.
Freckles’ chickens were awaiting him at the edge of the clearing. They cut the frosty air around his head into curves and circles of crimson, blue, and black. They chased each other from Freckles, and swept so closely themselves that they brushed him with their outspread wings.
At their feeding-ground Freckles set down his old pail of scraps and swept the snow from a small level space with a broom improvised of twigs. As soon as his back was turned, the birds clustered over the food, snatching scraps to carry to the nearest bushes. Several of the boldest, a big crow and a couple of jays, settled on the rim and feasted at leisure, while a cardinal, that hesitated to venture, fumed and scolded from a twig overhead.
Then Freckles scattered his store. At once the ground resembled the spread mantle of Montezuma, except that this mass of gaily colored feathers was on the backs of living birds. While they feasted, Duncan gripped his wife’s arm and stared in astonishment; for from the bushes and dry grass, with gentle cheeping and queer, throaty chatter, as if to encourage each other, came flocks of quail. Before anyone saw it arrive, a big gray rabbit sat in the midst of the feast, contentedly gnawing a cabbage-leaf.

===Chinese===
从此弗雷克尔斯说起那些鸟，总管它们叫“俺的鸡”，邓肯一家也依样跟着叫。就在下一个安息日，邓肯带着妻儿，跟着弗雷克尔斯进了沼泽。他们见着的那一幕实在奇妙，够他们念叨后半辈子，也够叫他们一辈子做众鸟不离不弃的朋友。
弗雷克尔斯的鸡早在垦地边上候着他了。它们绕着他的头顶，把霜天的空气裁成一道道绯红、湛蓝与乌黑的弧线圆圈。它们一只只从弗雷克尔斯身边互相追赶着飞开，又一只只贴着他自家掠得那样近，张开的翅膀都扫着他的身子。
到了喂食场，弗雷克尔斯放下那口盛残食的旧桶，拿一把用树枝现扎的扫帚，扫出小小一块平地。他刚一转身，鸟儿们便围拢到食物上，抢了残食就往最近的灌木丛里叼。几只胆子最大的——一只大乌鸦和两只松鸦——索性落上桶沿，从容大嚼；头顶的小枝上，一只迟迟不敢下场的红雀憋着一肚子火，骂个不休。
随后弗雷克尔斯撒开他带的口粮。刹那间，地面就像铺开了蒙特祖马（Montezuma）的羽氅，只是这一大片斑斓彩羽，是披在活鸟背上的。众鸟大嚼之际，邓肯紧紧抓住妻子的胳膊，瞪大了眼睛；只见一丛丛灌木和干草里，伴着轻轻的啾鸣和古怪的喉音絮语——仿佛在互相壮胆——走出来一群群鹌鹑。

===Original===
“Weel, I be drawed on!” came Mrs. Duncan’s tense whisper.
“Shu-shu,” cautioned Duncan.
Lastly Freckles removed his cap. He began filling it with handfuls of wheat from his pockets. In a swarm the grain-eaters arose around him as a flock of tame pigeons. They perched on his arms and the cap, and in the stress of hunger, forgetting all caution, a brilliant cock cardinal and an equally gaudy jay fought for a perching-place on his head.
“Weel, I’m beat,” muttered Duncan, forgetting the silence imposed on his wife. “I’ll hae to give in. ‘Seein’ is believin’.’ A man wad hae to see that to believe it. We mauna let the Boss miss that sight, for it’s a chance will no likely come twice in a life. Everything is snowed under and thae craturs near starved, but trustin’ Freckles that complete they are tamer than our chickens. Look hard, bairns!” he whispered. “Ye winna see the like o’ yon again, while God lets ye live. Notice their color against the ice and snow, and the pretty skippin’ ways of them! And spunky! Weel, I’m heat fair!”

===Chinese===
“哎呀，俺可开了眼了！”传来邓肯太太紧绷绷的耳语。
“嘘——嘘，”邓肯小声止住她。
最后，弗雷克尔斯摘下帽子，开始一把一把从口袋里掏出麦粒往帽子里盛。食谷的鸟儿们像一群家鸽，嗡的一声在他四周腾起。它们落上他的手臂和帽子；饥饿当头，什么小心都忘了个干净，一只鲜亮的雄红雀和一只同样花哨的松鸦，竟为他头顶上的一个落点争斗起来。
“哎呀，俺服了，”邓肯喃喃道，忘了自己方才还嘱咐妻子别出声。“俺只好认输。‘眼见为实。’这样的场面，不亲眼瞧见是断乎不能信的。咱们千万别让老板错过这一景，这样的机缘，一辈子怕也难逢第二回。天底下全叫雪埋了，这些小东西眼看要饿死，可它们信弗雷克尔斯信到这个地步，竟比咱家的鸡还驯。仔细看，娃儿们！”他悄声道。“只要上帝还叫你们活着，这样的景致再也见不着第二回了。瞧它们衬着冰雪的颜色，瞧它们那轻盈蹦跳的身法！还有那股子精气神！哎呀，俺浑身都热了！”

===Original===
Freckles emptied his cap, turned his pockets and scattered his last grain. Then he waved his watching friends goodbye and started down the timberline.
A week later, Duncan and Freckles arose from breakfast to face the bitterest morning of the winter. When Freckles, warmly capped and gloved, stepped to the corner of the kitchen for his scrap-pail, he found a big pan of steaming boiled wheat on the top of it. He wheeled to Mrs. Duncan with a shining face.
“Were you fixing this warm food for me chickens or yours?” he asked.
“It’s for yours, Freckles,” she said. “I was afeared this cold weather they wadna lay good without a warm bite now and then.”

===Chinese===
弗雷克尔斯倒空帽子，翻出口袋，撒下最后一把麦粒。然后他朝注视着他的朋友们挥手作别，沿林界线走下去了。
一星期后，邓肯和弗雷克尔斯用过早饭，起身去迎这个冬天最凛冽的一个早晨。弗雷克尔斯戴暖了帽子、套好手套，走到厨房角落去取他的残食桶，却发现桶顶上搁着一大盘冒着热气的煮麦子。他转过身来望着邓肯太太，满脸放光。
“这热乎的吃食，您是给俺的鸡备的，还是给您的鸡备的？”他问。
“是给你的，弗雷克尔斯，”她说。“天这么冷，我生怕它们不时不时吃口热乎的，就下不好蛋了。”

===Original===
Duncan laughed as he stepped to the other room for his pipe; but Freckles faced Mrs. Duncan with a trace of every pang of starved mother-hunger he ever had suffered written large on his homely, splotched, narrow features.
“Oh, how I wish you were my mother!” he cried.
Mrs. Duncan attempted an echo of her husband’s laugh.
“Lord love the lad!” she exclaimed. “Why, Freckles, are ye no bright enough to learn without being taught by a woman that I am your mither? If a great man like yoursel’ dinna ken that, learn it now and ne’er forget it. Ance a woman is the wife of any man, she becomes wife to all men for having had the wifely experience she kens! Ance a man-child has beaten his way to life under the heart of a woman, she is mither to all men, for the hearts of mithers are everywhere the same. Bless ye, laddie, I am your mither!”

===Chinese===
邓肯到隔壁屋里去取烟斗，一路笑着；弗雷克尔斯却正对着邓肯太太，他那张相貌平常、又窄又多斑点的脸上，明明白白写着他此生饱尝过的每一阵渴念亲娘的痛楚。
“啊，俺多想您就是俺的亲娘！”他喊道。
邓肯太太想跟着丈夫笑一声，却没有笑出来。
“上帝疼这个孩子！”她脱口喊道。“嗐，弗雷克尔斯，难道你还不够能干、非得一个女人来点破，才知道俺是你娘吗？像你这样一条大汉若还不明白这个，那就趁现在弄明白，一辈子也别忘。一个女人但凡做过哪个男人的妻子，凭着她做妻子的那份阅历，她便成了天下男人的妻子！一个男娃娃但凡在女人的心口底下闯出一条命来，她便是天下男人的娘——因为做娘的心，天底下处处都是一样的。上帝保佑你，孩子，俺就是你的娘！”

===Original===
She tucked the coarse scarf she had knit for him closer over his chest and pulled his cap lower over his ears, but Freckles, whipping it off and holding it under his arm, caught her rough, reddened hand and pressed it to his lips in a long kiss. Then he hurried away to hide the happy, embarrassing tears that were coming straight from his swelling heart.
Mrs. Duncan, sobbing unrestrainedly, swept into the adjoining room and threw herself into Duncan’s arms.
“Oh, the puir lad!” she wailed. “Oh, the puir mither-hungry lad! He breaks my heart!”
Duncan’s arms closed convulsively around his wife. With a big, brown hand he lovingly stroked her rough, sorrel hair.

===Chinese===
她替他把亲手织的那条粗围巾往胸口掖得更紧，又把他的帽子往下拉了拉，盖住耳朵；弗雷克尔斯却一把将帽子扯下来夹在腋下，捉起她那只粗糙发红的手，久久地按在自己的唇上。随后他急急走开，好去藏起那阵直往上涌的、又欢喜又害臊的眼泪——那是一颗涨满的心里，直截了当涌出来的。
邓肯太太再也忍不住呜咽，一阵风似的冲进隔壁房间，扑进邓肯怀里。
“啊，可怜的孩子！”她哭着说。“啊，可怜这渴娘的孩子！他叫俺的心都碎了！”
邓肯的双臂紧紧搂住妻子。他用一只棕色的大手，怜爱地抚着她那头粗糙的红发。

===Original===
“Sarah, you’re a guid woman!” he said. “You’re a michty guid woman! Ye hae a way o’ speakin’ out at times that’s like the inspired prophets of the Lord. If that had been put to me, now, I’d ’a’ felt all I kent how to and been keen enough to say the richt thing; but dang it, I’d ’a’ stuttered and stammered and got naething out that would ha’ done onybody a mite o’ good. But ye, Sarah! Did ye see his face, woman? Ye sent him off lookin’ leke a white light of holiness had passed ower and settled on him. Ye sent the lad away too happy for mortal words, Sarah. And ye made me that proud o’ ye! I wouldna trade ye an’ my share o’ the Limberlost with ony king ye could mention.”
He relaxed his clasp, and setting a heavy hand on each shoulder, he looked straight into her eyes.
“Ye’re prime, Sarah! Juist prime!” he said.
Sarah Duncan stood alone in the middle of her two-roomed log cabin and lifted a bony, clawlike pair of hands, reddened by frequent immersion in hot water, cracked and chafed by exposure to cold, black-lined by constant battle with swamp-loam, calloused with burns, and stared at them wonderingly.
“Pretty-lookin’ things ye are!” she whispered. “But ye hae juist been kissed. And by such a man! Fine as God ever made at His verra best. Duncan wouldna trade wi’ a king! Na! Nor I wadna trade with a queen wi’ a palace, an’ velvet gowns, an’ diamonds big as hazelnuts, an’ a hundred visitors a day into the bargain. Ye’ve been that honored I’m blest if I can bear to souse ye in dishwater. Still, that kiss winna come off! Naething can take it from me, for it’s mine till I dee. Lord, if I amna proud! Kisses on these old claws! Weel, I be drawed on!”

===Chinese===
“莎拉（Sarah），你是个好女人！”他说。“你是个了不得的好女人！你有一种本事，逢到节骨眼上便脱口而出，像主的众先知受了默示一般。这话要是搁到我身上，我也会把我知道的滋味都受一遍，也急着想把话说得妥帖；可是见鬼，我准得结结巴巴、颠三倒四，一句于人有益的话也吐不出来。可你呢，莎拉！你瞧见他的脸了没有，婆娘？你打发他走的时候，他那模样，就像有一道圣洁的白光拂过他身上、落定在他身上了。你叫那孩子走得那份欢天喜地，人间的言语都形容不尽，莎拉。你叫我多么替你骄傲！随你举出哪一位国王来，我也绝不拿你，加上我在林伯洛斯特的那份家业，去同他换。”
他松开怀抱，两只沉重的大手各按住她一边肩膀，直直地望进她的眼睛。
“你是头一流的，莎拉！顶顶好的头一流！”他说。
莎拉·邓肯独自站在她那两间屋的木屋正中，举起一双瘦骨嶙峋、爪子似的手——这双手，叫滚热的水泡得通红，叫寒天冻得皴裂，叫同沼泽淤泥的常年厮打染上道道黑痕，叫炉火烧得结了茧——怔怔地端详着它们。
“你们这模样儿，可真俊俏！”她悄声道。“可你们方才偏偏叫人亲过了。还是这么一个人亲的！上帝使出他十足的功夫、攒着劲儿造出来的头等人物。邓肯不肯拿你换国王！是不换！俺也不拿你们换王后——哪怕她有宫殿，有天鹅绒的裙子，有榛子那么大的钻石，外带一天一百位客人来道贺。你们享了这样的体面，俺真是舍不得再把你们摁进洗碗水里去。再说，那一吻也洗不掉啦！什么也夺它不走，因为到俺闭眼那天为止，它都是俺的。主啊，瞧俺多风光！这样一双老爪子上，落了吻！哎呀，俺可真是开了眼了！”

## III Wherein a feather falls and a soul is born. / ## III 本章一根羽毛落下，一个灵魂降生。

===Original===
So Freckles fared through the bitter winter. He was very happy. He had hungered for freedom, love, and appreciation so long! He had been unspeakably lonely at the Home; and the utter loneliness of a great desert or forest is not so difficult to endure as the loneliness of being constantly surrounded by crowds of people who do not care in the least whether one is living or dead.

All through the winter Freckles’ entire energy was given to keeping up his lines and his “chickens” from freezing or starving. When the first breath of spring touched the Limberlost, and the snow receded before it; when the catkins began to bloom; when there came a hint of green to the trees, bushes, and swale; when the rushes lifted their heads, and the pulse of the newly resurrected season beat strongly in the heart of nature, something new stirred in the breast of the boy.

Nature always levies her tribute. Now she laid a powerful hand on the soul of Freckles, to which the boy’s whole being responded, though he had not the least idea what was troubling him. Duncan accepted his wife’s theory that it was a touch of spring fever, but Freckles knew better. He never had been so well. Clean, hot, and steady the blood pulsed in his veins. He was always hungry, and his most difficult work tired him not at all. For long months, without a single intermission, he had tramped those seven miles of trail twice each day, through every conceivable state of weather. With the heavy club he gave his wires a sure test, and between sections, first in play, afterward to keep his circulation going, he had acquired the skill of an expert drum major. In his work there was exercise for every muscle of his body each hour of the day, at night a bath, wholesome food, and sound sleep in a room that never knew fire. He had gained flesh and color, and developed a greater strength and endurance than anyone ever could have guessed.

Nor did the Limberlost contain last year’s terrors. He had been with her in her hour of desolation, when stripped bare and deserted, she had stood shivering, as if herself afraid. He had made excursions into the interior until he was familiar with every path and road that ever had been cut. He had sounded the depths of her deepest pools, and had learned why the trees grew so magnificently. He had found that places of swamp and swale were few compared with miles of solid timberland, concealed by summer’s luxuriant undergrowth.

===Chinese===
就这样，弗雷克尔斯熬过了那个苦寒的冬天。他快活得很。自由、爱还有赏识，他渴慕得太久太久了！在孤儿院里，他曾说不出的孤单；其实大漠或密林那种彻底的荒凉，也远不及终日被人丛包围、却无一人把你放在心上、不管你死活的那种孤单难熬。

整个冬天，弗雷克尔斯的全部心力都用在维护铁丝线路、不让他的「鸡儿们」冻着饿着上。当春的第一缕气息拂过林伯洛斯特，积雪在它面前退去；当柔荑花开始绽放；当树木、灌木和沼泽草甸透出一线新绿；当灯心草昂起头来，新生的季节在大自然的心头强劲搏动——某种新的东西，便在这少年的胸口骚动起来。

大自然总要收取她的贡赋。此刻她把一只强有力的手按在弗雷克尔斯的灵魂上，少年全副身心都应和着，却全然不知是什么在搅扰自己。邓肯赞成太太的推断，说这是染了点春倦，弗雷克尔斯心里却明白不是。他从没这样康健过。血液在血管里搏动，洁净、滚热而平稳。他总是胃口大开，最重的活计也一点累不着他。一连好几个月，他不曾间断过一天，每天两趟徒步巡完那七英里小道，什么天气都赶过。他用那根沉甸甸的木棒敲查铁丝，稳稳当当；段与段之间，起初是打着玩儿，后来是为了让血脉活络，他竟练出了老练鼓乐队长一般的娴熟手法。他的活计使他全身每块肌肉一天到晚时时得到活动，夜里洗个澡，吃下可口滋养的饭食，在一间从不生火的屋子里酣然入睡。他长了肉，添了血色，练出了谁也料想不到的气力和耐力。

如今的林伯洛斯特，也不复去年那些骇人的景象了。他曾陪着她度过荒凉的时辰——那时她被剥得精光、遭人抛下，立在那里瑟瑟发抖，仿佛自己也害怕似的。他一次次深入腹地，把历来砍出的每一条小径和道路都摸熟了。他测过她最深水潭的深浅，也明白了这里的树木为什么长得这般壮美。他发现，比起绵延多少英里的坚实林地，真正的沼泽和草甸其实没有几处，只是让夏日繁茂的下层草木遮掩住了。

===Original===
The sounds that at first had struck cold fear into his soul he now knew had left on wing and silent foot at the approach of winter. As flock after flock of the birds returned and he recognized the old echoes reawakening, he found to his surprise that he had been lonely for them and was hailing their return with great joy. All his fears were forgotten. Instead, he was possessed of an overpowering desire to know what they were, to learn where they had been, and whether they would make friends with him as the winter birds had done; and if they did, would they be as fickle? For, with the running sap, creeping worm, and winging bug, most of Freckles’ “chickens” had deserted him, entered the swamp, and feasted to such a state of plethora on its store that they cared little for his supply, so that in the strenuous days of mating and nest-building the boy was deserted.

He chafed at the birds’ ingratitude, but he found speedy consolation in watching and befriending the newcomers. He surely would have been proud and highly pleased if he had known that many of the former inhabitants of the interior swamp now grouped their nests beside the timberline solely for the sake of his protection and company.

The yearly resurrection of the Limberlost is a mighty revival. Freckles stood back and watched with awe and envy the gradual reclothing and repopulation of the swamp. Keen-eyed and alert through danger and loneliness, he noted every stage of development, from the first piping frog and unsheathing bud, to full leafage and the return of the last migrant.

The knowledge of his complete loneliness and utter insignificance was hourly thrust upon him. He brooded and fretted until he was in a fever; yet he never guessed the cause. He was filled with a vast impatience, a longing that he scarcely could endure.

===Chinese===
当初一响便叫他灵魂里生出寒意的那些声音，如今他知道，都在冬天来临时振着翅、蹑着脚走掉了。当鸟儿一群又一群归来，他听出那些旧日的回声重新苏醒，便惊讶地发现自己原来一直在惦记它们，此刻正满心欢喜地迎它们回来。所有的恐惧都忘光了。取而代之的，是一种压不住的热望：想知道它们是什么，想弄清它们去过哪里，想问它们肯不肯像冬天的鸟儿那样跟他交朋友；要是肯，它们会不会也那样薄情？因为随着树汁奔流、虫豸蠕动、飞虫振翅，弗雷克尔斯的「鸡儿们」多半都抛下他进了沼泽，把里面的出产吃了个肚圆，对他的那份口粮再瞧不上眼——于是在求偶营巢这些忙忙乱乱的日子里，少年反倒遭了冷落。

鸟儿们这般忘恩负义，他心里不是不恼，可他很快就在守望新来客、同它们结交之中寻得了安慰。倘若他知道，沼泽腹地的许多老住户如今纷纷把巢搬到林线旁边，图的正是他的保护和作伴，那他准会又得意又欢喜。

林伯洛斯特一年一度的复苏，是一场盛大的复兴。弗雷克尔斯站在一旁，怀着敬畏与歆羡，看沼泽渐渐重新披上衣裳、住满住客。历经危险与孤独磨出的锐目与警觉，使他把每一阶段的进展都收在眼里——从最先开嗓鸣叫的青蛙和最先破鞘而出的芽苞，直到满林绿叶成荫、最后一只候鸟归来。

自己那份彻底的孤单和彻头彻尾的微不足道，时时刻刻都在向他逼来。他愁肠百结、烦躁不安，简直要发起热来；可他怎么也猜不透病根。他心里涨满了一种巨大的焦躁，一种几乎承受不住的渴望。

===Original===
It was June by the zodiac, June by the Limberlost, and by every delight of a newly resurrected season it should have been June in the hearts of all men. Yet Freckles scowled darkly as he came down the trail, and the running *tap, tap* that tested the sagging wire and telegraphed word of his coming to his furred and feathered friends of the swamp, this morning carried the story of his discontent a mile ahead of him.

Freckles’ special pet, a dainty, yellow-coated, black-sleeved, cock goldfinch, had remained on the wire for several days past the bravest of all; and Freckles, absorbed with the cunning and beauty of the tiny fellow, never guessed that he was being duped. For the goldfinch was skipping, flirting, and swinging for the express purpose of so holding his attention that he would not look up and see a small cradle of thistledown and wool perilously near his head. In the beginning of brooding, the spunky little homesteader had clung heroically to the wire when he was almost paralyzed with fright. When day after day passed and brought only softly whistled repetitions of his call, a handful of crumbs on the top of a locust line-post, and gently worded coaxings, he grew in confidence. Of late he had sung and swung during the passing of Freckles, who, not dreaming of the nest and the solemn-eyed little hen so close above, thought himself unusually gifted in his power to attract the birds. This morning the goldfinch scarcely could believe his ears, and clung to the wire until an unusually vicious rap sent him spinning a foot in air, and his *ptseet* came with a squall of utter panic.

The wires were ringing with a story the birds could not translate, and Freckles was quite as ignorant of the trouble as they.

A peculiar movement beneath a small walnut tree caught his attention. He stopped to investigate. There was an unusually large Luna cocoon, and the moth was bursting the upper end in its struggles to reach light and air. Freckles stood and stared.

===Chinese===
按黄道算，是六月；按林伯洛斯特算，也是六月；照新生季节里种种的欢愉，世人的心坎上也本该个个都是六月才对。可弗雷克尔斯顺着小道走来时，脸色却阴沉沉的。那一串不停的*哒、哒*声敲在下垂的铁丝上，向沼泽里长毛带羽的朋友们拍发电报，通报他的到来——今天早晨，这声音却把他的烦闷抢先一英里传了出去。

弗雷克尔斯格外宠爱的一只宝贝，是一只玲珑俏丽的雄金翅雀：通身黄羽，翅膀上却像描了黑袖。这几天里，数它在铁丝线上停得最久、胆子最大；弗雷克尔斯一心贪看这小东西的机灵俏皮，却压根没想到自己一直被它蒙在鼓里。原来那金翅雀又蹦又摆又荡，正是要勾住他的眼光，好教他不抬头，瞧不见就在他头顶险处那个蓟绒和羊毛编成的小摇篮。刚开孵那阵子，这勇敢的小户主吓了个半死，还硬是扒在铁丝上，好不英雄。一天天过去，等来的只有轻声的口哨一遍遍学它叫唤，只有洋槐线柱顶上的一撮面包屑，只有温言软语的哄劝，它的胆气便渐渐壮了。到后来，弗雷克尔斯走过时它索性又唱又荡；弗雷克尔斯呢，梦里也想不到头顶近旁有巢，还有那位瞪着一双严肃眼睛的小母鸟，只道自己招引鸟儿的本事异乎寻常。今天早晨，金翅雀简直不敢相信自己的耳朵，死死扒住铁丝不放，直到一记格外凶狠的敲击把它打得在半空里翻了个跟头，那一声*啾哧*，带着一嗓子惊慌失措的尖叫。

铁丝线震响着一个鸟儿们翻译不出的故事，而这桩烦恼的根底，弗雷克尔斯同它们一样一无所知。

一棵小胡桃树底下有个奇怪的动静，引起了他的注意。他停下来察看。那是一只大得少见的月神蛾（Luna）的茧，茧里的蛾子正奋力顶破茧的上端，要够着光和空气。弗雷克尔斯站在那儿，直瞪瞪地望着。

===Original===
“There’s something in there trying to get out,” he muttered. “Wonder if I could help it? Guess I best not be trying. If I hadn’t happened along, there wouldn’t have been anyone to do anything, and maybe I’d only be hurting it. It’s﻿—it’s﻿—Oh, skaggany! It’s just being born!”

Freckles gasped with surprise. The moth cleared the opening, and with many wabblings and contortions climbed up the tree. He stared speechless with amazement as the moth crept around a limb and clung to the under side. There was a big pursy body, almost as large as his thumb, and of the very snowiest white that Freckles ever had seen. There was a band of delicate lavender across its forehead, and its feet were of the same colour; there were antlers, like tiny, straw-colored ferns, on its head, and from its shoulders hung the crumpled wet wings. As Freckles gazed, tense with astonishment, he saw that these were expanding, drooping, taking on color, and small, oval markings were beginning to show.

The minutes passed. Freckles’ steady gaze never wavered. Without realizing it, he was trembling with eagerness and anxiety. As he saw what was taking place, “It’s going to fly,” he breathed in hushed wonder. The morning sun fell on the moth and dried its velvet down, while the warm air made it fluffy. The rapidly growing wings began to show the most delicate green, with lavender fore-ribs, transparent, eye-shaped markings, edged with lines of red, tan, and black, and long, crisp trailers.

Freckles was whispering to himself for fear of disturbing the moth. It began a systematic exercise of raising and lowering its exquisite wings to dry them and to establish circulation. The boy realized that soon it would be able to spread them and sail away. His long-coming soul sent up its first shivering cry.

===Chinese===
「那里头有个东西想出来哪，」他喃喃道。「不知道俺能不能帮它一把？俺看还是别伸手的好。要是俺没恰好打这儿过，本来也不会有谁来管，俺瞎帮忙，说不定反倒害了它。它是——它是——哎呀，乖乖！它这是在出生哪！」

弗雷克尔斯惊得倒吸一口气。蛾子钻出茧口，摇摇晃晃、扭来扭去地往树上爬。它绕过一根枝子，扒在枝条底下，他看得目瞪口呆，说不出话来。好一个胖嘟嘟的大身子，差不多有他的拇指那么大，白得像雪——弗雷克尔斯生平没见过那样的雪白。额上一道娇嫩的淡紫，脚也是同一种颜色；头上一对羽角，活像两小株麦秆色的蕨草；肩头垂着一对湿漉漉、皱巴巴的翅膀。弗雷克尔斯屏息凝望，看得它们渐渐舒展、垂落、着上颜色，一些小小的椭圆斑纹开始显了出来。

一分一分局过去。弗雷克尔斯凝定的目光一瞬不移。不知不觉间，他已经急切牵挂得浑身发颤。看着眼前这一幕，他屏住气，轻声惊叹：「它要飞了。」早晨的太阳照在蛾子身上，晒干它天鹅绒似的绒毛，暖空气又把它吹得蓬松。长得飞快的翅膀开始透出最娇嫩的绿色，前缘的翅脉泛着淡紫，还有透明的眼形斑纹，镶着红、褐、黑几道细边，拖着两条长长的、脆生生的飘带。

弗雷克尔斯怕惊动了蛾子，声音低成了自言自语。蛾子开始有板有眼地操练起来，把那对精巧的翅膀一抬一落，好晾干它们，好让血脉通畅。少年明白，用不了多久它就能展开翅膀飞走了。他那姗姗来迟的灵魂，发出了第一声颤巍巍的呼喊。

===Original===
“I don’t know what it is! Oh, I wish I knew! How I wish I knew! It must be something grand! It can’t be a butterfly! It’s away too big. Oh, I wish there was someone to tell me what it is!”

He climbed on the locust post, and balancing himself with the wire, held a finger in the line of the moth’s advance up the twig. It unhesitatingly climbed on, so he stepped to the path, holding it to the light and examining it closely. Then he held it in the shade and turned it, gloating over its markings and beautiful coloring. When he held the moth to the limb, it climbed on, still waving those magnificent wings.

“My, but I’d like to be staying with you!” he said. “But if I was to stand here all day you couldn’t grow any prettier than you are right now, and I wouldn’t grow smart enough to tell what you are. I suppose there’s someone who knows. Of course there is! Mr. McLean said there were people who knew every leaf, bird, and flower in the Limberlost. Oh Lord! How I wish You’d be telling me just this one thing!”

The goldfinch had ventured back to the wire, for there was his mate, only a few inches above the man-creature’s head; and indeed, he simply must not be allowed to look up, so the brave little fellow rocked on the wire and piped, as he had done every day for a week: *See me? See me?*

===Chinese===
「俺不知道它是什么！唉，俺多想知道！俺多想知道啊！它准是个了不得的东西！它不会是蝴蝶！它个头太大啦。唉，要有个人能告诉俺它是什么，该多好！」

他爬上洋槐线柱，一只手攀着铁丝稳住身子，伸出一根手指，拦在蛾子沿细枝前行的去路上。它毫不迟疑地爬了上来，他便跨回小道，把它举到亮处细细端详，又挪到荫凉里翻来覆去地看，把它的斑纹和漂亮的颜色看了个够。等他把蛾子凑近树枝，它又爬了上去，照旧扇着那对华丽的翅膀。

「哎呀，俺真想陪着你待下去！」他说。「可俺要是在这儿站上一整天，你也不会比眼下再俊一分，俺也不会变聪明到说得出你是什么。俺想，总有人是知道的。当然有！麦克莱恩先生说过，有人认得林伯洛斯特的每一片叶、每一只鸟、每一朵花。主啊！您要是肯把单这一件事告诉俺，该有多好！」

金翅雀又大着胆子溜回了铁丝上，因为它的伴侣就在这个人儿头顶不过几寸的地方；说什么也不能容他抬起头来。于是这勇敢的小家伙在铁丝上摇啊摆啊，叫啊唱啊，跟这一个星期天天一个样：*看俺呀？看俺呀？*

===Original===
“See you! Of course I see you,” growled Freckles. “I see you day after day, and what good is it doing me? I might see you every morning for a year, and then not be able to be telling anyone about it. ‘Seen a bird with black silk wings﻿—little, and yellow as any canary.’ That’s as far as I’d get. What you doing here, anyway? Have you a mate? What’s your name? ‘See you?’ I reckon I see you; but I might as well be blind, for any good it’s doing me!”

Freckles impatiently struck the wire. With a screech of fear, the goldfinch fled precipitately. His mate arose from the nest with a whirr﻿—Freckles looked up and saw it.

“O﻿—ho!” he cried. “So *that’s* what you are doing here! You have a wife. And so close my head I have been mighty near wearing a bird on my bonnet, and never knew it!”

Freckles laughed at his own jest, while in better humor he climbed to examine the neat, tiny cradle and its contents. The hen darted at him in a frenzy. “Now, where do you come in?” he demanded, when he saw that she was not similar to the goldfinch.

===Chinese===
「看你！俺当然看得见你，」弗雷克尔斯闷声闷气地说。「俺一天又一天地看你，看了于俺有什么好处？俺就是每天早晨看你，看上一整年，到头来也没法跟人说个明白。『见过一只鸟，黑绸子翅膀——小小的，黄得赛过金丝雀。』俺能说出口的，也就这一句。你到底在这儿干什么？你有媳妇吗？你叫什么名字？『看你？』俺看是看见你了；可这于俺有半点益处？俺还不如个瞎子呢！」

弗雷克尔斯不耐烦地敲了一下铁丝。金翅雀惊叫一声，没命地飞逃。它的伴侣扑棱一声离巢而起——弗雷克尔斯一抬头，看见了。

「哦——嗬！」他喊道。「原来*你*在这儿干的是这个！你有媳妇啦。它就蹲在俺脑瓜顶上，俺差一点帽子上就戴了一只鸟，自己还蒙在鼓里呢！」

弗雷克尔斯被自己这句玩笑逗乐了，兴致好了些，便爬上去细看那个精巧的小摇篮和里面的东西。母鸟没命似的朝他直扑过来。等看清她跟金翅雀长得并不相像，他质问道：「你又是打哪儿冒出来的？」

===Original===
“You be clearing out of here! This is none of your fry. This is the nest of me little, yellow friend of the wire, and you shan’t be touching it. Don’t blame you for wanting to see, though. My, but it’s a fine nest and beauties of eggs. Will you be keeping away, or will I fire this stick at you?”

Freckles dropped to the trail. The hen darted to the nest and settled on it with a tender, coddling movement. He of the yellow coat flew to the edge to make sure that everything was right. It would have been plain to the veriest novice that they were partners in that cradle.

“Well, I’ll be switched!” muttered Freckles. “If that ain’t both their nest! And he’s yellow and she’s green, or she’s yellow and he’s green. Of course, I don’t know, and I haven’t any way to find out, but it’s plain as the nose on your face that they are both ready to be fighting for that nest, so, of course, they belong. Doesn’t that beat you? Say, that’s what’s been sticking me all of this week on that grass nest in the thorn tree down the line. One day a blue bird is setting, so I think it is hers. The next day a brown bird is on, and I chase it off because the nest is blue’s. Next day the brown bird is on again, and I let her be, because I think it must be hers. Next day, be golly, blue’s on, and off I send her because it’s brown’s; and now, I bet my hat, it’s both their nest and I’ve only been bothering them and making a big fool of mesilf. Pretty specimen I am, pretending to be a friend to the birds, and so blamed ignorant I don’t know which ones go in pairs, and blue and brown are a pair, of course, if yellow and green are﻿—and there’s the red birds! I never thought of them! He’s red and she’s gray﻿—and now I want to be knowing, are they all different? Why no! Of course, they ain’t! There’s the jays all blue, and the crows all black.”

===Chinese===
「你给我躲开！这里头没有你的崽。这是俺那铁丝上的小黄朋友的巢，不许你碰它。不过你想瞧瞧，俺也不怪你。哎呀，这巢可真不赖，蛋也真是一等一的漂亮。你是躲远点儿呢，还是等俺拿这根树枝子扔你？」

弗雷克尔斯落回小道。母鸟一扑就回了巢，带着一股温柔娇纵的劲儿伏了下去。黄衣裳的那位飞到巢边，查看一切都妥妥当当。再外行的人也看得分明：他们是这摇篮里同甘共营的一对儿。

「乖乖，真是稀奇了！」弗雷克尔斯嘟囔道。「那巢居然是他们俩的！他是黄的她就是绿的，要么她是黄的他就是绿的。俺自然说不上来，也没法子上哪儿弄清楚，可他们俩都豁得出去为那个巢打一架，这不是明摆着巢是他们一家的吗？你说怪不怪？对了，线那头刺树上那个草巢，就是这桩事把俺绊了一整个星期。今天一只蓝鸟伏窝，俺想巢是她的。第二天换了只棕鸟在上头，俺把她赶走了，因为巢是蓝鸟的。第三天棕鸟又来了，俺由她去了，心想这巢八成是她的。第四天，好家伙，蓝鸟又伏上了，俺又把她轰走，因为巢是棕鸟的；如今俺敢拿帽子打赌，那也是他俩的巢，俺净添乱，把自个儿弄成了个大傻瓜。瞧俺这份德行，还充鸟儿们的朋友，蠢得连哪两只配一对儿都不知道；黄的和绿的是一对儿，那蓝的和棕的自然也是一对儿——还有红鸟呢！俺压根儿把他们忘啦！他是红的她是灰的——俺这会儿倒想弄明白，鸟儿两口子都长得不一样吗？不对，不对！当然不！蓝松鸦不就个个蓝，乌鸦不就个个黑。」

===Original===
The tide of Freckles’ discontent welled until he almost choked with anger and chagrin. He plodded down the trail, scowling blackly and viciously spanging the wire. At the finches’ nest he left the line and peered into the thorn tree. There was no bird brooding. He pressed closer to take a peep at the snowy, spotless little eggs he had found so beautiful, when at the slight noise up raised four tiny baby heads with wide-open mouths, uttering hunger cries. Freckles stepped back. The brown bird alighted on the edge and closed one cavity with a wiggling green worm, while not two minutes later the blue filled another with a white. That settled it. The blue and brown were mates. Once again Freckles repeated his “How I wish I knew!”

Around the bridge spanning Sleepy Snake Creek the swale spread widely, the timber was scattering, and willows, rushes, marsh-grass, and splendid wild flowers grew abundantly. Here lazy, big, black water snakes, for which the creek was named, sunned on the bushes, wild ducks and grebe chattered, cranes and herons fished, and muskrats plowed the bank in queer, rolling furrows. It was always a place full of interest, so Freckles loved to linger on the bridge, watching the marsh and water people. He also transacted affairs of importance with the wild flowers and sweet marsh-grass. He enjoyed splashing through the shallow pools on either side of the bridge.

Then, too, where the creek entered the swamp was a place of unusual beauty. The water spread in darksome, mossy, green pools. Water-plants and lilies grew luxuriantly, throwing up large, rank, green leaves. Nowhere else in the Limberlost could be found frog-music to equal that of the mouth of the creek. The drumming and piping rolled in never-ending orchestral effect, while the full chorus rang to its accompaniment throughout the season.

===Chinese===
弗雷克尔斯心头的怨愤一阵阵往上涌，涌得他又气又臊，几乎噎住。他闷头沿小道走下去，黑着脸，恶狠狠地敲着铁丝。到了雀巢那里，他离开线路，往刺树里张望。没有鸟伏窝。他凑近些，想再看一眼那些雪白无瑕的小蛋——他从前觉得它们美极了——微微一响，四颗小小的脑袋猛地昂起，嘴张得溜圆，发出讨食的尖叫。弗雷克尔斯退了回来。棕鸟落在巢沿，用一条扭动的青虫填上一张嘴；不出两分钟，蓝鸟又用一条白虫填上了另一张。这下定局了。蓝的和棕的是两口子。弗雷克尔斯又一次念叨起他那句话：「俺多想知道啊！」

在横跨睡蛇溪的木桥四周，沼泽草甸铺展得开阔，林木渐见稀疏，柳树、灯心草、沼草和各种绚烂的野花长得正盛。懒洋洋的大黑水蛇——这条溪就因它们得了名——趴在灌木上晒太阳；野鸭和鸊鷉叽叽喳喳，鹤和鹭在捕鱼，麝鼠把岸边犁出一道道古里古怪、圆滚滚的垄。这地方从来有的是看头，所以弗雷克尔斯爱在桥上流连，看这些沼泽与水乡的居民。他还同野花和香甜的沼草办过几件要紧的大事。他也爱蹚过桥两侧的浅水洼，哗啦哗啦溅着水玩。

再说小溪注入沼泽的地方，又是一处出奇的美景。水面漫开一个个幽暗生苔的绿潭。水草和睡莲长得繁茂，擎起大片大片肥壮的绿叶。整个林伯洛斯特，再没有哪里的蛙乐比得上溪口这一处。鼓声与笛声滚成连绵不绝的管弦之效，齐声大合唱和着这伴奏，整整一季唱个不停。

===Original===
Freckles slowly followed the path leading from the bridge to the line. It was the one spot at which he might relax his vigilance. The boldest timber thief the swamp ever had known would not have attempted to enter it by the mouth of the creek, on account of the water and because there was no protection from surrounding trees. He was bending the rank grass with his cudgel, and thinking of the shade the denser swamp afforded, when he suddenly dodged sidewise; the cudgel whistled sharply through the air and Freckles sprang back.

From the clear sky above him, first level with his face, then skimming, dipping, tilting, whirling until it struck, quill down, in the path in front of him, came a glossy, iridescent, big black feather. As it touched the ground, Freckles snatched it up with almost a continuous movement facing the sky. There was not a tree of any size in a large open space. There was no wind to carry it. From the clear sky it had fallen, and Freckles, gazing eagerly into the arch of June blue with a few lazy clouds floating high in the sea of ether, had neither mind nor knowledge to dream of a bird hanging as if frozen there. He turned the big quill questioningly, and again his awed eyes swept the sky.

“A feather dropped from Heaven!” he breathed reverently. “Are the holy angels moulting? But no; if they were, it would be white. Maybe all the angels are not for being white. What if the angels of God are white and those of the devil are black? But a black one has no business up there. Maybe some poor black angel is so tired of being punished it’s for slipping to the gates, beating its wings trying to make the Master hear!”

Again and again Freckles searched the sky, but there was no answering gleam of golden gates, no form of sailing bird; then he went slowly on his way, turning the feather and wondering about it. It was a wing quill, eighteen inches in length, with a heavy spine, gray at the base, shading to jet black at the tip, and it caught the play of the sun’s rays in slanting gleams of green and bronze. Again Freckles’ “old man of the sea” sat sullen and heavy on his shoulders and weighted him down until his step lagged and his heart ached.

===Chinese===
弗雷克尔斯缓缓走上从桥头通到林线的那条小路。这是唯一一处他可以松一松警觉的地方。沼泽里有史以来最胆大的盗木贼，也不会想从溪口闯进来——一是水拦着，二是四围没有树木可以遮身。他正用木棒压着高草走，想着密沼深处那片荫凉，忽然朝旁边一闪；木棒在空中锐声一啸，弗雷克尔斯猛地跳了回去。

从他头顶晴朗的天上，落下来一根油光水滑、闪着虹彩的黑色大羽毛：起初跟他脸齐平，接着掠着、沉着、斜着、旋着，最后羽根朝下，戳在他面前的小路上。羽毛一沾地，弗雷克尔斯几乎不换气地一把抓起，仰面朝天。一大片开阔地里，没有一棵像样的树。也没有风把它吹送。它是从晴空里落下来的；弗雷克尔斯急切地仰望六月的蓝色穹苍——高高的以太之海里漂着几朵懒云——他心里想不到，见识也不够，梦不到那上头会悬着一只仿佛冻僵了的鸟。他把大翎羽将信将疑地翻来转去，敬畏的目光又一次扫过天空。

「一根从天上掉下来的羽毛！」他虔敬地低声说。「是圣洁的天使们在换羽吗？不对；要是他们换羽，羽该是白的。兴许天使们不全是白的。万一上帝的天使是白的，魔鬼的天使是黑的呢？可黑天使不该待在那上头呀。兴许哪个可怜的黑天使，挨罚挨得实在受不住了，正偷偷溜到天门边上，扑打翅膀，想叫主听见呢！」

弗雷克尔斯一遍又一遍地搜索天空，可既没有金门闪出回光，也没有飞鸟的身影；他这才慢慢上路，一面捻着羽毛，一面纳闷。那是一根翅膀上的大翎羽，长十八英寸，羽轴粗壮，根部灰色，渐到羽尖化作乌黑，迎着日光，泛出一道道斜斜的绿与青铜的光泽。弗雷克尔斯那位「海上老人」又阴沉沉、重甸甸地骑上了他的肩头，压得他脚步拖沓，心口发疼。

===Original===
“Where did it come from? What is it? Oh, how I wish I knew!” he kept repeating as he turned and studied the feather, with almost unseeing eyes, so intently was he thinking.

Before him spread a large, green pool, filled with rotting logs and leaves, bordered with delicate ferns and grasses among which lifted the creamy spikes of the arrowhead, the blue of water-hyacinth, and the delicate yellow of the jewel-flower. As Freckles leaned, handling the feather and staring at it, then into the depths of the pool, he once more gave voice to his old query: “I wonder what it is!”

Straight across from him, couched in the mosses of a soggy old log, a big green bullfrog, with palpitant throat and batting eyes, lifted his head and bellowed in answer. *Fin’ dout! Fin’ dout!*

“Wha﻿—what’s that?” stammered Freckles, almost too much bewildered to speak. “I﻿—I know you are only a bullfrog, but, be jabbers, that sounded mightily like speech. Wouldn’t you please to be saying it over?”

===Chinese===
「它打哪儿来？它是什么？唉，俺多想知道啊！」他一遍遍念叨着，一面翻来覆去地端详那根羽毛；心思太专注了，一双眼睛几乎什么都没看见。

眼前铺开一泓宽宽的绿潭，潭里横满朽木烂叶，四缘生着纤细的蕨与草，其间擎起慈姑乳白的花穗、凤眼莲的蓝、宝石草的娇黄。弗雷克尔斯俯身捻着羽毛，望望它，又望进潭水深处，嘴里又一次把那句老疑问说了出口：「它到底是什么呢？」

正对着他，蹲踞在一段湿透的老木头苔藓里，一只碧绿的大牛蛙鼓着一颤一颤的咽喉、眨巴着眼睛，昂起头来吼着作答：*弄明白！弄明白！*

「什——什么？」弗雷克尔斯结结巴巴，慌得几乎话都说不成。「俺——俺知道你不过是只牛蛙，可是，老天爷在上，这话听着可太像人话了。劳你的驾，再说一遍成不成？」

===Original===
The bullfrog cuddled contentedly in the ooze. Then suddenly he lifted his voice, and, as an imperative drumbeat, rolled it again: *Fin’ dout! Fin’ dout! Fin dout!*

Freckles had the answer. Something seemed to snap in his brain. There was a wavering flame before his eyes. Then his mind cleared. His head lifted in a new poise, his shoulders squared, while his spine straightened. The agony was over. His soul floated free. Freckles came into his birthright.

“Before God, I will!” He uttered the oath so impressively that the recording angel never winced as he posted it in the prayer column.

Freckles set his hat over the top of one of the locust posts used between trees to hold up the wire while he fastened the feather securely in the band. Then he started down the line, talking to himself as men who have worked long alone always fall into the habit of doing.

===Chinese===
牛蛙心满意足地偎在泥浆里。忽然他放开嗓门，像一通不容分说的鼓点，又把那话滚了出来：*弄明白！弄明白！弄明白！*

弗雷克尔斯得着答案了。脑子里仿佛有什么东西啪的一声接上了。眼前晃过一道摇曳的火光。然后他的心头豁然清明。他的头以一种崭新的姿态昂起，双肩展平，脊背挺直。苦楚到头了。他的灵魂自由地飘了起来。弗雷克尔斯承继了他与生俱来的权利。

「在上帝面前，俺一定办到！」他这一句誓言说得那样庄重，连记录天使把它记进祷告栏里的时候，眼皮都不曾眨一下。

弗雷克尔斯把帽子扣上那根洋槐线柱的柱顶——两树之间撑铁丝用的那种柱子——一面把羽毛牢牢插进帽箍。随后他顺着线路走去，自言自语起来；长年独自做工的人，总会落下这个习惯。

===Original===
“What a fool I have been!” he muttered. “Of course that’s what I have to do! There wouldn’t likely anybody be doing it for me. Of course I can! What am I a man for? If I was a four-footed thing of the swamp, maybe I couldn’t; but a man can do anything if he’s the grit to work hard enough and stick at it, Mr. McLean is always saying, and here’s the way I am to do it. He said, too, that there were people that knew everything in the swamp. Of course they have written books! The thing for me to be doing is to quit moping and be buying some. Never bought a book in me life, or anything else of much account, for that matter. Oh, ain’t I glad I didn’t waste me money! I’ll surely be having enough to get a few. Let me see.”

Freckles sat on a log, took his pencil and account-book, and figured on a back page. He had walked the timberline ten months. His pay was thirty dollars a month, and his board cost him eight. That left twenty-two dollars a month, and his clothing had cost him very little. At the least he had two hundred dollars in the bank. He drew a deep breath and smiled at the sky with satisfaction.

“I’ll be having a book about all the birds, trees, flowers, butterflies, and﻿—Yes, by gummy! I’ll be having one about the frogs﻿—if it takes every cent I have,” he promised himself.

He put away the account-book, that was his most cherished possession, caught up his stick, and started down the line. The even *tap, tap*, and the cheery, gladsome whistle carried far ahead of him the message that Freckles was himself again.

===Chinese===
「俺一直是个大傻瓜！」他嘟囔道。「俺该做的，自然就是这个！这事儿多半不会有谁替俺来办。俺当然办得到！不然俺生做一场人干什么？俺若是沼泽里四条腿的畜生，兴许办不成；可麦克莱恩先生常说，一个人只要有狠劲苦干、咬住不放，就没有办不成的事——俺该怎么办，道道儿也就在这儿了。他还说过，有人认得沼泽里的一切。他们当然写过书！俺该做的，就是别再愁眉苦脸，去买上几本。俺这辈子还没买过一本书，旁的像样东西也没置过什么。哎呀，亏得俺没把那点钱胡花了！买几本，钱准是够的。让俺算算。」

弗雷克尔斯在一段木头上坐下，掏出铅笔和账本，在一页背面算起来。他巡林线已经十个月。工钱每月 30 美元，伙食每月花去 8 美元，一个月净剩 22 美元，衣裳上又几乎没花什么。他在银行里少说存着 200 美元。他深深吸一口气，心满意足地望着天空笑了。

「俺要有一本书，讲尽所有的鸟儿、树木、花儿、蝴蝶，还有——对了，乖乖！俺还要一本讲青蛙的——哪怕花光俺最后一个子儿，」他对自己许诺道。

他收起那本他最心爱的账本，抓起木棒，顺着线路出发了。均匀的*哒、哒*声，配上欢欢喜喜的口哨，远远送到他前头，捎去一句话：弗雷克尔斯又变回他自己了。

===Original===
He fell into a rapid pace, for he had lost time that morning; when he rounded the last curve he was almost running. There was a chance that the Boss might be there for his weekly report.

Then, wavering, flickering, darting here and there over the sweet marsh-grass, came a large black shadow, sweeping so closely before him that for the second time that morning Freckles dodged and sprang back. He had seen some owls and hawks of the swamp that he thought might be classed as large birds, but never anything like this, for six feet it spread its big, shining wings. Its strong feet could be seen drawn among its feathers. The sun glinted on its sharp, hooked beak. Its eyes glowed, caught the light, and seemed able to pierce the ground at his feet. It cared no more for Freckles than if he had not been there; for it perched on a low tree, while a second later it awkwardly hopped to the trunk of a lightning-riven elm, turned its back, and began searching the blue.

Freckles looked just in time to see a second shadow sweep the grass; and another bird, a trifle smaller and not quite so brilliant in the light, slowly sailed down to perch beside the first.
