“I don’t understand,” said Freckles. “Were you expecting me?”

===Chinese===
他一直等到确信邓肯要是当夜回来、这会儿准已到家了，才去吃晚饭。他穿过沼泽草甸时，头一眼看见的就是院子里的那两匹高大的栗色马。

那一天没有人打这里路过，邓肯一口答应替他守着，好让弗雷克尔斯骑车进城。他把脚印的事告诉了邓肯，叮嘱他务必严防。邓肯说他尽管放心，随后装满烟斗，提上一支好左轮手枪，这条大汉便往林伯洛斯特去了。

弗雷克尔斯把自己收拾得干干净净、清清爽爽，一路飞骑进了城；可等他到鸟太太家时，已经是夜晚，星星都出来了。远远地他就望见那宅子里灯火通明。草坪上、游廊上挂着一串串彩灯，满是攒动的人影。他想着自己这趟差事要紧，压根儿没想到回头。他有的工夫和机会也就这一回了。他必须见到鸟太太，而且必须立刻见到。他把自行车靠进栅栏里边，顺着宽阔的正门甬道走上去。快到台阶时，他看见满院子都是年轻人，天使跟围着她的一群人告了个便，匆匆朝他走来。

「哎呀，弗雷克尔斯！」她喜出望外地喊道。「你到底能来呀？我们还怕你来不了呢！我高兴得不能再高兴了！」

「俺不明白，」弗雷克尔斯说。「你们是在等俺吗？」

===Original===
“Why of course!” exclaimed the Angel. “Haven’t you come to my party? Didn’t you get my invitation? I sent you one.”

“By mail?” asked Freckles.

“Yes,” said the Angel. “I had to help with the preparations, and I couldn’t find time to drive out; but I wrote you a letter, and told you that the Bird Woman was giving a party for me, and we wanted you to come, surely. I told them at the office to put it with Mr. Duncan’s mail.”

“Then that’s likely where it is at present,” said Freckles. “Duncan comes to town only once a week, and at times not that. He’s home tonight for the first in a week. He’s watching an hour for me until I come to the Bird Woman with a bit of work I thought she’d be caring to hear about bad. Is she where I can see her?”

The Angel’s face clouded.

===Chinese===
「可不是嘛！」天使叫道。「你这不是来参加我的宴会了么？没收到我的请帖吗？我给你寄了一张来着。」

「邮寄的？」弗雷克尔斯问。

「是啊，」天使说。「我得帮着操办，抽不出工夫赶出去；可我给你写了一封信，告诉你鸟太太要为我办一场宴会，我们真心盼着你来。我嘱咐办事处的人，把它跟邓肯先生的邮件放在一处。」

「那信这会儿多半还在那儿呢，」弗雷克尔斯说。「邓肯一星期只进城一回，有时候连一回也捞不着。今晚是他一个星期来头一次到家。他替俺看一个钟头，好让俺来找鸟太太，俺带来一件小事，俺寻思她会非常非常想听。她这会儿能见俺么？」

天使的脸笼上了一层阴云。

===Original===
“What a disappointment!” she cried. “I did so want all my friends to know you. Can’t you stay anyway?”

Freckles glanced from his wading-boots to the patent leathers of some of the Angel’s friends, and smiled whimsically, but there was no danger of his ever misjudging her again.

“You know I cannot, Angel,” he said.

“I am afraid I do,” she said ruefully. “It’s too bad! But there is a thing I want for you more than to come to my party, and that is to hang on and win with your work. I think of you every day, and I just pray that those thieves are not getting ahead of you. Oh, Freckles, do watch closely!”

She was so lovely a picture as she stood before him, ardent in his cause, that Freckles could not take his eyes from her to notice what her friends were thinking. If she did not mind, why should he? Anyway, if they really were the Angel’s friends, probably they were better accustomed to her ways than he.

===Chinese===
「多叫人失望啊！」她喊道。「我多想让我的朋友个个都认识你呀。你就不能留下来吗？」

弗雷克尔斯瞅了瞅自己的涉水靴，又瞅了瞅天使几位朋友的漆皮鞋，古怪地一笑；不过他是再也不会错怪她了。

「你知道俺不能留，天使。」他说。

「怕是真不能。」她怅然地说。「太可惜了！可有一样，比起你来赴我的宴会，我更盼着你能咬住牙，在你的差事上赢到底。我天天想着你，一心祷告那些贼可别抢了你的先。噢，弗雷克尔斯，千万要盯紧了！」

她站在他面前，一心向着他，那模样真像一幅可爱的画，弗雷克尔斯望着她，再顾不上留意她的朋友们在怎么想。她自己都不在意，他又何必在意呢？再说，他们果真是天使的朋友，只怕也早比他更惯于她这副脾气了。

===Original===
Her face and bared neck and arms were like the wild rose bloom. Her soft frock of white tulle lifted and stirred around her with the gentle evening air. The beautiful golden hair, that crept around her temples and ears as if it loved to cling there, was caught back and bound with broad blue satin ribbon. There was a sash of blue at her waist, and knots of it catching up her draperies.

“Must I go after the Bird Woman?” she pleaded.

“Indade, you must,” answered Freckles firmly.

The Angel went away, but returned to say that the Bird Woman was telling a story to those inside and she could not come for a short time.

“You won’t come in?” she pleaded.

===Chinese===
她的脸庞和裸露的颈项、手臂，就像盛开的野玫瑰。她那件白色薄纱软裙，随着傍晚温柔的气息在她周身轻轻掀动。她那头金发美极了，依依贴着她的鬓角和耳朵，仿佛舍不得离开那儿，此刻拢向脑后，用一条宽宽的蓝缎带束住。腰间系一条蓝腰带，裙裾上也缀着一簇簇的蓝蝴蝶结。

「非得我去把鸟太太请出来么？」她恳求道。

「说真的，非去不可。」弗雷克尔斯斩钉截铁地答道。

天使去了，回来说鸟太太正在给屋里的人讲故事，一时半会儿还脱不开身。

「你不进来坐坐？」她又恳求道。

===Original===
“I must not,” said Freckles. “I am not dressed to be among your friends, and I might be forgetting meself and stay too long.”

“Then,” said the Angel, “we mustn’t go through the house, because it would disturb the story; but I want you to come the outside way to the conservatory and have some of my birthday lunch and some cake to take to Mrs. Duncan and the babies. Won’t that be fun?”

Freckles thought that it would be more than fun, and followed delightedly.

The Angel gave him a big glass, brimming with some icy, sparkling liquid that struck his palate as it never had been touched before, because a combination of frosty fruit juices had not been a frequent beverage with him. The night was warm, and the Angel most beautiful and kind. A triple delirium of spirit, mind, and body seized upon him and developed a boldness all unnatural. He slightly parted the heavy curtains that separated the conservatory from the company and looked between. He almost stopped breathing. He had read of things like that, but he never had seen them.

The open space seemed to stretch through half a dozen rooms, all ablaze with lights, perfumed with flowers, and filled with elegantly dressed people. There were glimpses of polished floors, sparkling glass, and fine furnishings. From somewhere, the voice of his beloved Bird Woman arose and fell.

===Chinese===
「俺不能进，」弗雷克尔斯说。「俺这一身穿戴，不好站到你的朋友中间去，再说俺怕一高兴忘了形，待得过了时候。」

「那么，」天使说，「咱们可不能穿堂过屋，会搅了人家听故事；你从外头绕到花房去吧，尝尝我生日午点的味道，再带些点心给邓肯太太和娃娃们。这不有趣吗？」

弗雷克尔斯觉得这岂止是有趣，便欢欢喜喜地跟她去了。

天使递给他一大杯冰凉起泡的饮品，那滋味落到他舌头上，是从未有过的受用，因为掺和着冰霜果味的汁水，从来不是他常喝的东西。夜气温暖，天使又是那样美丽亲切。一种精神、心灵和血肉的三重迷醉攫住了他，生出一种全然不像他自己的胆气。他微微撩开隔开花房与宾客的厚帷幔，朝里面张望。他几乎屏住了呼吸。这样的场面他在书上读到过，可从来没有亲眼见过。

那开阔的纵深仿佛一连穿过六七间屋子，全都灯火辉煌，花香四溢，满是衣着华贵的人。光亮的地板、晶亮的玻璃器皿、精美的陈设，一处接一处映入眼帘。不知从哪里，传来他敬爱的鸟太太那抑扬起伏的嗓音。

===Original===
The Angel crowded beside him and was watching also.

“Doesn’t it look pretty?” she whispered.

“Do you suppose Heaven is any finer than that?” asked Freckles.

The Angel began to laugh.

“Do you want to be laughing harder than that?” queried Freckles.

===Chinese===
天使也挤到他身旁，一起张望着。

「好看得很吧？」她悄声说。

「你说天堂还能比这更漂亮么？」弗雷克尔斯问。

天使笑了起来。

「想不想笑得更痛快些？」弗雷克尔斯追问。

===Original===
“A laugh is always good,” said the Angel. “A little more avoirdupois won’t hurt me. Go ahead.”

“Well then,” said Freckles, “it’s only that I feel all over as if I belonged there. I could wear fine clothes, and move over those floors, and hold me own against the best of them.”

“But where does my laugh come in?” demanded the Angel, as if she had been defrauded.

“And you ask me where the laugh comes in, looking me in the face after that,” marveled Freckles.

“I wouldn’t be so foolish as to laugh at such a manifest truth as that,” said the Angel. “Anyone who knows you even half as well as I do, knows that you are never guilty of a discourtesy, and you move with twice the grace of any man here. Why shouldn’t you feel as if you belonged where people are graceful and courteous?”

===Chinese===
「笑总是有益的，」天使说。「多添一两肉也坏不了我的事。你只管说。」

「那俺就说了，」弗雷克尔斯说，「俺只觉得浑身上下，就好像俺天生是那里面的人。俺也穿得起华服，也走得过那些地板，跟他们里头顶体面的比，俺也一点不输。」

「可我的笑从哪儿来呢？」天使追问，好像自己受了骗似的。

「你说了这话，还望着俺的脸问俺笑从哪儿来。」弗雷克尔斯啧啧称奇。

「这么明明白白的实话，我才不会傻到去笑话它呢。」天使说。「凡是像我这样、哪怕只及我一半了解你的人，都知道你从不曾有过半点失礼，你的仪态比这里随便哪一位男士都优雅一倍。人们又文雅又有礼的地方，你觉得自己本该在那里，这有什么不应该呢？」

===Original===
“On me soul!” said Freckles, “you are kind to be thinking it. You are doubly kind to be saying it.”

The curtains parted and a woman came toward them. Her silks and laces trailed across the polished floors. The lights gleamed on her neck and arms, and flashed from rare jewels. She was smiling brightly; and until she spoke, Freckles had not realized fully that it was his loved Bird Woman.

Noticing his bewilderment, she cried: “Why, Freckles! Don’t you know me in my war clothes?”

“I do in the uniform in which you fight the Limberlost,” said Freckles.

The Bird Woman laughed. Then he told her why he had come, but she scarcely could believe him. She could not say exactly when she would go, but she would make it as soon as possible, for she was most anxious for the study.

===Chinese===
「凭俺的良心！」弗雷克尔斯说。「你这样想，是心善；肯这样说，是加倍的善心。」

帷幔开了，一位妇人朝他们走来。她的绸衣和花边拖曳过光亮的地板。灯光照在她的颈项和手臂上，又从名贵的珠宝上闪出光来。她笑吟吟的；直到她开口，弗雷克尔斯才完全认出来，这就是他敬爱的鸟太太。

见他一脸怔忡，她扬声笑道：「哎呀，弗雷克尔斯！我穿上战袍，你就不认得我啦？」

「您穿着跟林伯洛斯特作战的那身行头时，俺认得。」弗雷克尔斯说。

鸟太太笑了。于是他说明来意，可她几乎不敢相信他的话。她说不准哪天能去，但一定尽早赶来，因为她太急于研究这个题材了。

===Original===
While they talked, the Angel was busy packing a box of sandwiches, cake, fruit, and flowers. She gave him a last frosty glass, thanked him repeatedly for bringing news of new material; then Freckles went into the night. He rode toward the Limberlost with his eyes on the stars. Presently he removed his hat, hung it to his belt, and ruffled his hair to the sweep of the night wind. He filled the air all the way with snatches of oratorios, gospel hymns, and dialect and coon songs, in a startlingly varied programme. The one thing Freckles knew that he could do was to sing. The Duncans heard him coming a mile up the corduroy and could not believe their senses. Freckles unfastened the box from his belt, and gave Mrs. Duncan and the children all the eatables it contained, except one big piece of cake that he carried to the sweet-loving Duncan. He put the flowers back in the box and set it among his books. He did not say anything, but they understood it was not to be touched.

“Thae’s Freckles’ flow’rs,” said a tiny Scotsman, “but,” he added cheerfully, “it’s oor sweeties!”

Freckles’ face slowly flushed as he took Duncan’s cake and started toward the swamp. While Duncan ate, Freckles told him something about the evening, as well as he could find words to express himself, and the big man was so amazed he kept forgetting the treat in his hands.

Then Freckles mounted his wheel and began a spin that terminated only when the biggest Plymouth Rock in Duncan’s coop saluted a new day, and long lines of light reddened the east. As he rode he sang, while he sang he worshiped, but the god he tried to glorify was a dim and faraway mystery. The Angel was warm flesh and blood.

Every time he passed the little bark-covered imprint on the trail he dismounted, removed his hat, solemnly knelt and laid his lips on the impression. Because he kept no account himself, only the laughing-faced old man of the moon knew how often it happened; and as from the beginning, to the follies of earth that gentleman has ever been kind.

With the near approach of dawn Freckles tuned his last note. Wearied almost to falling, he turned from the trail into the path leading to the cabin for a few hours’ rest.

===Chinese===
他们说话的当儿，天使正忙着装一盒子三明治、糕点、水果和鲜花。她又递给他最后一杯结着白霜的冷饮，再三谢他带来新题材的消息；随后弗雷克尔斯便走进了夜色。他朝林伯洛斯特骑去，眼睛望着星星。过了一会儿他摘下帽子，挂在腰带上，任夜风扫过他蓬乱的头发。他一路唱个不停，清唱剧、福音圣歌、方言小调、黑人歌谣（coon song），东一段西一段，那份节目单五花八门得吓人。弗雷克尔斯有一样自己知道拿得起来的本事，就是唱歌。邓肯一家在一英里外的圆木路上就听见他来了，简直不敢相信自己的耳朵。弗雷克尔斯解开腰带上的盒子，把里头所有吃食都给了邓肯太太和孩子们，只留下一大块蛋糕，带给爱吃甜食的邓肯。他把花放回盒里，摆在自己的书中间。他什么也没说，可他们都明白，那是不许碰的。

「那是弗雷克尔斯的花儿，」一个小不点儿的苏格兰人说道，「不过，」他快活地补了一句，「糖果可是咱们大家的！」

弗雷克尔斯接过邓肯那份蛋糕，朝沼泽走去，脸慢慢地红了。邓肯吃着，弗雷克尔斯搜肠刮肚，尽量把晚间的事讲给他听，这条大汉听得目瞪口呆，连连忘了吃手里那份好点心。

随后弗雷克尔斯跨上自行车，开始了一趟疯骑，一直骑到邓肯家鸡笼里那只顶大的普利茅斯洛克鸡（Plymouth Rock）向新的一天啼唱致敬，骑到一道道长长的曙光染红了东方，才算罢休。他一路骑，一路唱；一路唱，一路敬拜；只是他要颂扬的那位神，还只是个影影绰绰、遥不可及的神秘。天使却是温热的血肉之身。

每回经过巡林小道上那处盖着树皮的小小印痕，他都要下车，脱帽，郑重其事地跪下，把嘴唇印在那印记上。他自己从不记账，只有月亮上那位笑呵呵的老人知道这事有多少回；而且，打从太初起，对世间的痴愚，这位老先生向来是宽厚的。

天将破晓，弗雷克尔斯收了最后一个音符。他几乎累得要散架，这才离开巡林小道，拐上通往小屋的路，去歇上几个钟头。

## XII Wherein Black Jack captures Freckles and the Angel captures Jack. / ## XII 本章黑杰克擒住弗雷克尔斯，天使擒住了杰克。

===Original===
As Freckles left the trail, from the swale close the south entrance, four large muscular men arose and swiftly and carefully entered the swamp by the wagon road. Two of them carried a big saw, the third, coils of rope and wire, and all of them were heavily armed. They left one man on guard at the entrance. The other three made their way through the darkness as best they could, and were soon at Freckles’ room. He had left the swamp on his wheel from the west trail. They counted on his returning on the wheel and circling the east line before he came there.

A little below the west entrance to Freckles’ room, Black Jack stepped into the swale, and binding a wire tightly around a scrub oak, carried it below the waving grasses, stretched it taut across the trail, and fastened it to a tree in the swamp. Then he obliterated all signs of his work, and arranged the grass over the wire until it was so completely covered that only minute examination would reveal it. They entered Freckles’ room with coarse oaths and jests. In a few moments, his specimen case with its precious contents was rolled into the swamp, while the saw was eating into one of the finest trees of the Limberlost.

The first report from the man on watch was that Duncan had driven to the South camp; the second, that Freckles was coming. The man watching was sent to see on which side the boy turned into the path; as they had expected, he took the east. He was a little tired and his head was rather stupid, for he had not been able to sleep as he had hoped, but he was very happy. Although he watched until his eyes ached, he could see no sign of anyone having entered the swamp.

He called a cheery greeting to all his chickens. At Sleepy Snake Creek he almost fell from his wheel with surprise: the sawbird was surrounded by four lanky youngsters clamoring for breakfast. The father was strutting with all the importance of a drum major.

“No use to expect the Bird Woman today,” said Freckles; “but now wouldn’t she be jumping for a chance at that?”

As soon as Freckles was far down the east line, the watch was posted below the room on the west to report his coming. It was only a few moments before the signal came. Then the saw stopped, and the rope was brought out and uncoiled close to a sapling. Wessner and Black Jack crowded to the very edge of the swamp a little above the wire, and crouched, waiting.

===Chinese===
弗雷克尔斯刚离开巡林小道，南入口近旁的沼泽草甸里就站起来四条魁梧的大汉，迅捷而小心地顺着马车道进了沼泽。他们两个扛着一把大锯，第三个提着几卷绳子和铁丝，一个个都带了重家伙。他们留一个人把守入口，其余三个摸着黑尽量悄悄赶路，不多时便到了弗雷克尔斯的住处。他是从西边的小道骑自行车离开沼泽的，他们料定他回来时还会骑着车，先绕东线巡视，然后才到那儿。

在弗雷克尔斯住处西入口下方一点的地方，黑杰克跨进沼泽草甸，把一根铁丝紧紧缠上一棵矮橡树（scrub oak），让铁丝从起伏的草浪底下穿过小道，绷得紧紧的，拴在沼泽里的一棵树上。然后他把自己动手的痕迹抹得干干净净，又把草拨拢来盖在铁丝上，盖得严严实实，不细细查看根本发现不了。他们骂骂咧咧、说着浑话闯进了弗雷克尔斯的住处。不消片刻，他那只装着珍贵收藏的标本箱就被滚进了沼泽，与此同时，那把大锯已经咬进林伯洛斯特数一数二的好树。

放哨的先报来第一个信儿：邓肯赶车到南营地去了；第二个信儿：弗雷克尔斯回来了。他们又差放哨的去看那孩子从哪一边拐上小道；果然不出所料，他走的是东边。他有点儿累，脑子也昏昏沉沉的，因为他没能像指望的那样睡上一觉，可他心里快活极了。他一路望啊望，望得眼睛都疼了，也没瞧出半点有人进过沼泽的迹象。

他一路向所有的鸡们快活地打着招呼。到了睡蛇溪，他简直惊得要从车上跌下来：锯鸟身边围了四只瘦长腿的雏鸟，正嚷着讨早饭吃。鸟爸爸挺胸凸肚地踱步，那副了不得的神气劲儿，活像个鼓乐队队长。

「今天是指望不上鸟太太了，」弗雷克尔斯说，「可她要是能碰上这么个机会，还不得乐得蹦起来！」

弗雷克尔斯刚沿东线走得远了，放哨的就挪到住处西面下方，专等报告他回来的动静。没过几分钟信号就来了。锯随即停下，绳子搬了出来，在一棵小树旁解开摊好。韦斯纳和黑杰克挤到铁丝上方一点的沼泽边上，蹲下来守着。

===Original===
They heard Freckles before they saw him. He came gliding down the line swiftly, and as he rode he was singing softly:

> “Oh, do you love, Oh, say you love﻿—”

He got no farther. The sharply driven wheel struck the tense wire and bounded back. Freckles shot over the handlebar and coasted down the trail on his chest. As he struck, Black Jack and Wessner were upon him. Wessner caught off an old felt hat and clapped it over Freckles’ mouth, while Black Jack twisted the boy’s arms behind him and they rushed him into his room. Almost before he realized that anything had happened, he was trussed to a tree and securely gagged.

Then three of the men resumed work on the tree. The other followed the path Freckles had worn to Little Chicken’s tree, and presently he reported that the wires were down and two teams with the loading apparatus coming to take out the timber. All the time the saw was slowly eating, eating into the big tree.

Wessner went to the trail and removed the wire. He picked up Freckles’ wheel, that did not seem to be injured, and leaned it against the bushes so that if anyone did pass on the trail he would not see it doubled in the swamp-grass.

===Chinese===
他们还没看见弗雷克尔斯，先听见了他。他顺着铁丝网线飞快地滑下来，一边骑一边轻轻唱着：

> 「啊，你可真心爱？啊，说你爱——」

他没能再唱下去。疾驰的车轮撞上紧绷的铁丝，猛地弹了回来。弗雷克尔斯从车把上一头射了出去，胸口贴着小道滑了下去。他刚一落地，黑杰克和韦斯纳就扑到了他身上。韦斯纳抓下自己一顶旧毡帽，扣住弗雷克尔斯的嘴，黑杰克把他的胳膊反拧到背后，两人把他拖进他的住处。他还没弄明白出了什么事，就已经被捆在树上，嘴也堵得严严实实。

随后三个汉子接着回去锯树。另一个顺着弗雷克尔斯踩出来的、通往小鸡那棵树的小路走去，不多时就回来报告：铁丝网已经放倒，两套马车连同装车的家伙什正开进来运木料。那把锯一直在慢慢地咬啊、咬啊，咬进大树。

韦斯纳走到小道上，把铁丝撤了。他捡起弗雷克尔斯那辆看上去没摔坏的自行车，靠到灌木丛上，免得真有人打小道经过时，看见它翻倒在沼泽草里惹眼。

===Original===
Then he came and stood in front of Freckles and laughed in devilish hate. To his own amazement, Freckles found himself looking fear in the face, and marveled that he was not afraid. Four to one! The tree halfway eaten through, the wagons coming up the inside road﻿—he, bound and gagged! The men with Black Jack and Wessner had belonged to McLean’s gang when last he had heard of them, but who those coming with the wagons might be he could not guess.

If they secured that tree, McLean lost its value, lost his wager, and lost his faith in him. The words of the Angel hammered in his ears. “Oh, Freckles, do watch closely!”

The saw worked steadily.

When the tree was down and loaded, what would they do? Pull out, and leave him there to report them? It was not to be hoped for. The place always had been lawless. It could mean but one thing.

A mist swept before his eyes, while his head swam. Was it only last night that he had worshiped the Angel in a delirium of happiness? And now, what? Wessner, released from a turn at the saw, walked to the flower bed, and tearing up a handful of rare ferns by the roots, started toward Freckles. His intention was obvious. Black Jack stopped him, with an oath.

“You see here, Dutchy,” he bawled, “mebby you think you’ll wash his face with that, but you won’t. A contract’s a contract. We agreed to take out these trees and leave him for you to dispose of whatever way you please, provided you shut him up eternally on this deal. But I’ll not see a tied man tormented by a fellow that he can lick up the ground with, loose, and that’s flat. It raises my gorge to think what he’ll get when we’re gone, but you needn’t think you’re free to begin before. Don’t you lay a hand on him while I’m here! What do you say, boys?”

===Chinese===
然后他走过来，站在弗雷克尔斯面前，怀着魔鬼似的仇恨大笑。让弗雷克尔斯自己都吃惊的是，他发现自己正面对面直视着恐惧，却诧异自己竟不怕。四个对一个！树已经锯进去一半，马车正顺着里面的路赶上来——而他，被捆着、堵着嘴！跟黑杰克和韦斯纳来的那几个，据他最后听说的消息，本来都是麦克莱恩的人马，可跟着马车来的又是些什么人，他猜不出来。

他们要是把那棵树弄到手，麦克莱恩就损失了它的价值，输了那场赌，也失了对他的信任。天使的话在他耳边锤打：「哦，弗雷克尔斯，千万看紧了！」

锯子不紧不慢地干着活。

等树倒了、装上车，他们会拿他怎么办？撤走，把他留在那儿去告发他们？那是指望不上的。这地方向来无法无天。这只可能意味着一件事。

一阵迷雾掠过他的眼前，脑袋发晕。就在昨天夜里，他不是还在幸福的狂喜里敬拜着天使吗？而现在呢？韦斯纳锯了一轮下来歇手，走到花圃边，连根拔起一大把珍稀的蕨草，朝弗雷克尔斯走来。他的用意再明白不过。黑杰克一声咒骂，拦住了他。

「你给我听着，荷兰佬，」他吼道，「你八成想着拿那个给他洗洗脸，可你甭想。契约就是契约。我们讲定了：这些树我们运走，人留给你，随你怎么发落，条件是在这桩买卖里你把他永远封了口。可我不愿看见一个被捆住的人受一个家伙摆弄——换了松绑的时候，他把你能打得满地找牙——这话就这么说定了。想到我们走之后他会落个什么下场，我心里也作呕，可你们别以为可以提前动起手来。我在这儿的时候，你就不许碰他一根指头！弟兄们，你们说呢？」

===Original===
“I say yes,” growled one of McLean’s latest deserters. “What’s more, we’re a pack of fools to risk the dirty work of silencing him. You had him face down and you on his back; why the hell didn’t you cover his head and roll him into the bushes until we were gone? When I went into this, I didn’t understand that he was to see all of us and that there was murder on the ticket. I’m not up to it. I don’t mind lifting trees we came for, but I’m cursed if I want blood on my hands.”

“Well, you ain’t going to get it,” bellowed Jack. “You fellows only contracted to help me get out my marked trees. He belong to Wessner, and it ain’t in our deal what happens to him.”

“Yes, and if Wessner finishes him safely, we are practically in for murder as well as stealing the trees; and if he don’t, all hell’s to pay. I think you’ve made a damnable bungle of this thing; that’s what I think!”

“Then keep your thoughts to yourself,” cried Jack. “We’re doing this, and it’s all planned safe and sure. As for killing that buck﻿—come to think of it, killing is what he needs. He’s away too good for this world of woe, anyhow. I tell you, it’s all safe enough. His dropping out won’t be the only secret the old Limberlost has never told. It’s too dead easy to make it look like he helped take the timber and then cut. Why, he’s played right into our hands. He was here at the swamp all last night, and back again in an hour or so. When we get our plan worked out, even old fool Duncan won’t lift a finger to look for his carcass. We couldn’t have him going in better shape.”

“You just bet,” said Wessner. “I owe him all he’ll get, and be damned to you, but I’ll pay!” he snarled at Freckles.

So it was killing, then. They were not only after this one tree, but many, and with his body it was their plan to kill his honor. To brand him a thief, with them, before the Angel, the Bird Woman, the dear Boss, and the Duncans﻿—Freckles, in sick despair, sagged against the ropes.

===Chinese===
「我说不许，」麦克莱恩手下最近才叛出去的一个汉子低吼道。「再说，咱们冒这个险来干封口的脏活，真是一群傻瓜。当时他脸朝下趴着，你骑在他背上；你见鬼的怎么不干脆蒙住他的脑袋，把他滚进灌木丛里，等咱们走了再说？我入伙的时候，可没弄明白要让他把咱们一个个都看清楚，这票买卖上还写着一条人命。这种事我做不来。抬咱们要的树我不在乎，可要叫我这双手沾上血，打死我也不干。」

「行了，这血落不到你们身上，」杰克大吼。「你们这帮家伙讲好的，只是帮我运出我标好的树。他是韦斯纳的人，他落个什么下场，不归咱们这桩买卖管。」

「是吗，可韦斯纳要是顺顺当当地把他办了，咱们这就不光偷了树，实际上还背上了人命；他要是不办，那更是天塌地陷的大祸。我看你把这件事搞成一团糟了，糟得没法再糟；我就是这么看的！」

「那就把你的想法烂在自个儿肚子里，」杰克嚷道。「这事咱们干定了，安排得稳稳当当，万无一失。至于宰了那小子——细想起来，宰了倒是便宜他。反正他这样的人，活在这么个苦难的世上，实在是太好了，留不得。我跟你说，保险得很。他这么一没影儿，不过给老林伯洛斯特添一桩从来没说出口的秘密罢了。把他弄成帮着运了木料、然后卷逃开溜的样子，再容易不过了。嘿，他简直是白白把自己送进了咱们手心。他昨儿整夜都在沼泽里，个把钟头后又转了回来。等咱们把计划做好手脚，连老傻瓜邓肯也懒得动一根指头去找他那具尸首。他这一走，落进去的把柄再没有比这更齐全的了。」

「那还用说，」韦斯纳说。「他该得的那一切，都是他欠我的——你们统统给我见鬼去，可这笔账我会讨回来！」他冲弗雷克尔斯狞声低吼。

原来是要杀人。他们图的不止这一棵树，还有好多棵；他们还要用他的性命，去杀他的名誉。要给他烙上贼的烙印，把他跟他们拴作一伙，摆在天使、鸟太太、亲爱的老板和邓肯一家面前——弗雷克尔斯悲从中来，绝望得浑身发软，整个人坠在绳子上。

===Original===
Then he gathered his forces and thought swiftly. There was no hope of McLean’s coming. They had chosen a day when they knew he had a big contract at the South camp. The Boss could not come before tomorrow by any possibility, and there would be no tomorrow for the boy. Duncan was on his way to the South camp, and the Bird Woman had said she would come as soon as she could. After the fatigue of the party, it was useless to expect her and the Angel today, and God save them from coming! The Angel’s father had said they would be as safe in the Limberlost as at home. What would he think of this?

The sweat broke on Freckles’ forehead. He tugged at the ropes whenever he felt that he dared, but they were passed around the tree and his body several times, and knotted on his chest. He was helpless. There was no hope, no help. And after they had conspired to make him appear a runaway thief to his loved ones, what was it that Wessner would do to him?

Whatever it was, Freckles lifted his head and resolved that he would bear in mind what he had once heard the Bird Woman say. He would go out bonnily. Never would he let them see, if he grew afraid. After all, what did it matter what they did to his body if by some scheme of the devil they could encompass his disgrace?

Then hope suddenly rose high in Freckles’ breast. They could not do that! The Angel would not believe. Neither would McLean. He would keep up his courage. Kill him they could; dishonor him they could not.

Yet, summon all the fortitude he might, that saw eating into the tree rasped his nerves worse and worse. With whirling brain he gazed into the Limberlost, searching for something, he knew not what, and in blank horror found his eyes focusing on the Angel. She was quite a distance away, but he could see her white lips and angry expression.

Last week he had taken her and the Bird Woman across the swamp over the path he followed in going from his room to the chicken tree. He had told them the night before, that the butterfly tree was on the line close to this path. In figuring on their not coming that day, he failed to reckon with the enthusiasm of the Bird Woman. They must be there for the study, and the Angel had risked crossing the swamp in search of him. Or was there something in his room they needed? The blood surged in his ears as the roar of the Limberlost in the wrath of a storm.

===Chinese===
随后他攒起全身的力气，飞快地想。麦克莱恩是没指望来的。他们挑的这一天，正是他们知道他在南营地有笔大买卖的日子。老板无论如何明天之前来不了，而这孩子已经没有明天了。邓肯正往南营地去了，鸟太太说过她会尽快来。昨天的宴会累成那样，今天休想指望她带着天使来——上帝保佑，千万别来！天使的父亲说过，她们在林伯洛斯特会跟在家里一样安全。他现在会怎么想呢？

弗雷克尔斯的额头上冒出汗来。他觉得谅不被察觉的时候，就偷偷拽一拽绳子，可绳子绕着树和他的身子缠了一道又一道，结头打在他胸口。他一点办法也没有。没有指望，没有救兵。等他们串通好了，把他做成一个卷逃的贼给亲爱的人们看之后，韦斯纳还要把他怎么样呢？

不管怎么样，弗雷克尔斯抬起头，打定主意记着鸟太太有一次说过的话。他要漂漂亮亮地走出门去。就算害怕起来，也决不让他们看出来。说到底，他们若能用什么魔鬼的诡计毁掉他的名誉，那这副皮肉受什么罪，又有什么要紧呢？

就在这时，希望陡然在弗雷克尔斯胸中高高升起。他们办不到！天使不会信的。麦克莱恩也不会。他要鼓起勇气来。杀死他，他们办得到；羞辱他，他们办不到。

可是，任凭他唤起全部的坚忍，那把咬进树身的锯还是把他的神经磨得越来越受不住。他脑子里天旋地转，凝望着林伯洛斯特，想寻找点什么——他自己也不知道是什么——却在一片空白的惊恐中发现，自己的眼睛正对准了天使。她隔着相当远一段距离，可他看得见她发白的嘴唇和愠怒的神情。

上个星期，他领着她和鸟太太穿过沼泽，走的就是他从住处去鸡树的那条小路。昨天晚上他还告诉过她们，蝴蝶树就在铁丝网线上，紧挨着这条路。他盘算她们那天不会来，却没把鸟太太的热忱算进去。她们准是为着研究赶来的，天使竟冒了险，独自穿沼泽来找他。要不，就是他住处里有什么她们需要的东西？血在他耳朵里轰涌，像林伯洛斯特风暴大怒时的咆哮。

===Original===
He looked again, and it had been a dream. She was not there. Had she been? For his life, Freckles could not tell whether he really had seen the Angel, or whether his strained senses had played him the most cruel trick of all. Or was it not the kindest? Now he could go with the vision of her lovely face fresh with him.

“Thank You for that, oh God!” whispered Freckles. “ ’Twas more than kind of You and I don’t s’pose I ought to be wanting anything else; but if You can, oh, I wish I could know before this ends, if ’twas me mother”﻿—Freckles could not even whisper the words, for he hesitated a second and ended﻿—“*If ’twas me mother did it!*”

“Freckles! Freckles! Oh, Freckles!” the voice of the Angel came calling. Freckles swayed forward and wrenched at the rope until it cut deeply into his body.

“Hell!” cried Black Jack. “Who is that? Do you know?”

Freckles nodded.

Jack whipped out a revolver and snatched the gag from Freckles’ mouth.

===Chinese===
他再看，那原来是一场梦。她不在那儿。她刚才在过吗？弗雷克尔斯拼命想也说不清，自己究竟真看见了天使呢，还是绷得快要断掉的神经跟他开了一个最残酷的玩笑。可这难道不也是最仁慈的玩笑吗？这样一来，他走的时候，就能带着她那张可爱脸庞崭新的幻影一道走了。

「为这个谢谢您，哦，上帝！」弗雷克尔斯悄声说。「您这不止是仁慈了，俺想俺本不该再盼别的什么；可要是您能，哦，俺但愿能在这一切了结之前知道，那是不是俺娘——」弗雷克尔斯连悄声说都说不出来，他迟疑了一秒钟，末了成了——「*要是知道那是不是俺娘干的就好了！*」

「弗雷克尔斯！弗雷克尔斯！哦，弗雷克尔斯！」天使的声音一路呼唤过来。弗雷克尔斯朝前猛地一扑，死命拽那绳子，绳子深深勒进了他的身子。

「见鬼！」黑杰克喊道。「那是谁？你知道吗？」

弗雷克尔斯点了点头。

杰克唰地抽出一把左轮手枪，一把扯掉弗雷克尔斯嘴里的堵物。

===Original===
“Say quick, or it’s up with you right now, and whoever that is with you!”

“It’s the girl the Bird Woman takes with her,” whispered Freckles through dry, swollen lips.

“They ain’t due here for five days yet,” said Wessner. “We got on to that last week.”

“Yes,” said Freckles, “but I found a tree covered with butterflies and things along the east line yesterday that I thought the Bird Woman would want extra, and I went to town to tell her last night. She said she’d come soon, but she didn’t say when. They must be here. I take care of the girl while the Bird Woman works. Untie me quick until she is gone. I’ll try to send her back, and then you can go on with your dirty work.”

“He ain’t lying,” volunteered Wessner. “I saw that tree covered with butterflies and him watching around it when we were spying on him yesterday.”

“No, he leaves lying to your sort,” snapped Black Jack, as he undid the rope and pitched it across the room. “Remember that you’re covered every move you make, my buck,” he cautioned.

===Chinese===
「快说！不然你这会儿就完蛋，连那边跟你一起的那个也跑不了！」

「是鸟太太带在身边的那位小姐，」弗雷克尔斯从干肿的嘴唇里挤出这句话。

「她们还有五天才该来呢，」韦斯纳说。「这事儿咱们上星期就打听明白了。」

「是，」弗雷克尔斯说，「可俺昨天在东线边上发现了一棵落满蝴蝶和那些东西的树，俺寻思鸟太太准会特别想要，昨儿夜里就进城告诉她去了。她说她很快就来，只是没说哪天。她们准是到了。鸟太太干活的时候，一向是俺照看这位小姐的。趁她还没走，快解开俺。俺想法子打发她回去，然后你们再接着干你们的脏活儿不迟。」

「他没撒谎，」韦斯纳主动搭腔。「昨天咱们盯他梢的时候，我看见那棵落满蝴蝶的树了，还看见他在四下守着它。」

「是啊，撒谎的营生他留给你们这号人，」黑杰克厉声说，一边解开绳子，把它扔到屋子那头去。「记住，你每动一下都有枪口罩着，小子，」他警告道。

===Original===
“Freckles! Freckles!” came the Angel’s impatient voice, closer and closer.

“I must be answering,” said Freckles, and Jack nodded. “Right here!” he called, and to the men: “You go on with your work, and remember one thing yourselves. The work of the Bird Woman is known all over the world. This girl’s father is a rich man, and she is all he has. If you offer hurt of any kind to either of them, this world has no place far enough away or dark enough for you to be hiding in. Hell will be easy to what any man will get if he touches either of them!”

“Freckles, where are you?” demanded the Angel.

Soulsick with fear for her, Freckles went toward her and parted the bushes that she might enter. She came through without apparently giving him a glance, and the first words she said were: “Why have the gang come so soon? I didn’t know you expected them for three weeks yet. Or is this some especial tree that Mr. McLean needs to fill an order right now?”

Freckles hesitated. Would a man dare lie to save himself? No. But to save the Angel﻿—surely that was different. He opened his lips, but the Angel was capable of saving herself. She walked among them, exactly as if she had been reared in a lumber camp, and never waited for an answer.

“Why, your specimen case!” she cried. “Look! Haven’t you noticed that it’s tipped over? Set it straight, quickly!”

===Chinese===
「弗雷克尔斯！弗雷克尔斯！」天使的声音不耐烦起来，越来越近。

「俺总得应一声儿，」弗雷克尔斯说，杰克点了点头。「在这儿哪！」他喊道，又对那伙人说：「你们只管干你们的活，可有件事自己掂量着。鸟太太的作品全世界都知道。这位小姐的爸爸是个有钱人，她就是他的一切。你们谁要是碰她们俩一根毫毛——不管什么样的伤损——这个世界上就再没有够远、够黑的地方，容得下你们藏身。谁敢动她们俩中任何一个，将来落到他头上的，下地狱都算便宜！」

「弗雷克尔斯，你在哪儿？」天使问。

弗雷克尔斯为她的安危怕得魂都要碎了，他迎上去，拨开灌木好让她进来。她穿进来，像是一眼也没看他，开口头一句就是：「那伙人怎么来得这么早？我记得你说还要等三个星期呢。要不，就是麦克莱恩先生眼下急着要哪棵特别的树，去凑一笔订货？」

弗雷克尔斯迟疑了。为救自己，一个人敢撒谎吗？不敢。可为救天使——那自然不一样。他张开嘴，可天使自己就有本事救自己。她在那伙人中间走来走去，那份自在，就像她是在伐木营里长大的，而且压根儿不等回答。

「哎呀，你的标本箱！」她叫道。「瞧！你都没发现它翻倒了吗？快把它扶正！」

===Original===
A couple of the men stepped out and carefully righted the case.

“There! That’s better,” she said. “Freckles, I’m surprised at your being so careless. It would be a shame to break those lovely butterflies for one old tree! Is that a valuable tree? Why didn’t you tell us last night you were going to take out a tree this morning? Oh, say, did you put your case there to protect that tree from that stealing old Black Jack and his gang? I bet you did! Well, if that wasn’t bright! What kind of a tree is it?”

“It’s a white oak,” said Freckles.

“Like those they make dining-tables and sideboards from?”

“Yes.”

“My! How interesting!” she cried. “I don’t know a thing about timber, but my father wants me to learn just everything I can. I am going to ask him to let me come here and watch you until I know enough to boss a gang myself. Do you like to cut trees, gentlemen?” she asked with angelic sweetness of the men.

===Chinese===
有两个汉子走出来，小心翼翼地把箱子扶正了。

「这就对了！好多了，」她说。「弗雷克尔斯，你怎么这样不小心，真让我吃惊。为了一棵老树，摔坏了那些可爱的蝴蝶，多可惜呀！这棵树很值钱吗？昨天晚上你怎么不告诉我们，今天早上要运一棵树出去？哦，对了，你是不是把箱子搁在那儿，护着那棵树，防着那个爱偷东西的老黑杰克和他那伙人？我敢打赌你就是！嘿，这可真聪明！这是棵什么树呀？」

「是一棵白橡木（white oak），」弗雷克尔斯说。

「就是人家用来做餐桌和餐具柜的那种？」

「是。」

「哎呀！多有意思呀！」她叫道。「木料的事我一窍不通，可我爸爸要我能学什么就学什么。我要去求他许我上这儿来，看着你们干活，一直看到我自己也懂得够多了，能亲自领一伙人干活为止。各位先生，你们喜欢砍树吗？」她带着天使般的甜美问那伙人。

===Original===
Some of them appeared foolish and some grim, but one managed to say they did.

Then the Angel’s eyes turned full on Black Jack, and she gave the most natural little start of astonishment.

“Oh! I almost thought that you were a ghost!” she cried. “But I see now that you are really and truly. Were you ever in Colorado?”

“No,” said Jack.

“I see you aren’t the same man,” said the Angel. “You know, we were in Colorado last year, and there was a cowboy who was the handsomest man anywhere around. He’d come riding into town every night, and all we girls just adored him! Oh, but he was a beauty! I thought at first glance you were really he, but I see now he wasn’t nearly so tall nor so broad as you, and only half as handsome.”

The men began to laugh while Jack flushed crimson. The Angel joined in the laugh.

===Chinese===
他们有的显得傻了眼，有的绷着脸，总算有一个支支吾吾地说了声喜欢。

接着，天使的眼睛整个转到了黑杰克身上，她那副吃惊的小模样，自然得不能再自然。

「哎呀！我差点儿以为你是个鬼魂呢！」她叫道。「不过现在我可看清楚了，你是有血有肉的真人。你去过科罗拉多（Colorado）吗？」

「没有，」杰克说。

「我就说你不是同一个人嘛，」天使说。「是这样，我们去年在科罗拉多，那儿有个牛仔，是方圆左近最俊俏的男人。他每天夜里骑马进城来，我们一帮姑娘家呀，把他崇拜得不得了！哦，他可真是个美男子！我乍一看，还当真以为你就是他呢，可现在我瞧明白了，他哪里有你这么高、这么壮，俊俏嘛，也只有你的一半。」

那伙人笑了起来，杰克的脸涨得通红。天使也跟着笑了。

===Original===
“Well, I’ll leave it to you! Isn’t he handsome?” she challenged. “As for that cowboy’s face, it couldn’t be compared with yours. The only trouble with you is that your clothes are spoiling you. It’s the dress those cowboys wear that makes half their attraction. If you were properly clothed, you could break the heart of the prettiest girl in the country.”

With one accord the other men looked at Black Jack, and for the first time realized that he was a superb specimen of manhood, for he stood six feet tall, was broad, well-rounded, and had dark, even skin, big black eyes, and full red lips.

“I’ll tell you what!” exclaimed the Angel. “I’d just love to see you on horseback. Nothing sets a handsome man off so splendidly. Do you ride?”

“Yes,” said Jack, and his eyes were burning on the Angel as if he would fathom the depths of her soul.

“Well,” said the Angel winsomely, “I know what I just wish you’d do. I wish you would let your hair grow a little longer. Then wear a blue flannel shirt a little open at the throat, a red tie, and a broad-brimmed felt hat, and ride past my house of evenings. I’m always at home then, and almost always on the veranda, and, oh! but I would like to see you! Will you do that for me?” It is impossible to describe the art with which the Angel asked the question. She was looking straight into Jack’s face, coarse and hardened with sin and careless living, which was now taking on a wholly different expression. The evil lines of it were softening and fading under her clear gaze. A dull red flamed into his bronze cheeks, while his eyes were growing brightly tender.

“Yes,” he said, and the glance he gave the men was of such a nature that no one saw fit even to change countenance.

===Chinese===
「好了，你们大家给评评理！他难道不俊俏吗？」她挑起话头。「至于那个牛仔的脸嘛，根本没法跟你的比。你唯一的毛病，就是这身穿戴拖累了你。牛仔们那身行头，才占了他们一半的风头。你要是穿得像模像样，全国最漂亮的姑娘的心，你都能叫它碎掉。」

那伙人都不约而同地打量起黑杰克来，头一回发现他真是条百里挑一的汉子：他身高六英尺，肩宽背圆，皮肤是深色的，匀净细腻，一双黑黑的大眼睛，两片饱满的红嘴唇。

「我告诉你个事儿！」天使喊道。「我真想看看你骑在马上的样子。什么也及不上骏马衬托一个俊俏的男人。你会骑马吗？」

「会，」杰克说，他的眼睛灼灼地盯着天使，仿佛要把她灵魂的深处看穿。

「那么，」天使妩媚地说，「我知道我心里正盼着你做一件事。我盼着你把头发稍稍留长一点儿。然后穿一件蓝法兰绒衬衫，领口敞开一点，系一条红领带，戴一顶宽檐毡帽，每天傍晚骑马打我家门前过。那时候我总在家，而且差不多总在阳台上，哦！我多想看见你呀！你肯为我这么做吗？」天使问这句话时的那份艺术，是怎么也形容不出来的。她直直地望进杰克那张脸——那张被罪恶和荒唐日子磨得粗粝僵硬的脸——那张脸此刻正换上一副全然不同的神情。在她澄澈的注视下，脸上的凶横线条正渐渐软化、消退。一点暗红在他古铜色的脸颊上烧起来，他的眼睛则越来越亮，越来越温柔。

「肯，」他说。他朝那伙人扫过去的那一眼，有那样的分量，谁都不敢哪怕变一变脸色。

===Original===
“Oh, goody!” she cried, tilting on her toes. “I’ll ask all the girls to come see, but they needn’t stick in! We can get along without them, can’t we?”

Jack leaned toward her. He was the charmed fluttering bird, while the Angel was the snake.

“Well, I rather guess!” he cried.

The Angel drew a deep breath and surveyed him rapturously.

“My, but you’re tall!” she commented. “Do you suppose I ever will grow to reach your shoulders?”

She stood on tiptoe and measured the distance with her eyes. Then she developed timid confusion, while her glance sought the ground.

===Chinese===
「哦，太好啦！」她踮着脚尖欢呼。「我要请所有的姑娘们都来看，可她们不用往里挤！没有她们咱们也过得去，对不对？」

杰克朝她俯下身去。他成了那只中了魔法、扑腾着翅膀的小鸟，而天使才是那条蛇。

「嘿，那可不！」他喊道。

天使深深吸了一口气，如醉如痴地打量着他。

「天哪，你可真高！」她评说道。「你说，我将来能长得够得着你的肩膀吗？」

她踮起脚尖，用眼睛丈量这段距离。随后她生出一种羞怯的慌乱，目光垂向了地面。

===Original===
“I wish I could do something,” she half whispered.

Jack seemed to increase an inch in height.

“What?” he asked hoarsely.

“Lariat Bill used always to have a bunch of red flowers in his shirt pocket. The red lit up his dark eyes and olive cheeks and made him splendid. May I put some red flowers on you?”

Freckles stared as he wheezed for breath. He wished the earth would open and swallow him. Was he dead or alive? Since his Angel had seen Black Jack she never had glanced his way. Was she completely bewitched? Would she throw herself at the man’s feet before them all? Couldn’t she give him even one thought? Hadn’t she seen that he was gagged and bound? Did she truly think that these were McLean’s men? Why, she could not! It was only a few days ago that she had been close enough to this man and angry enough with him to peel the hat from his head with a shot! Suddenly a thing she had said jestingly to him one day came back with startling force: “You must take Angels on trust.” Of course you must! She was his Angel. She must have seen! His life, and what was far more, her own, was in her hands. There was nothing he could do but trust her. Surely she was working out some plan.

The Angel knelt beside his flower bed and recklessly tore up by the roots a big bunch of foxfire.

===Chinese===
「真希望我能做点什么，」她半是耳语地说。

杰克像是又长高了一英寸。

「做什么？」他声音沙哑地问。

「套索比尔（Lariat Bill）以前衬衫口袋里总是插着一束红花。那红色把他乌黑的眼睛和橄榄色的脸颊一映，叫他神气极了。我能给你也别上一些红花吗？」

弗雷克尔斯喘着粗气，眼睛直直地瞪着。他恨不得大地裂开把他吞下去。他到底是死了还是活着？自打他的天使看见黑杰克，她就再没往他这边瞥过一眼。她是彻底中了魔吗？她要当着他们所有人的面，扑到那个男人脚下去吗？她连一个念头都不能分给他吗？她没看见他被堵着嘴、捆着吗？她当真以为这些人是麦克莱恩的人马？不，她不可能！就在几天之前，她还离这个男人那么近、对他那么怒不可遏，一枪把他头上的帽子掀了下来呢！忽然，她有一天跟他打趣说过的一句话，带着惊人的力量回到了他心里：「对天使，你必须全凭信任。」当然必须！她是他的天使。她一定看见了！他的性命，还有比性命贵重得多的、她自己的性命，都攥在她手里。他毫无办法，只有信她。她准是在一步步实施什么计划。

天使在他的花圃旁跪下，不管不顾地连根拔起一大把狐火。

===Original===
“These stems are so tough and sticky,” she said. “I can’t break them. Loan me your knife,” she ordered Freckles.

As she reached for the knife, her back was for one second toward the men. She looked into his eyes and deliberately winked.

She severed the stems, tossed the knife to Freckles, and walking to Jack, laid the flowers over his heart.

Freckles broke into a sweat of agony. He had said she would be safe in a herd of howling savages. Would she? If Black Jack even made a motion toward touching her, Freckles knew that from somewhere he would muster the strength to kill him. He mentally measured the distance to where his club lay and set his muscles for a spring. But no﻿—by the splendor of God! The big fellow was baring his head with a hand that was unsteady. The Angel pulled one of the long silver pins from her hat and fastened her flowers securely.

Freckles was quaking. What was to come next? What was she planning, and oh! did she understand the danger of her presence among those men; the real necessity for action?

As the Angel stepped from Jack, she turned her head to one side and peered at him, quite as Freckles had seen the little yellow fellow do on the line a hundred times, and said: “Well, that does the trick! Isn’t that fine? See how it sets him off, boys? Don’t you forget the tie is to be red, and the first ride soon. I can’t wait very long. Now I must go. The Bird Woman will be ready to start, and she will come here hunting me next, for she is busy today. What did I come here for anyway?”

===Chinese===
「这些花茎又韧又黏，」她说。「我掐不断。把你的刀借我，」她吩咐弗雷克尔斯。

她伸手接刀的时候，背朝那伙人有一秒钟。她望进他的眼睛，不慌不忙地眨了一下。

她铰断花茎，把刀抛还给弗雷克尔斯，走到杰克跟前，把那束花放在他心口上。

弗雷克尔斯出了一身痛苦的冷汗。他说过，她就是混在一群嚎叫的野人里也万无一失。真的吗？黑杰克哪怕朝她抬一抬手，弗雷克尔斯也知道，自己不知从哪儿会攒出杀了他的力气来。他在心里丈量他那根木棒躺着的距离，绷紧了肌肉准备扑出去。可是不——上帝的光荣啊！那条大汉正用一只发颤的手，摘下自己的帽子。天使从帽子上抽下一根长长的银别针，把她那束花牢牢别住。

弗雷克尔斯浑身发颤。接下来会是什么？她在盘算什么？哦！她明白不明白，她身在这些人中间有多危险；明白不明白非动手不可的道理？

天使从杰克身边退开时，把头偏向一侧，眯眼端详他，那模样，活脱脱是弗雷克尔斯在铁丝网线上见过一百回的那个黄衣小家伙（金翅雀），嘴里说：「嘿，这就成了！好不好看？弟兄们，瞧这多给他添彩呀！你们可别忘了，领带要红的，头一回骑马来要趁早。我可等不了太久。现在我得走了。鸟太太该动身了，她忙着呢，回头准要找到这儿来寻我。咦，我到底是来干什么的呀？」

===Original===
She glanced inquiringly around, and several of the men laughed. Oh, the delight of it! She had forgotten her errand for him! Jack had a second increase in height. The Angel glanced helplessly as if seeking a clue. Then her eyes fell, as if by accident, on Freckles, and she cried, “Oh, I know now! It was those magazines the Bird Woman promised you. I came to tell you that we put them under the box where we hide things, at the entrance to the swamp as we came in. I knew I would need my hands crossing the swamp, so I hid them there. You’ll find them at the same old place.”

Then Freckles spoke.

“It’s mighty risky for you to be crossing the swamp alone,” he said. “I’m surprised that the Bird Woman would be letting you try it. I know it’s a little farther, but it’s begging you I am to be going back by the trail. That’s bad enough, but it’s far safer than the swamp.”

The Angel laughed merrily.

“Oh stop your nonsense!” she cried. “I’m not afraid! Not in the least! The Bird Woman didn’t want me to try following a path that I’d been over only once, but I was sure I could do it, and I’m rather proud of the performance. Now, don’t go babying! You know I’m not afraid!”

“No,” said Freckles gently, “I know you’re not; but that has nothing to do with the fact that your friends are afraid for you. On the trail you can see your way a bit ahead, and you’ve all the world a better chance if you meet a snake.”

===Chinese===
她询问似的环顾四周，那伙人里有几个笑出了声。哦，这有多妙呀！她竟把为他办的正事给忘了！杰克又长高了第二截。天使无可奈何地四下张望，像在找什么线索。然后她的目光像是碰巧落在了弗雷克尔斯身上，她叫起来：「哦，我想起来了！是鸟太太答应你的那几本杂志。我是来告诉你，我们把它们搁在那块藏东西的箱子底下，就在我们进沼泽来的入口那儿。我知道过沼泽要腾出两只手，就把它们藏在那儿了。你到老地方去取就行。」

这时弗雷克尔斯开口了。

「您一个人穿沼泽，太悬了，」他说。「鸟太太竟肯让您试这个，俺真没想到。俺知道绕小道要多走一点儿路，可俺求您了，回去还是走巡林小道吧。小道也够糟的了，可比沼泽安稳得多。」

天使快活地笑了。

「哦，别胡说啦！」她叫道。「我才不怕呢！一点儿也不怕！鸟太太本来不愿我去认一条只走过一回的小路，可我拿得准自己走得下来，这份成绩我还挺得意的呢。好了，别婆婆妈妈的！你知道我不害怕！」

「不，」弗雷克尔斯温和地说，「俺知道您不怕；可您的朋友们替您怕，这是两码事。走小道，前头的路看得见个大概，真遇上蛇，您也十倍百倍地更有脱身的指望。」

===Original===
Then Freckles had an inspiration. He turned to Jack imploringly.

“You tell her!” he pleaded. “Tell her to go by the trail. She will for you.”

The implication of this statement was so gratifying to Black Jack that he seemed again to expand and take on increase before their very eyes.

“You bet!” exclaimed Jack. And to the Angel: “You better take Freckles’ word for it, miss. He knows the old swamp better than any of us, except me, and if he says ‘go by the trail,’ you’d best do it.”

The Angel hesitated. She wanted to recross the swamp and try to reach the horse. She knew Freckles would brave any danger to save her crossing the swamp alone, but she really was not afraid, while the trail added over a mile to the walk. She knew the path. She intended to run for dear life the instant she felt herself from their sight, and tucked in the folds of her blouse was a fine little 32-caliber revolver that her father had presented her for her share in what he was pleased to call her military exploit. One last glance at Freckles showed her the agony in his eyes, and immediately she imagined he had some other reason. She would follow the trail.

“All right,” she said, giving Jack a thrilling glance. “If you say so, I’ll return by the trail to please you. Goodbye, everybody.”

===Chinese===
这时弗雷克尔斯灵机一动。他转向杰克，满眼恳求。

「您跟她说！」他恳求道。「您劝她走小道回去。您的话她准听。」

这句话暗含的意思让黑杰克这样受用，他仿佛当着众人的眼睛，又胀大了一圈，长高了一截。

「那还用说！」杰克喊道。又对天使说：「小姐，你还是听弗雷克尔斯的话吧。这片老沼泽，他比咱们谁都熟——只除了我。他说『走小道』，你就照办准没错。」

天使迟疑了。她本想再穿一遍沼泽，去够到那匹马。她知道，为了不让她独自穿沼泽，弗雷克尔斯什么险都肯冒；可她实在不怕，而小道要多走一英里多的路。她认得那条小路。她打定主意，一觉得自己的身影离开了他们的视线，就撒开腿没命地跑；她衬衫的褶皱里还别着一支精巧的 32 口径小左轮，是父亲为着她那份被他笑称为「赫赫战功」的功劳送给她的。最后望弗雷克尔斯一眼，她看见了他眼里的痛苦，立刻便想到，他多半还有别的缘故。她决定走小道。

「好吧，」她说，给了杰克一个动人心魄的眼色。「既然你这么说，为了让你高兴，我就从巡林小道回去。诸位，再见啦。」

===Original===
She lifted the bushes and started toward the entrance.

“You damned fool! Stop her!” growled Wessner. “Keep her till we’re loaded, anyhow. You’re playing hell! Can’t you see that when this thing is found out, there she’ll be to ruin all of us. If you let her go, every man of us has got to cut, and some of us will be caught sure.”

Jack sprang forward. Freckles’ heart muffled in his throat. The Angel seemed to divine Jack’s coming. She was humming a little song. She deliberately stopped and began pulling the heads of the curious grasses that grew all around her. When she straightened, she took a step backward and called: “Ho! Freckles, the Bird Woman wants that natural history pamphlet returned. It belongs to a set she is going to have bound. That’s one of the reasons we put it under the box. You be sure to get them as you go home tonight, for fear it rains or becomes damp with the heavy dews.”

“All right,” said Freckles, but it was in a voice that he never had heard before.

Then the Angel turned and sent a parting glance at Jack. She was overpoweringly human and bewitchingly lovely.

“You won’t forget that ride and the red tie,” she half asserted, half questioned.

===Chinese===
她掀起灌木，朝入口走去。

「你这该死的蠢货！拦住她！」韦斯纳咆哮道。「哪怕留她到咱们装完车再放。你这是在玩火！你不明白吗，等这事儿败露了，有她在场，咱们一个都跑不了。你放她走，咱们人人都得开溜，有几个准得被抓。」

杰克一个箭步蹿上前。弗雷克尔斯的心堵在了嗓子眼里。天使却像是料到了杰克要来。她正哼着一支小曲儿。她故意停下脚步，动手去揪四周那些长相古怪的草的草穗。等她直起身，她朝后退了一步，喊道：「嗬！弗雷克尔斯，鸟太太要你把那本博物学小册子还回去。那是一套里的一本，她正要拿去装订成册的呢。这也是我们把它搁在箱子底下的一个缘故。今晚你回家的时候千万记得取上，怕的是下雨，或是叫大浓露水打潮了。」

「好的，」弗雷克尔斯说，可那嗓音，他自己从来没有听见过。

然后天使转过身，送给杰克临去的一瞥。她的人间味儿摄人心魄，模样儿妩媚得叫人着魔。

「你不会忘了那次骑马、那条红领带的，」她半是断言，半是探问。

===Original===
Jack succumbed. Freckles was his captive, but he was the Angel’s, soul and body. His face wore the holiest look it ever had known as he softly reechoed Freckles’ “All right.” With her head held well up, the Angel walked slowly away, and Jack turned to the men.

“Drop your damned staring and saw wood,” he shouted. “Don’t you know anything at all about how to treat a lady?” It might have been a question which of the cronies that crouched over green wood fires in the cabins of Wildcat Hollow, eternally sucking a corncob pipe and stirring the endless kettles of stewing coon and opossum, had taught him to do even as well as he had by the Angel.

The men muttered and threatened among themselves, but they began working desperately. Someone suggested that a man be sent to follow the Angel and to watch her and the Bird Woman leave the swamp. Freckles’ heart sank within him, but Jack was in a delirium and past all caution.

“Yes,” he sneered. “Mebby all of you had better give over on the saw and run after the girl. I guess not! Seems to me I got the favors. I didn’t see no bouquets on the rest of you! If anybody follows her, I do, and I’m needed here among such a pack of idiots. There’s no danger in that baby face. She wouldn’t give me away! You double and work like forty, while me and Wessner will take the axes and begin to cut in on the other side.”

“What about the noise?” asked Wessner.

“No difference about the noise,” answered Jack. “She took us to be from McLean’s gang, slick as grease. Make the chips fly!”

===Chinese===
杰克降服了。弗雷克尔斯是他的俘虏，他却是天使的俘虏，连灵魂带肉体。他柔声应了一句弗雷克尔斯方才那句「好的」时，那张脸上竟带着它从未有过的神圣神情。天使高高地昂着头，慢慢走远了，杰克这才转向那伙人。

「都把你们那死鱼眼收起来，干活儿锯木头！」他吼道。「要怎么款待一位小姐，你们就一点儿也不懂吗？」这倒可以算个问题了：野猫谷那些小屋里、蹲在青柴火堆旁的老哥们儿——一个个永远叼着玉米芯烟斗（corncob pipe），搅着永远熬不完的浣熊（coon）肉负鼠（opossum）肉炖锅——究竟是哪一位教会了他方才对天使的那一手，还真说不清。

那伙人自伙里嘀嘀咕咕、恫吓来恫吓去，可还是拼命干起活来。有人提议派个人去跟上天使，盯住她，看她同鸟太太离开沼泽。弗雷克尔斯的心直往下沉，可杰克正晕晕乎乎在兴头上，什么小心都顾不上了。

「好啊，」他冷笑。「你们各位干脆都撂下锯，去追那位小姐得了，那敢情好。我看不必！依我看，得彩头的是我。你们大伙儿身上，我可没见着谁别着花球！要真有人跟她去，那也是我——何况这儿围着我这么一群笨蛋，还缺不得我。那么一张娃娃脸，能有什么危险。她才不会出卖我呢！你们一个顶四十地卖力干，我和韦斯纳拿斧头，从对面下斧。」

「声音吵出去怎么办？」韦斯纳问。

「吵就吵呗，没关系，」杰克答道。「她把咱们当成麦克莱恩的人马了，信得个溜光水滑。让木屑飞起来吧！」

===Original===
So all of them attacked the big tree.

Freckles sat on one of his benches and waited. In their haste to fell the tree and load it, so that the teamsters could start, and leave them free to attack another, they had forgotten to rebind him.

The Angel was on the trail and safely started. The cold perspiration made Freckles’ temples clammy and ran in little streams down his chest. It would take her more time to follow the trail, but her safety was Freckles’ sole thought in urging her to go that way. He tried to figure on how long it would require to walk to the carriage. He wondered if the Bird Woman had unhitched. He followed the Angel every step of the way. He figured on when she would cross the path of the clearing, pass the deep pool where his “find-out” frog lived, cross Sleepy Snake Creek, and reach the carriage.

He wondered what she would say to the Bird Woman, and how long it would take them to pack and start. He knew now that they would understand, and the Angel would try to get the Boss there in time to save his wager. She could never do it, for the saw was over half through, and Jack and Wessner cutting into the opposite side of the tree. It appeared as if they could fell at least that tree, before McLean could come, and if they did he lost his wager.

When it was down, would they rebind him and leave him for Wessner to wreak his insane vengeance on, or would they take him along to the next tree and dispose of him when they had stolen all the timber they could? Jack had said that he should not be touched until he left. Surely he would not run all that risk for one tree, when he had many others of far greater value marked. Freckles felt that he had some hope to cling to now, but he found himself praying that the Angel would hurry.

Once Jack came to Freckles and asked if he had any water. Freckles arose and showed him where he kept his drinking-water. Jack drank in great gulps, and as he passed back the bucket, he said: “When a man’s got a chance of catching a fine girl like that, he ought not be mixed up in any dirty business. I wish to God I was out of this!”

===Chinese===
于是他们全体向那棵大树进攻了。

弗雷克尔斯坐在自己的一条长凳上等着。他们急着放倒这棵树、装上车，好打发赶车的动身，腾出手去攻下一棵，竟忘了把他重新捆上。

天使上了巡林小道，顺顺当当走起来了。冷汗浸得弗雷克尔斯两鬓又湿又凉，一股股细流顺着胸口往下淌。走小道要多费些时候，可劝她走那条道，图的就是她的安全——这是弗雷克尔斯唯一的念头。他试着估算走到马车那儿要多长时间。他寻思鸟太太是不是已经卸了车。他跟着天使一步一步地走。他盘算她什么时候会横过林间空地的小径，什么时候经过他那「弄明白」牛蛙住的深潭，什么时候跨过睡蛇溪，走到马车跟前。

他猜她会对鸟太太说什么，猜她们收拾启程要多久。他现在明白了，她们准会懂的，天使会想法子把老板及时搬来，救下他那场赌。可她怎么也赶不上了：锯已经过了大半，杰克和韦斯纳正从树身对面往里砍。看样子，麦克莱恩赶到之前，他们至少放得倒这一棵；真放倒了，他的赌就输了。

树倒了之后，他们会把他重新捆上，留给韦斯纳去发泄那疯狂的毒恨呢，还是会把他带到下一棵树，等偷够了木料再处置他？杰克说过，他走之前不许碰他。他标下的树里还有好多棵值钱得多的，他总不会单单为这一棵，就去冒那么大的险。弗雷克尔斯觉得，这下总算有点指望可以抓了，可他发现自己一直在祷告，求天使快一点儿。

有一回杰克走到弗雷克尔斯跟前，问他有没有水。弗雷克尔斯站起来，指给他自己存饮水的地方。杰克大口大口地喝着，把桶递回来的时候，他说：「一个人既然有机会娶到那样一位好姑娘，就不该搅和在什么脏买卖里。我对天发誓，真想从这摊子里拔出脚来！」

===Original===
Freckles answered heartily: “I wish I was, too!”

Jack stared at him a minute and then broke into a roar of rough laughter.

“Blest if I blame you,” he said. “But you had your chance! We offered you a fair thing and you gave Wessner his answer. I ain’t envying you when he gives you his.”

“You’re six to one,” answered Freckles. “It will be easy enough for you to be killing the body of me, but, curse you all, you can’t blacken me soul!”

“Well, I’d give anything you could name if I had your honesty,” said Jack.

When the mighty tree fell, the Limberlost shivered and screamed with the echo. Freckles groaned in despair, but the gang took heart. That was so much accomplished. They knew where to dispose of it safely, with no questions asked. Before the day was over, they could remove three others, all suitable for veneer and worth far more than this. Then they would leave Freckles to Wessner and scatter for safety, with more money than they had ever hoped for in their possession.

===Chinese===
弗雷克尔斯真心实意地答：「俺也想拔出来！」

杰克瞪了他一分钟，随即爆出一阵粗野的大笑。

「说真格的，我不怪你，」他说。「可你的机会是有过的！我们开给你的条件公道，是你自己给了韦斯纳那个回答。等他把他的回答给你的时候，我才不眼红你呢。」

「你们是六个打俺一个，」弗雷克尔斯答道。「要杀俺这条身子，对你们容易得很；可你们这帮该咒的，俺的灵魂你们抹不了黑！」

「唉，我要是有你这份诚实，你开什么价我都肯出，」杰克说。

那棵巨树轰然倒下时，林伯洛斯特随着回声簌簌发抖、尖声长啸。弗雷克尔斯绝望地呻吟出声，那伙人却壮起了胆。这就算办成了这么些。他们知道上哪儿脱手最稳妥，没人会多问一句。天黑之前，他们还能再运走三棵，棵棵都是做镶面板的好料，比这一棵值钱得多。然后他们就把弗雷克尔斯丢给韦斯纳，各自逃散保平安，揣着他们做梦也没指望过的那么多的钱。

## XIII Wherein the Angel releases Freckles, and the curse of Black Jack falls upon her. / ## XIII 本章天使解下弗雷克尔斯，黑杰克的毒咒落到她头上。

===Original===
On the line, the Angel gave one backward glance at Black Jack, to see that he had returned to his work. Then she gathered her skirts above her knees and leaped forward on the run. In the first three yards she passed Freckles’ wheel. Instantly she imagined that was why he had insisted on her coming by the trail. She seized it and sprang on. The saddle was too high, but she was an expert rider and could catch the pedals as they came up. She stopped at Duncan’s cabin long enough to remedy this, telling Mrs. Duncan while working what was happening, and for her to follow the east trail until she found the Bird Woman, and told her that she had gone after McLean and for her to leave the swamp as quickly as possible.

Even with her fear for Freckles to spur her, Sarah Duncan blanched and began shivering at the idea of facing the Limberlost. The Angel looked her in the eyes.

“No matter how afraid you are, you have to go,” she said. “If you don’t the Bird Woman will go to Freckles’ room, hunting me, and they will have trouble with her. If she isn’t told to leave at once, they may follow me, and, finding I’m gone, do some terrible thing to Freckles. I can’t go﻿—that’s flat﻿—for if they caught me, then there’d be no one to go for help. You don’t suppose they are going to take out the trees they’re after and then leave Freckles to run and tell? They are going to murder the boy; that’s what they are going to do. You run, and run for life! For Freckles’ life! You can ride back with the Bird Woman.”

The Angel saw Mrs. Duncan started; then began her race.

===Chinese===
在铁丝线旁，天使回头瞥了黑杰克一眼，看清他已回到活计上，便撩起裙裾掖过膝头，拔腿向前飞奔。头三码路她就经过了弗雷克尔斯的自行车。她立刻恍然：他先前执意要她从小道这边走，原来是为了这个。她一把抓过车子跨上去。车座太高，可她是骑术好手，脚蹬转上来时总归踩得住。到邓肯家的小屋时她稍停了停，把座位放低，一边动手一边把出了什么事讲给邓肯太太听，请她顺着东边小道去寻鸟太太，就说她已去搬麦克莱恩，请鸟太太尽快离开沼泽。

就算有对弗雷克尔斯的忧惧在背后催着，一想到要独自面对林伯洛斯特，莎拉·邓肯还是脸色发白，浑身打起颤来。天使直直望进她的眼睛。

「不管你多害怕，都必须去。」她说。「你若不去，鸟太太就会到弗雷克尔斯的屋里来寻我，他们就会对她下毒手。若不立刻叫她走，他们没准会追上来，一见我跑了，就对弗雷克尔斯痛下杀手。我不能去——没得商量——要是他们把我也捉住，就再没有人去搬救兵了。你总不至于以为，他们把想要的树弄到手之后，还会留下弗雷克尔斯，放他跑去报信吧？他们要谋害这孩子；他们打的就是这个主意。你跑吧，拼着命跑！为的是弗雷克尔斯的命！回头你可以跟鸟太太一道坐车回来。」

天使看着邓肯太太上了路，才转身开始自己的一场赛跑。

===Original===
Those awful miles of corduroy! Would they never end? She did not dare use the wheel too roughly, for if it broke she never could arrive on time afoot. Where her way was impassable for the wheel, she jumped off, and pushing it beside her or carrying it, she ran as fast as she could. The day was fearfully warm. The sun poured with the fierce baking heat of August. The bushes claimed her hat, and she did not stop for it.

Where it was at all possible, the Angel mounted and pounded over the corduroy again. She was panting for breath and almost worn out when she reached the level pike. She had no idea how long she had been﻿—and only two miles covered. She leaned over the bars, almost standing on the pedals, racing with all the strength in her body. The blood surged in her ears while her head swam, but she kept a straight course, and rode and rode. It seemed to her that she was standing still, while the trees and houses were racing past her.

Once a farmer’s big dog rushed angrily into the road and she swerved until she almost fell, but she regained her balance, and setting her muscles, pedaled as fast as she could. At last she lifted her head. Surely it could not be over a mile more. She had covered two of corduroy and at least three of gravel, and it was only six in all.

She was reeling in the saddle, but she gripped the bars with new energy, and raced desperately. The sun beat on her bare head and hands. Just when she was choking with dust, and almost prostrate with heat and exhaustion﻿—crash, she ran into a broken bottle. Snap! went the tire; the wheel swerved and pitched over. The Angel rolled into the thick yellow dust of the road and lay quietly.

===Chinese===
圆木路上那可怕的几英里！就没有个尽头吗？她不敢把车子使唤得太狠，万一摔坏了，靠两条腿是决计不能按时赶到的。哪一段路车过不去，她就跳下来，或推着车、或扛起车，撒开腿能跑多快跑多快。这天热得吓人。太阳泼下八月里那种火辣辣的烘烤。灌木丛勾走了她的帽子，她也没有停下来捡。

只要有一线可能，天使就重新上车，在圆木路上一路猛颠疾驰。待赶到平坦的大路时，她已气喘吁吁，累得几乎垮了。她说不上自己骑了多久——才走完两英里。她俯身扑在车把上，几乎站着蹬踏板，拿出全身的力气拼命赶。血在耳中轰鸣奔涌，头一阵阵发晕，她却咬定直路不偏，骑呀，骑呀。她恍惚觉得自己是定住不动的，倒是树木和房屋在她身边飞驰而过。

有一回，一家农夫的大狗怒冲冲蹿上路面，她急闪车躲让，险些摔倒，终究稳住了车身，绷紧全身筋肉，蹬得飞快。终于她抬起头来。剩下的总不能再有一英里多了。圆木路她骑完了两英里，砾石路至少三英里，全程拢共不过六英里。

她在车座上摇摇欲坠，却重新攒起劲来攥紧车把，拼死赶路。太阳直打在她光着的头顶和双手上。正当她被尘土呛得窒息、又热又乏几乎瘫倒的时候——哗啦，车子撞上了一个碎瓶子。啪！车胎爆了；车身一歪，栽翻过去。天使滚进路上厚厚的黄土里，静静躺着一动不动。

===Original===
From afar, Duncan began to notice a strange, dust-covered object in the road, as he headed toward town with the first load of the day’s felling.

He chirruped to the bays and hurried them all he could. As he neared the Angel, he saw it was a woman and a broken wheel. He was beside her in an instant. He carried her to a shaded fence-corner, stretched her on the grass, and wiped the dust from the lovely face all dirt-streaked, crimson, and bearing a startling whiteness around the mouth and nose.

Wheels were common enough. Many of the farmers’ daughters owned and rode them, but he knew these same farmers’ daughters; this face was a stranger’s. He glanced at the Angel’s tumbled clothing, the silkiness of her hair, with its pale satin ribbon, and noticed that she had lost her hat. Her lips tightened in an ominous quiver. He left her and picked up the wheel: as he had surmised, he knew it. This, then, was Freckles’ Swamp Angel. There was trouble in the Limberlost, and she had broken down racing to McLean. Duncan turned the bays into a fence-corner, tied one of them, unharnessed the other, fastened up the trace chains, and hurried to the nearest farmhouse to send help to the Angel. He found a woman, who took a bottle of camphor, a jug of water, and some towels, and started on the run.

Then Duncan put the bay to speed and raced to camp.

===Chinese===
邓肯赶着当天伐下木料的第一车往镇上去，远远便注意到路上有个蒙满尘土的奇怪东西。

他朝那两匹栗色马啧啧打了个呼哨，把它们催到了脚力的极限。离得近了，他看清那是一位姑娘和一辆摔坏的车。转眼间他已到她身旁。他把她抱到栅栏旁有荫凉的角落，平放在草地上，拭去那张可爱脸庞上的尘土——满脸泥痕，一片绯红，口鼻四周却白得吓人。

自行车如今不算稀罕物。许多农家的女儿都有车、也骑车，可这些农家姑娘他全都认得；这张脸却是生的。他看了一眼天使那凌乱的衣衫、那丝缎般的头发和发上的淡色缎带，注意到她的帽子丢了。她的嘴唇抽紧，颤得叫人心惊。他放下她，拾起那辆车：不出所料，他认得它。这么说，这位就是弗雷克尔斯的沼泽天使了。林伯洛斯特出了乱子，她是赶去搬麦克莱恩的路上摔坏的。邓肯把两匹栗色马赶进栅栏一角，拴住一匹，给另一匹卸下挽具，把挽链收拾妥当，便急匆匆赶往最近的一家农舍，替天使搬救兵。他寻到一个女人，那女人拿上一瓶樟脑、一罐水和几条毛巾，拔腿便奔了来。

随后邓肯催动那匹栗色马，朝营地飞奔而去。

===Original===
The Angel, left alone, lay still for a second, then she shivered and opened her eyes. She saw that she was on the grass and the broken wheel beside her. Instantly she realized that someone had carried her there and gone after help. She sat up and looked around. She noticed the load of logs and the one horse. Someone was riding after help for her!

“Oh, poor Freckles!” she wailed. “They may be killing him by now. Oh, how much time have I wasted?”

She hurried to the other bay, her fingers flying as she set him free. Snatching up a big blacksnake whip that lay on the ground, she caught the hames, stretched along the horse’s neck, and, for the first time, the fine, big fellow felt on his back the quality of the lash that Duncan was accustomed to crack over him. He was frightened, and ran at top speed.

The Angel passed a wildly waving, screaming woman on the road, and a little later a man riding as if he, too, were in great haste. The man called to her, but she only lay lower and used the whip. Soon the feet of the man’s horse sounded farther and farther away.

===Chinese===
天使独自被留下，静静躺了一小会儿，接着打了个哆嗦，睁开眼睛。她见自己躺在草地上，摔坏的车就在身旁。她立刻明白，是有人把她抱到了这里，又搬救兵去了。她坐起来四下张望，看见了那车原木和那匹独马。有人正骑马去为她搬救兵呢！

「噢，可怜的弗雷克尔斯！」她恸声喊道。「这会儿他们说不定正在害他。噢，我到底耽搁了多少工夫啊？」

她奔向另一匹栗色马，十指翻飞，麻利地把它解开。她抓起躺在地上的那根黑蛇长鞭，攀住颈轭，整个人伏上马颈——这匹骏美的高头大马平生头一回，领教了邓肯惯常在它头顶炸响的那条鞭子的真正分量。它吓得魂飞魄散，撒开四蹄狂奔起来。

天使在路上超过了一个狂挥手臂、尖声喊叫的女人，隔不多时又超过一个骑马的男人，那人也像是十万火急。男人朝她呼喊，她只是把身子伏得更低，鞭子抽得更急。不多时，那男人坐骑的马蹄声便落得越来越远了。

===Original===
At the South camp they were loading a second wagon, when the Angel appeared riding one of Duncan’s bays, lathered and dripping, and cried: “Everybody go to Freckles! There are thieves stealing trees, and they had him bound. They’re going to kill him!”

She wheeled the horse toward the Limberlost. The alarm sounded through camp. The gang were not unprepared. McLean sprang to Nellie’s back and raced after the Angel. As they passed Duncan, he wheeled and followed. Soon the pike was an irregular procession of barebacked riders, wildly driving flying horses toward the swamp.

The Boss rode neck-and-neck with the Angel. He repeatedly commanded her to stop and fall out of line, until he remembered that he would need her to lead him to Freckles. Then he gave up and rode beside her, for she was sending the bay at as sharp a pace as the other horses could keep and hold out. He could see that she was not hearing him. He glanced back and saw that Duncan was close. There was something terrifying in the appearance of the big man, and the manner in which he sat his beast and rode. It would be a sad day for the man on whom Duncan’s wrath broke. There were four others close behind him, and the pike filling with the remainder of the gang; so McLean took heart and raced beside the Angel. Over and over he asked her where the trouble was, but she only gripped the hames, leaned along the bay’s neck, and slashed away with the blacksnake. The steaming horse, with crimson nostrils and heaving sides, stretched out and ran for home with all the speed there was in him.

When they passed the cabin, the Bird Woman’s carriage was there and Mrs. Duncan in the door wringing her hands, but the Bird Woman was nowhere to be seen. The Angel sent the bay along the path and turned into the west trail, while the men bunched and followed her. When she reached the entrance to Freckles’ room, there were four men with her, and two more very close behind. She slid from the horse, and snatching the little revolver from her pocket, darted toward the bushes. McLean caught them back, and with drawn weapon, pressed beside her. There they stopped in astonishment.

===Chinese===
南营里，人们正往第二辆大车上装货，忽见天使骑着邓肯的一匹栗色马闯到——那马浑身汗沫，汗珠直滴。她大声喊道：「大家都去救弗雷克尔斯！有贼在偷树，他们把他捆起来了。他们要杀他！」

她拨转马头直奔林伯洛斯特。警报响遍营地。这班人本不是毫无防备的。麦克莱恩一跃跨上内莉，紧随着天使追去。他们经过邓肯身边时，邓肯也拨转马头跟了上来。不多时，大路上便成了一支不成行列的骑队，人人骣背疾驰，发疯般赶着飞奔的坐骑直扑沼泽。

老板与天使并辔齐驱。他再三喝令她停下、退出队伍，直到想起还须由她引路去找弗雷克尔斯，这才作罢，改在她身旁骑行——她把那匹栗色马催得如此火急，别的马能跟得上、撑得住已属勉强。他看得出她压根儿听不见他说话。他回头一瞥，见邓肯紧随在后。这大个子的模样，他骑在牲口上的那副身架和派头，自有一股叫人胆寒的东西。邓肯的雷霆之怒落到谁头上，谁那一天就惨了。他身后还紧跟着四个人，大路上又涌满工队的其余人马；麦克莱恩于是定了心，伴着天使一路疾驰。他一遍又一遍地问出事地点在哪儿，她却只是攥紧颈轭，伏在栗色马的颈子上，黑蛇鞭一个劲儿地抽。那匹热气蒸腾的马，鼻孔赤红，两肋起伏，撒开身子，拿出浑身的脚力朝家奔去。

经过那座小屋时，鸟太太的马车正停在门前，邓肯太太立在门内绞着双手，鸟太太却不见踪影。天使策马顺小径前行，拐入西边小道，男人们挤作一团跟了上来。到弗雷克尔斯那间屋子的入口时，她身边有四个人，另有两个紧随其后。她翻身滑下马背，从衣袋里掏出那把小左轮，直朝灌木丛冲去。麦克莱恩把众人拦在身后，拔出枪来，紧贴着她跟上。到了那里，众人都惊得收住了脚。

===Original===
The Bird Woman blocked the entrance. Over a small limb lay her revolver. It was trained at short range on Black Jack and Wessner, who stood with their hands above their heads.

Freckles, with the blood trickling down his face, from an ugly cut in his temple, was gagged and bound to the tree again; the remainder of the men were gone. Black Jack was raving as a maniac, and when they looked closer it was only the left arm that he raised. His right, with the hand shattered, hung helpless at his side, while his revolver lay at Freckles’ feet. Wessner’s weapon was in his belt, and beside him Freckles’ club.

Freckles’ face was white, with colorless lips, but in his eyes was the strength of undying courage. McLean pushed past the Bird Woman crying. “Hold steady on them only one minute more!”

He snatched the revolver from Wessner’s belt, and stooped for Jack’s.

===Chinese===
鸟太太堵在入口处。她那把左轮搁在一根小树枝上，近距离对准了黑杰克和韦斯纳——两人双手高举过头，直挺挺站着。

弗雷克尔斯太阳穴上挨了凶狠一刀，鲜血顺着脸颊往下淌；他又被塞住嘴，捆在树上；其余的人已经跑光。黑杰克疯子似的狂吼乱骂，众人细看时，才发现他高举的只是左臂。那条右臂瘫垂在身侧动弹不得，手已打得稀烂，他的左轮则躺在弗雷克尔斯脚边。韦斯纳的枪插在腰带上，他身旁扔着弗雷克尔斯的木棍。

弗雷克尔斯脸色煞白，嘴唇毫无血色，眼里却盛着一种打不垮的无畏气概。麦克莱恩从鸟太太身边挤过去，喊道：「再稳稳盯住他们一分钟！」

他从韦斯纳腰带上夺下左轮，又俯身去捡杰克那把。

===Original===
At that instant the Angel rushed past. She tore the gag from Freckles, and seizing the rope knotted on his chest, she tugged at it desperately. Under her fingers it gave way, and she hurled it to McLean. The men were crowding in, and Duncan seized Wessner. As the Angel saw Freckles stand out, free, she reached her arms to him and pitched forward. A fearful oath burst from the lips of Black Jack. To have saved his life, Freckles could not have avoided the glance of triumph he gave Jack, when folding the Angel in his arms and stretching her on the mosses.

The Bird Woman cried out sharply for water as she ran to them. Someone sprang to bring that, and another to break open the case for brandy. As McLean arose from binding Wessner, there was a cry that Jack was escaping.

He was already far in the swamp, running for its densest part in leaping bounds. Every man who could be spared plunged after him.

Other members of the gang arriving, were sent to follow the tracks of the wagons. The teamsters had driven from the west entrance, and crossing the swale, had taken the same route the Bird Woman and the Angel had before them. There had been ample time for the drivers to reach the road; after that they could take any one of four directions. Traffic was heavy, and lumber wagons were passing almost constantly, so the men turned back and joined the more exciting hunt for a man. The remainder of the gang joined them, also farmers of the region and travelers attracted by the disturbance.

===Chinese===
就在这一瞬间，天使冲了过去。她扯掉弗雷克尔斯嘴里的堵物，一把抓住捆在他胸前的绳结，拼命地拽。绳子在她指下松脱了，她把它甩给麦克莱恩。男人们蜂拥而上，邓肯一把擒住韦斯纳。天使眼见弗雷克尔斯脱身站起、自由了，便朝他张开双臂，一头栽倒过去。黑杰克嘴里迸出一句狠毒的咒骂。弗雷克尔斯把天使搂进怀里，放平在青苔上；这时他朝杰克投去的那一眼得意的目光，便是拿性命来换，他也躲不开。

鸟太太一边朝他们奔去，一边尖声喊着要水。有人一跃而起去取水，另一个去撬开箱子拿白兰地。麦克莱恩捆好韦斯纳刚站起身，就有人喊杰克逃了。

他已经远远窜进沼泽，连蹦带跳朝最密的地方奔逃。凡能抽出身来的人都一头扎进去追他。

工队的其他人陆续赶到，被打发去追大车的辙迹。车夫们是打西口出去的，穿过沼泽草甸，走的正是鸟太太和天使先前走的那条道。他们有的是工夫赶到大路；此后东西南北，任凭往哪个方向去。路上车马稠密，运木料的大车几乎络绎不绝，众人只得折回来，加入那场更带劲的追捕。工队其余的人也来会合，还有本地的农夫和被这场骚动引来的过路人。

===Original===
Watchers were set along the trail at short intervals. They patrolled the line and roads through the swamp that night, with lighted torches, and the next day McLean headed as thorough a search as he felt could be made of one side, while Duncan covered the other; but Black Jack could not be found. Spies were set around his home, in Wildcat Hollow, to ascertain if he reached there or aid was being sent in any direction to him; but it was soon clear that his relatives were ignorant of his hiding-place, and were searching for him.

Great is the elasticity of youth. A hot bath and a sound night’s sleep renewed Freckles’ strength, and it needed but little more to work the same result with the Angel. Freckles was on the trail early the next morning. Besides a crowd of people anxious to witness Jack’s capture, he found four stalwart guards, one at each turn. In his heart he was compelled to admit that he was glad to have them there. Close noon, McLean placed his men in charge of Duncan, and taking Freckles, drove to town to see how the Angel fared. McLean visited a greenhouse and bought an armload of its finest products; but Freckles would have none of them. He would carry his message in a glowing mass of the Limberlost’s first goldenrod.

The Bird Woman received them, and in answer to their eager inquiries, said that the Angel was in no way seriously injured, only so bruised and shaken that their doctor had ordered her to lie quietly for the day. Though she was sore and stiff, they were having work to keep her in bed. Her callers sent up their flowers with their grateful regards, and the Angel promptly returned word that she wanted to see them.

===Chinese===
小道沿线隔不多远便布下一个个守望的人。当夜他们举着火把，沿铁丝线和穿过沼泽的各条道路巡逻；第二天，麦克莱恩领着人，在一边进行了他认为人力所能做到的最严密的搜查，邓肯则兜住另一边；可黑杰克还是踪影全无。又在他野猫谷的住处四周布了眼线，看他是否逃到了那里，有没有人朝任何方向接济他；但很快就弄清了：他的亲属并不知道他的藏身之处，也在四处寻他。

青春的复元力真了不起。一个热水澡、一夜酣睡，弗雷克尔斯的气力便养了回来；天使呢，也不过多将息了片刻，便有了同样的效果。第二天一早，弗雷克尔斯就上了小道。除了赶来等着看杰克落网的一大群人，他还见到四名剽悍的守卫，每个拐角站着一个。他心里不得不承认，有他们在，他是高兴的。将近正午，麦克莱恩把手下人交由邓肯统领，带上弗雷克尔斯驱车进城，去看天使的情形。麦克莱恩光顾了一家花房，买下一抱其中最上品的花；弗雷克尔斯却一概不要。他要用自己的信物传话——一大捧林伯洛斯特初绽的、灿灿放光的一枝黄花（goldenrod）。

鸟太太接待了他们，答复他们急切的询问：天使绝非伤得要紧，只是跌得青一块紫一块、震得不轻，家里的医生吩咐她静卧一日。她虽浑身酸痛僵硬，家里人还得费些气力才留得住她在床上。来访者托人把花连同感激的心意一并送上去，天使即刻传下话来，说她要见他们。

===Original===
She reached both hands to McLean. “What if one old tree is gone? You don’t care, sir? You feel that Freckles has kept his trust as nobody ever did before, don’t you? You won’t forget all those long first days of fright that you told us of, the fearful cold of winter, the rain, heat, and lonesomeness, and the brave days, and lately, nights, too, and let him feel that his trust is broken? Oh, Mr. McLean,” she begged, “say something to him! Do something to make him feel that it isn’t for nothing he has watched and suffered it out with that old Limberlost. Make him see how great and fine it is, and how far, far better he has done than you or any of us expected! What’s one old tree, anyway?” she cried passionately.

“I was thinking before you came. Those other men were rank big cowards. They were scared for their lives. If they were the drivers, I wager you gloves against gloves they never took those logs out to the pike. My coming upset them. Before you feel bad any more, you go look and see if they didn’t lose courage the minute they left Wessner and Black Jack, dump that timber and run. I don’t believe they ever had the grit to drive out with it in daylight. Go see if they didn’t figure on leaving the way we did the other morning, and you’ll find the logs before you reach the road. They never risked taking them into the open, when they got away and had time to think. Of course they didn’t!

“And, then, another thing. You haven’t lost your wager! It never will be claimed, because you made it with a stout, dark, red-faced man who drives a bay and a gray. He was right back of you, Mr. McLean, when I came yesterday. He went deathly white and shook on his feet when he saw those men probably would be caught. Some one of them was something to him, and you can just spot him for one of the men at the bottom of your troubles, and urging those younger fellows to steal from you. I suppose he’d promised to divide. You settle with him, and that business will stop.”

She turned to Freckles. “And you be the happiest man alive, because you have kept your trust. Go look where I tell you and you’ll find the logs. I can see just about where they are. When they go up that steep little hill, into the next woods after the cornfield, why, they could unloose the chains and the logs would roll from the wagons themselves. Now, you go look; and Mr. McLean, you do feel that Freckles has been brave and faithful? You won’t love him any the less even if you don’t find the logs.”

===Chinese===
她把双手都伸向麦克莱恩。「丢了一棵老树，又算得了什么？您不心疼的吧，先生？您是觉得弗雷克尔斯守住了他的托付，从没有人守得这样好，是不是？您给我们讲过的那些事，您总不会都忘了吧——起初那些担惊受怕的漫长日子，冬天冷得可怕，雨水、暑热和孤单，还有那些勇敢的白昼，近来连夜晚也在内——您总不会丢开这些，反倒让他觉得他的托付毁在了自己手里吧？噢，麦克莱恩先生，」她恳求道，「对他说句话吧！做点什么，让他明白：他守着那片老林伯洛斯特熬出来、挺过来，不是白熬的。让他看看这件事多么重大、多么出色，他干得比您、比我们任何人所指望的，好出千倍万倍！一棵老树，到底又算得了什么呢？」她激愤地喊道。

「你们来之前我就想过了。另外那几个人是十足的大胆小鬼。他们怕的是自己的性命。车若真是他们赶的，我拿一对手套跟您赌一对手套，他们压根儿没把那些原木拉到大路上去。我一去，就把他们吓破了胆。您先别再难过，去看看他们是不是一离开韦斯纳和黑杰克就泄了气，把木料一倒、撒腿就跑了。我不信他们有那个胆量，敢在大白天赶着木料出来。去看看他们是不是盘算着照我们那天早晨的法子开溜，您还没到大路，就会找到那些原木。他们脱了身、定下神来一想，是决计不肯冒险把木料拉到明处去的。当然不肯！」

「还有一件事。您那场赌并没有输！它永远兑现不了，因为跟您打赌的是个矮壮黢黑、红脸膛的家伙，赶着一匹栗色马、一匹灰马。昨天我来的时候，他就在您正后方，麦克莱恩先生。他眼见那几个人多半要被抓，霎时白得死人一般，站在那里直打晃。那几个人里有一个跟他有牵连，您只管断定，他就是给您惹下这场祸的根子之一，就是他在撺掇那些年轻人偷您的树。我料想他是许了分赃的。您跟他清算清算，这桩买卖自然就断了。」

她转向弗雷克尔斯。「你要做天底下最快活的人才好，因为你守住了你的托付。到我说的地方去看看，保管找得到那些原木。我差不多看得见它们在哪儿。上那道陡陡的小坡、过了玉米地进第二片林子的地方，喏，只要把链条一松，原木自己就会从车上滚下来。好了，快去看吧；还有，麦克莱恩先生，您当真觉得弗雷克尔斯又勇敢又忠心，是不是？就算找不到那些原木，您对他的疼爱也一分不会减吧。」

===Original===
The Angel’s nerve gave way and she began to cry. Freckles could not endure it. He almost ran from the room, with the tears in his eyes; but McLean took the Angel from the Bird Woman’s arms, and kissed her brave little face, stroked her hair, and petted her into quietness before he left.

As they drove to the swamp, McLean so earnestly seconded all that the Angel had said that he soon had the boy feeling much better.

“Freckles, your Angel has a spice of the devil in her, but she’s superb! You needn’t spend any time questioning or bewailing anything she does. Just worship blindly, my boy. By heaven! she’s sense, courage, and beauty for half a dozen girls,” said McLean.

“It’s altogether right you are, sir,” affirmed Freckles heartily. Presently he added, “There’s no question but the series is over now.”

“Don’t think it!” answered McLean. “The Bird Woman is working for success, and success along any line is not won by being scared out. She will be back on the usual day, and ten to one, the Angel will be with her. They are made of pretty stern stuff, and they don’t scare worth a cent. Before I left, I told the Bird Woman it would be safe; and it will. You may do your usual walking, but those four guards are there to remain. They are under your orders absolutely. They are prohibited from firing on any bird or molesting anything that you want to protect, but there they remain, and this time it is useless for you to say one word. I have listened to your pride too long. You are too precious to me, and that voice of yours is too precious to the world to run any more risks.”

===Chinese===
天使的强撑终于垮了，哭了起来。弗雷克尔斯受不住这个。他眼含热泪，几乎是从屋里逃出去的；麦克莱恩却把天使从鸟太太怀里接过来，亲吻她那张勇敢的小脸，抚着她的头发，把她疼惜得安静下来，方才离开。

回沼泽的路上，麦克莱恩把天使的话那样恳切地一一附和，不多时便让这少年的心里好受多了。

「弗雷克尔斯，你这位天使身上带着几分小魔鬼的辣味，可她真了不起！她做的事，你不必费工夫去掂量、去哀叹。只管闭着眼睛崇拜就是了，我的孩子。老天在上！她的见识、勇气和美貌，抵得上六个姑娘。」麦克莱恩说。

「您说得一点儿也不错，先生。」弗雷克尔斯由衷地应道。过了一会儿他又说：「这小鸡系列毫无疑问，是到此为止了。」

「可别这么想！」麦克莱恩答道。「鸟太太做事求的是成功，而不论做哪一行，成功都不是靠吓退缩赢来的。她照旧会按平常的日子来，十有八九，天使也会同来。她们是顶硬扎的材料做的，想吓住她们，一毛钱都不值。临走前我对鸟太太说过，包管安全无事；就会安全无事。你照常巡你的林子，可那四名守卫要留在那里。他们一概听你的号令。他们被禁止向任何鸟儿开枪，也禁止惊扰任何你要保护的东西，但他们就守在那里，这一回你说什么都白搭。你的自尊心，我听得够久了。你对我太珍贵了，而你那条嗓子，对这个世界也太珍贵了，不能再冒任何风险。」

===Original===
“I am sorry to have anything spoil the series,” said Freckles, “and I’d love them to be coming, the Angel especial, but it can’t be. You’ll have to tell them so. You see, Jack would have been ready to stake his life she meant what she said and did to him. When the teams pulled out, Wessner seized me; then he and Jack went to quarreling over whether they should finish me then or take me to the next tree they were for felling. Between them they were pulling me around and hurting me bad. Wessner wanted to get at me right then, and Jack said he shouldn’t be touching me till the last tree was out and all the rest of them gone. I’m belaying Jack really hated to see me done for in the beginning; and I think, too, he was afraid if Wessner finished me then he’d lose his nerve and cut, and they couldn’t be managing the felling without him; anyway, they were hauling me round like I was already past all feeling, and they tied me up again. To keep me courage up, I twits Wessner about having to tie me and needing another man to help handle me. I told him what I’d do to him if I was free, and he grabs up me own club and lays open me head with it. When the blood came streaming, it set Jack raving, and he cursed and damned Wessner for a coward and a softy. Then Wessner turned on Jack and gives it to him for letting the Angel make a fool of him. Tells him she was just playing with him, and beyond all manner of doubt she’d gone after you, and there was nothing to do on account of his foolishness but finish me, get out, and let the rest of the timber go, for likely you was on the way right then. That drove Jack plum crazy.

“I don’t think he was for having a doubt of the Angel before, but then he just raved. He grabbed out his gun and turned on Wessner. Spang! It went out of his fist, and the order comes: ‘Hands up!’ Wessner reached for kingdom come like he was expecting to grab hold and pull himself up. Jack puts up what he has left. Then he leans over to me and tells me what he’ll do to me if he ever gets out of there alive. Then, just like a snake hissing, he spits out what he’ll do to her for playing him. He did get away, and with his strength, that wound in his hand won’t be bothering him long. He’ll do to me just what he said, and when he hears it really was she that went after you, why, he’ll keep his oath about her.

“He’s lived in the swamp all his life, sir, and everybody says it’s always been the home of cutthroats, outlaws, and runaways. He knows its most secret places as none of the others. He’s alive. He’s in there now, sir. Some way he’ll keep alive. If you’d seen his face, all scarlet with passion, twisted with pain, and black with hate, and heard him swearing that oath, you’d know it was a sure thing. I ain’t done with him yet, and I’ve brought this awful thing on her.”

“And I haven’t begun with him yet,” said McLean, setting his teeth. “I’ve been away too slow and too easy, believing there’d be no greater harm than the loss of a tree. I’ve sent for a couple of first-class detectives. We will put them on his track, and rout him out and rid the country of him. I don’t propose for him to stop either our work or our pleasure. As for his being in the swamp now, I don’t believe it. He’d find a way out last night, in spite of us. Don’t you worry! I am at the helm now, and I’ll see to that gentleman in my own way.”

“I wish to my soul you had seen and heard him!” said Freckles, unconvinced.

===Chinese===
「俺很遗憾让事情搅了这套小鸡系列，」弗雷克尔斯说，「俺也巴不得她们来，尤其是天使，可来不成了。您只好这样告诉她们。您想啊，天使跟杰克说的、做的那一套，杰克是肯拿性命打赌她句句当真的。大车一走，韦斯纳就揪住了俺；跟着他和杰克吵了起来，吵的是当场结果了俺呢，还是把俺拖到他们要伐的下一棵树那儿再说。他们俩把俺拖来拖去，把俺伤得不轻。韦斯纳想当场就对俺下手，杰克说，最后一棵树没运出去、其余人没散伙之前，不许碰俺。俺估摸着，杰克起初实在是不忍看俺完蛋；俺还想着，他也怕万一韦斯纳当时结果了俺，他会吓破了胆撒腿开溜，而没有韦斯纳，他们调度不了伐树的事；反正他们把俺拖来拖去，好像俺已经全无知觉，随后又把俺捆了回去。为了给俺自己壮胆，俺笑话韦斯纳，说他捆俺还得另请帮手。俺对他说，俺要是脱了身，要怎么收拾他；他就抄起俺自己的木棍，把俺的脑袋开了瓢。血一涌出来，杰克就狂了，把韦斯纳骂了个狗血淋头，骂他是胆小鬼、软蛋。于是韦斯纳反过来冲杰克发作，怨他让天使把他耍了个透。说天使不过是耍着他玩，说她十拿九稳是搬您去了，都怪他蠢，如今除了结果了俺、赶紧脱身、舍下其余的木料，再没有第二条路，因为那时您多半已经在路上了。这话把杰克气得整个儿疯了。

「俺看，在这之前他对天使本没什么疑心，可那时他就是满口狂言。他拔出枪来转向韦斯纳。砰！枪飞出了他的拳头，跟着一声断喝：『举起手来！』韦斯纳那双手直往天上够，像是打算攀住天国、把自己拽上去似的。杰克把剩下那只手举了起来。随后他俯下身对俺说，他要是活着出得去，要怎么怎么收拾俺。接着又像毒蛇吐信一般，嘶嘶地把耍了他的她要受什么，一字一字啐出来。他真的逃了，凭他那身力气，手上那点伤烦不了他多久。他对俺说过的话，他会一字不改地照办；等他听说去搬您的当真是她，哼，他对她赌的那个咒，他是一定要兑现的。

「他生来就住在这片沼泽里，先生，人人都说，这里从来就是刺客、亡命徒和逃犯的老窝。沼泽里最隐秘的所在，他比谁都要清楚。他还活着。他这会儿就在里头，先生。他总有法子活下来。您要是见过他那张脸——盛怒烧得通红，疼痛扭得歪斜，仇恨浸得乌黑——听过他赌的那个咒，您就知道这是板上钉钉的事。俺跟他的账还没了呢，俺还把这桩大祸引到了她身上。」

「我也还没跟他算账呢。」麦克莱恩咬紧牙关说。「我一直太迟、太宽了，总以为大不了丢一棵树。我已经派人去请两位一流的侦探。我们要让他们盯住他的踪迹，把他搜出来，把这地方的他除掉。我决不容他搅了我们的正事，也不容他搅了我们的乐趣。至于说他此刻还在沼泽里，我不信。昨天夜里，任凭我们怎么围堵，他也会摸出一条路来。你别担心！如今舵在我手里，那位绅士，我自有法子料理。」

「俺打心窝里盼着，您当时见了他、听了他才好！」弗雷克尔斯说，全然不信。

===Original===
They entered the swamp, taking the route followed by the Bird Woman and the Angel. They really did find the logs, almost where the Angel had predicted they would be. McLean went to the South camp and had an interview with Crowen that completely convinced him that the Angel was correct there also. But he had no proof, so all he could do was to discharge the man, although his guilt was so apparent that he offered to withdraw the wager.

Then McLean sent for a pack of bloodhounds and put them on the trail of Black Jack. They clung to it, on and on, into the depths of the swamp, leading their followers through what had been considered impassable and impenetrable ways, and finally, around near the west entrance and into the swale. Here the dogs bellowed, raved, and fell over each other in their excitement. They raced back and forth from swamp to swale, but follow the scent farther they would not, even though cruelly driven. At last their owner attributed their actions to snakes, and as they were very valuable dogs, abandoned the effort to urge them on. So that all they really established was the fact that Black Jack had eluded their vigilance and crossed the trail some time in the night. He had escaped to the swale; from there he probably crossed the corduroy, and reaching the lower end of the swamp, had found friends. It was a great relief to feel that he was not in the swamp, and it raised the spirits of every man on the line, though many of them expressed regrets that he who was undoubtedly most to blame should escape, while Wessner, who in the beginning was only his tool, should be left to punishment.

But for Freckles, with Jack’s fearful oath ringing in his ears, there was neither rest nor peace. He was almost ill when the day for the next study of the series arrived and he saw the Bird Woman and the Angel coming down the corduroy. The guards of the east line he left at their customary places, but those of the west he brought over and placed, one near Little Chicken’s tree, and the other at the carriage. He was firm about the Angel’s remaining in the carriage, that he did not offer to have unhitched. He went with the Bird Woman to secure the picture, which was the easiest matter it had been at any time yet, for the simple reason that the placing of the guards and the unusual movement around the swamp had made Mr. and Mrs. Chicken timid, and they had not carried Little Chicken the customary amount of food. Freckles, in the anxiety of the past few days, had neglected him, and he had been so hungry, much of the time, that when the Bird Woman held up a sweetbread, although he had started toward the recesses of the log at her coming, he stopped; with slightly opened beak, he waited anxiously for the treat, and gave a study of great value, showing every point of his head, also his wing and tail development.

When the Bird Woman proposed to look for other subjects close about the line, Freckles went so far as to tell her that Jack had made fearful threats against the Angel. He implored her to take the Angel home and keep her under unceasing guard until Jack was located. He wanted to tell her all about it, but he knew how dear the Angel was to her, and he dreaded to burden her with his fears when they might prove groundless. He allowed her to go, but afterward blamed himself severely for having done so.

===Chinese===
他们取鸟太太和天使先前走的那条道进了沼泽。那些原木果然找到了，几乎就在天使料定的地点。麦克莱恩到南营去同克罗恩（Crowen）谈了一回，这一谈叫他完全信服：天使在这件事上也没说错。只是他拿不出证据，充其量只能把那人开除，尽管那人罪责彰明较著，连那场赌注都自己提出撤销了。
